# Backend Interfaces

Canonical `SessionToolExecutor` is a tenant dispatch facade. It requires actual store-resolved tenant Run authority for every provider effect; configured providers, an allowlisted Definition and caller-built IDs/delegation are insufficient. There is no generic provider execution fallback. Browser metadata projection remains separate from effect execution, and server-owned working-directory cleanup/sandbox finalization retain generation fencing. Unowned fixture configuration may use its already-supplied immutable Definition but reads no private configuration, bindings, credentials or Workspace stores and receives no executable tool offers. Legacy capture rehydration, occurrence-browser opening, email-selection/automatic configuration continuation and completion-report admission callbacks are retired. Canonical services retain their own guarded image recovery, current configuration continuation and completion accounting. The global `IStructuredMemoryService` port, its service/admission implementation and DI registration are retired; private recall uses only the actual tenant Run's owning Memory/Experience/continuity sources. Global prompt reader methods are removed. `SessionMemoryPrompt` retains pure formatting and admission helpers, not store access. Argument/profile identifiers cannot select a global recall source.

## Owner credential ports retired

`IOwnerCapabilityService`, `IOwnerCapabilityStore`, `IssuedOwnerCapability` and their implementations are removed. No issuer, validator or store is registered. Public owner header/response DTOs and the old `AttachPayload` are removed from Contracts. Account cookie/server-session authentication and exact tenant admission remain the supported boundary. The only old-header test fixture is a fixed adversarial value; it has no authority or issuance path. Earlier owner-capability descriptions in this document are historical implementation records, not current interfaces. [Measured retirement](reports/owner-credential-retirement-verification-2026-10-21.md).


## Tenant application progress messages

`app.message.send` retains the existing Session Runtime mailbox owner. Offering and execution require an actual tenant human conversation (UserMessage or its human ApprovalContinuation, ConversationResponse output), current live claim, substantive work in an earlier model generation and no configuration-continuation target. Detached work, Automation tasks and completion reports use their existing output contracts. A caller-supplied intermediate-work flag cannot make the generic executor append a notice.

Before deduplication or admission, the mailbox repeats current Run/executor/requester and retained-source authorization through the existing Run authority owner. Notice text can quote private evidence just like a final reply. Ordinary mutable Workspace reads retain their existing observation semantics and current read checks; exact Composer/Resource sources retain their existing source-continuity rules. No new file-version authority scheme is introduced.

Core chooses the current Session and response. Existing text-only validation, 2000-character message limit, twelve-message/8000-character execution budget, deduplication, no speech/heard text and omission from future prompt/compaction/completion context remain unchanged. Cancellation/steering cannot append a late notice or erase already admitted ones. These are conversation outputs with no new permission, approval kind, destination selector or wire record. The [scoped verification](reports/tenant-application-messages-verification-2026-10-24.md) owns actual evidence and limits.


## Model-tool live claim boundary

`TenantToolAuthorization` uses the injected `TimeProvider` and current persisted Run claim for every tool family. Actual tenant Session ownership, a Running uncancelled Run, exact nonempty generation and an unexpired current deadline precede family eligibility, argument parsing and dispatch inside that adapter. IAM inspection repeats this check before returning its result. Runtime mailbox commands cannot borrow the generic IAM parser. Existing family capability, current IAM, policy, source/consent and effect-transaction checks remain independent. The [complete registered tool checkpoint](reports/tenant-model-tool-authority-verification-2026-10-24.md) records scope and outstanding parity work.


## Runtime registry cutover checkpoint

The registered dispatcher is `ITenantRunDispatcher` backed by the singleton `TenantSessionRuntimeHost`; input, foreground, cancellation and receipt controls resolve to that same object. The old unowned `IAgentRunDispatcher` registration, `IEnvironmentEventIngress`, live occurrence directory/mailbox, hosted state reader and profile live-update adapter are removed. Shared claimed-Run coordination still receives explicit tenant persistence and dispatcher bindings. No replacement empty authority adapter is registered.

`TenantRuntimeCapacity` owns bounded server lifetime reservations only. `SessionShutdownHostedService` drains the canonical host through its idempotent `StopAsync`. These are lifecycle services, not another authorization engine. Earlier owner-host interface descriptions below are historical implemented behavior and do not describe the current host registration. Canonical Runtime-state and Experience owners are documented below. This registry checkpoint does not by itself qualify the remaining store/schema inventory or proposal-wide acceptance. [Measured scope](reports/runtime-registry-cutover-verification-2026-10-21.md).


## Independent Automation protected-task execution

An admitted tenant Schedule, Core/Webhook event or explicit human Manual occurrence may use the configured protected file/native/account operations under its actual executor and existing exact consent/receipt owners. Its approval successor preserves task, root, source and original initiator, including a genuinely absent scheduled requester. Current source-kind capability replaces the human-chat `background.start` prerequisite for these independent roots; it never replaces IAM. Manual invocations retain current actual-invoker authority. Scheduled `WhenRequesterLacksPermission` does not invent a human; `Always` and independent reviewer requirements remain mandatory. Raw source reads, secondary permissions and recipient reads never come from a primary-only grant. The owning transactions recheck current authority and source witnesses, and unknown native effects never replay.

Discovery uses the trusted admitted Automation task source and supported origin, with stored lineage validated by the incumbent operation authorizer. Core/webhook event roots additionally use the tenant receipt and source-read owners described below; email-watch roots remain fenced pending conversion. An absent requester is not a shared human rate-limit identity; tenant/executor/operation/root quotas remain active. Independent task completion stays in its own Background Session; destination/report admission, waits/child creation and Automation admission Review remain separate work.

## Tenant model management observations

`ITenantManagementStore` observes an exact bounded configuration snapshot through the incumbent owned Instance/Run/security aggregate. `management.inspect`, `settings.list`, `settings.inspect` and `automation.preferences.inspect` require a real conversational human request, current raw `agents.read`, an owned live claim and both admitted/current capability and Self-Management scope. Actual trusted identity supplies Tenant, executor and Instance; selectors cannot name another owner. Contextual `capabilities.load` uses the existing Runtime mailbox and saved load budget, while newly granted capabilities cannot widen an admitted Run.

`TenantManagementReadService` returns explicit snapshot revisions, sources, defaults/constraints and excerpt markers. Sandbox configuration exposes only its selected profile reference. Required read Review withholds values and records `PreparedManagementRead` through the existing exact approval owner; it grants no raw permission, write or future read. Current independent human read and reviewer qualification govern inspection/decision. Inbox and conversation source visibility filter before counts/paging. Changed configuration or revoked management withholds substituted values and leaves authorized cancellation available.

`TenantManagementReadReference` survives checkpoint compaction and is revalidated before model reentry, protected output and outcome commit. A contiguous exact same-task receipt chain may explain the three incumbent conversation preferences or an approved management mutation, checking every before/after stable configuration hash. Unexplained human or external changes fail closed. Completed self-removal/freeze does not erase the task’s historical observation; fresh operations still require current authority. This is observed historical data, never a refreshed model/configuration pin. The canonical Skill/Resource, contextual Harness and inspected remedy owners below qualify those paths. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-management-read-verification-2026-10-22.md).

## Compound Harness and configuration observations

Canonical `harness.inspect({})` and `configuration.inspect({goal?,target?})` reuse `ITenantManagementStore`, exact `inspect_management` consent and the incumbent source/checkpoint/receipt owners. Harness inspection retains its specified trusted delegation authority separately from Definition tool selection; configuration inspection requires its Definition capability. Both require a real conversational human root, admitted/current management, current raw `agents.read` and independently readable child sources. Instructions, knowledge, tool selection, Settings, Skills, Resources and Connections are disclosed only in their intersected delegated areas. Harness knowledge includes Knowledge resources only. The Definition capability ceiling, current Agent selection and admitted Run version are separate facts; none grant execution authority.

The owned aggregate projects bounded Skill/resource and connection metadata through existing semantic views. No Skill procedure, resource bytes, credential metadata body or protected secret is returned. Credential name/kind/purpose metadata requires current `credentials.read`; it grants no binding, use or account-family operation. Filtering precedes metadata windows, and unreadable names/counts are absent. Optional canonical `SourcesJson` witnesses in prepared reads and retained references bind each delivered child key, metadata fingerprint and exact owned IAM address. No new record table or execution owner is introduced. The projection dependency is resolved when used, avoiding an eager IAM/provider-configuration DI cycle.

Every delivered child requires its current raw read. Relevant child `Always`/requester/independent policies can require the same exact saved Review even when `agents.read` itself allows execution. That Review supplies no raw permission or future read. Current independent reviewer source rights apply to inspection, decisions, receipt settlement and later reuse. Human child scopes filter Inbox and conversation totals before paging. Source or permission changes cannot release a captured snapshot into the next model turn or protected output.

Targeted diagnosis omits unrelated inventories. Supported exact preference/capability remedies preserve their complete replacement set and revision; Core withholds oversized remedies rather than clipping them into a different action. Definition denial, unavailable host setup, missing delegated area, current Run pins, unsupported targets/goals and readable binding absence produce distinct guidance with canonical Agent-settings destinations. Diagnosis wording normalizes to its target for refusal/root correlation; action hashes still bind the complete exact request and observation. Showing an option does not apply it. [Inspection qualification](reports/tenant-management-inspection-verification-2026-10-22.md).

`configuration.propose` recompiles only an exact option actually observed by this Run through `configuration.inspect`. Original option/target/goal/fields/revision and the independently authorized inspection reference remain bound to the incumbent settings plan and exact consent. Preparation and commit recheck current sources. The three Managed conversation preferences retain ordinary IAM/policy auto-apply; material capability/settings changes require exact review. `credentials.binding.propose/remove` uses the existing association writer, requiring Connections delegation, raw Agent read/update and credential read independently of primary `credentials.bind` consent. Exceptional consent supplies only that primary operation. Inspection exposes readable inactive binding metadata for removal; eligible new credentials remain active-only. No secret, credential use, provider setup or task effect is authorized.

## Canonical Skill and Resource observations

`skills.list({})`, `skills.inspect({key})`, `resources.list({})` and `resources.inspect({key})` share the existing management-read owner and exact `inspect_management` consent. Keys are canonical origin-qualified identities in the executing Agent; callers cannot select a tenant, principal or another Agent. Both admitted/current capabilities and delegated Skills/Resources areas, actual conversational human root, current raw Agent read and current child read remain required. These are current configuration observations for authoring; they do not replace the admitted Run catalog or activate content.

Lists return a bounded window of at most 12 independently readable metadata records. Filtering precedes the window; hidden names, counts, procedures, bytes and private resource dependencies are absent. Exact Skill inspection returns the complete bounded procedure and capability requirements as untrusted data. A distinct `skillProcedure` witness binds that text and its requirements; metadata-only `skill` witnesses remain independent of unshown private content. Procedures are complete or withheld by the observation budget, never silently shortened. Private `skills.load` activation has a separate [read/source boundary](#unified-self-management-application-owners).

Exact Resource inspection verifies immutable bytes against the current owned content hash and length. Supported UTF-8 text returns at most 2048 bytes with explicit `contentProvided` and `truncated` facts; malformed text, missing or corrupt content fails closed. Unsupported binary formats return readable metadata without fetching or returning their bytes. An excerpt cannot serve as a complete replacement value. Existing resource content/download operations retain their own independent authority.

Every delivered child participates in current read Review, independent reviewer and retained-source checks. Approval grants only the exact saved read; raw permissions remain mandatory. Child rights filter Inbox/conversation visibility before counts, and source/permission races withhold the observation before model reentry or protected output. The existing shared inspector labels exact procedures, resource facts/text and excerpt status as escaped data. No write, capability, Skill activation, future read or Credential access is granted. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-management-child-read-verification-2026-10-22.md).

## Tenant model management commands

`TenantManagementOperationTarget` composes the existing configuration planner, read service and preference owner. `settings.patch`, `management.policy.update`, `management.freeze` and `automation.preferences.patch` require the executing Agent’s actual conversational human root, admitted/current capability and management scope, current raw `agents.read`, exact Instance revision and current source. General commands require exact saved Review even with `agents.update`; a one-time exceptional grant supplies only that primary permission. Sandbox selection additionally needs current independent `sandbox.read`/`sandbox.manage` and an owned verified profile. Neither grants nor reviews supply those secondary rights.

Human HTTP and model settings share typed constraints, Definition/resource/Skill validation and exact Automation eligibility reconciliation. Sandbox patches select `profileId` only; they cannot author host setup. Freeze is available without ManagementPolicy scope, removes management immediately and cannot unfreeze or enable Disabled management. Only the existing three conversation preferences may use their incumbent direct effect owner when both policies permit it. Other settings do not inherit that exception.

The existing approval/local-effect transaction revalidates the reconstructed plan and exact preview, applies configuration and observed Automation revisions, and settles Run, receipt, grant, security revision and audit together. Approval continuations retain original configuration/model pins; future grants/settings apply to new Runs, while authority removal/freeze is immediate. Current independent human read and reviewer rights filter inbox counts and decisions. This adds no execution engine, table or permission. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-management-mutation-verification-2026-10-22.md).

## Tenant model Skill authoring

The same management/local-effect owners handle `skills.create`, `skills.update`, `skills.set_enabled`, `skills.reset_enabled`, `skills.delete` and `skills.customize`. `TenantAgentSkillPreparation` shares the incumbent semantic planner with human authoring. Current/admitted Skills delegation, capability, conversational lineage, parent `agents.read` and independent raw `skills.read` remain mandatory. The primary action is **`skills.manage`**, at tenant scope for create/customize and the exact owned child scope for other changes. Parent `agents.update` does not supply it. Models cannot choose another owner.

Managed `SkillChanges` may apply eligible create/update/choice operations only under current Allowed policy. Assisted changes, delete and customize require exact Review; stronger tenant policy and independence remain effective. Exceptional consent supplies only the exact primary action, never raw reads. A source requiring read Review must first receive its own exact consent. Identical content/choices retain their revisions and produce no effect, approval or receipt. Built-in procedures remain immutable choices; Skill tools cannot enable management or unfreeze it. Customization retains immutable source provenance, disables the inherited choice atomically, and rejects bound Definition resources instead of dropping them.

The prepared plan freezes child/configuration witnesses, exact next state and factual result metadata. Both stores commit child/parent state, removed-child assignment retirement, Run effect, immutable receipt, grant consumption and audit together. Results return committed keys, revisions, enabled state and customization source choice without copying procedure text or activating a Skill. Existing Runs retain their admitted catalogs; new content applies to new Runs.

Retained observations advance only through an unbroken chain of the same task's exact receipts, with current raw reads. Deleted content requires current tenant-wide read authority. Approval visibility checks child read/manage rights before counts; child-only history also requires the original Instance revision or its exact committed revision. Deleting, recreating and regranting a readable key cannot recover the old private proposal. Tenant-wide readers/managers can inspect their recorded deletion through its immutable receipt without reviving a child grant. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-skill-authoring-verification-2026-10-22.md). Canonical contextual Harness commands use the owner described below; [private Skill activation](reports/tenant-private-skill-verification-2026-10-22.md) is qualified separately.

## Canonical model Resource authoring

`resources.upsert`, `resources.copy`, `resources.set_enabled` and `resources.delete` use the incumbent configuration planner, exact consent owner and local-effect transaction. Current raw `agents.read` and independent `resources.read` are mandatory; `resources.manage` is the primary write, while deletion uses `resources.delete`. New bindings/copies require tenant destination authority. One child grant cannot create a sibling. `enabled:null` resets an inherited choice. Immutable inherited bytes remain read-only; a copy binds the actual selected published hash/length to a new local identity and provenance.

Upsert accepts a bounded literal UTF-8 draft derived from actual owned root user entries or a successful paired checkpoint observation. Source labels alone are insufficient. Workspace, Knowledge, Office, Attachment/virtual Attachment and Browser observations retain their existing current raw reads, source checks, capabilities and any separate exact read consent; an approved source retains its original reviewer requirements. The saved proposal identifies the factual source and explicitly labels the draft as derived literal text, without claiming byte equivalence. Copy uses actual verified immutable bytes instead. Reviewers need independent current source reads; source ownership is checked before historical fallback. Hidden proposals are filtered before Inbox counts and paging.

Only new bounded text Resources with both admitted/current Managed `TextResources` delegation and current Allowed operation policy may auto-apply. Replacement, copy, choices and deletion require exact consent. Stronger policy, independent review and exceptional primary-only grants remain effective. Identical local drafts and repeated choices produce no revision, blob staging, request or receipt. Private blob staging follows qualified execution preflight; the existing transaction verifies the binding and rechecks the exact plan, current authority and claim before committing child/parent/security/audit/Run/receipt/grant state.

`TenantManagementCommandCommit` serves the existing direct Skill and Resource variants; it does not add an execution owner. Resource receipts contain bounded factual metadata and source fingerprints, never source bodies or read authority. The current Run retains its original content pins. An unbroken exact same-root receipt chain can explain its own updates, disables and deletions for retained observations and original-byte Workspace/Knowledge/Artifact reads, with current raw permissions. External revision gaps, replacement ownership and revoked reads fail closed; a new Run sees the new configuration. [Scoped verification and remaining work](reports/multitenant-iam-delivery-2026-10-16.md#canonical-model-resource-authoring).

## Canonical contextual Harness commands

The existing five commands (`harness.knowledge.upsert/remove`, `harness.instructions.update`, `harness.tool.select/configure`) enter `TenantManagementOperationTarget` and compile to the existing Resource/settings planner. They retain their distinct KnowledgeResources, Instructions and ToolSelection delegation; generic Settings/Resources scopes neither supply nor become prerequisites for those areas. The actual conversational human root, claimed Run, admitted/current policy, current raw parent/child/source reads and exact original arguments remain mandatory. No tenant command falls back to the unscoped Harness authoring service or publishes a shared Definition.

Knowledge upsert maps a simple name to an Instance-local `knowledge/` Resource. Remove deletes a local Knowledge Resource or disables the exact inherited knowledge choice. Actual source evidence and independent read consent follow the Resource contract above. Canonical public Web/HTTP tools remain unavailable through the tenant execution boundary; naming their URLs supplies no provenance. Managed knowledge may apply under current Allowed operation policy without requiring the generic TextResources choice. Assisted knowledge, instruction replacement and all tool changes require exact consent; sensitive deletion, stronger tenant review, independent review and primary-only exceptional grants retain their controls.

Instruction replacement uses the incumbent complete 1–8000-character instruction bound and rejects larger input without clipping. Tool selection preserves other selections and order, and intersects admitted/current eligibility, Definition authority and configured availability. Configuration supports only the existing `attachments.read` legacy flag within its Definition ceiling. The original command and expected/observed/limitation assessment are frozen beside the derived plan; execution recompiles and compares them before the existing atomic owner commits. Every saved assessment is Agent-authored and PartiallyVerified regardless of wording in `observed`.

Content/settings and bounded Instance-local assessment history commit together. Identical writes return saved/unchanged without a new revision, request, receipt or assessment. A changed command returns factual result identity and partial verification; current Runs retain their original configuration. Resource receipts bind the assessment ID and bounded before metadata so the existing exact same-root receipt chain can retain original observations/content pins. Neither history nor a receipt supplies read authority. [Scoped evidence and remaining work](reports/multitenant-iam-delivery-2026-10-16.md#canonical-contextual-harness-commands).

## Tenant model Automation read port

`ITenantAutomationReadStore` observes a bounded exact `PreparedAutomationRead` under a trusted tenant Run/generation and validates retained `TenantAutomationReadReference` sources. Both incumbent IAM stores implement the facet over existing Automation, trigger, delivery and coverage records. No Profile-backed fallback exists. Own-Agent configuration requires current `automations.read`; returned event metadata additionally requires its Core/Webhook and Email-account source rights. A read Review cannot grant any missing raw permission. Approved inspection returns the saved observation only while every source and reviewer remains current; a changed parent or event fails closed. Empty readable lists need no consent or source proof.

## Tenant model Automation authoring port

`ITenantAutomationMutationStore` is a facet of the incumbent IAM/Automation/Run transaction. `TenantAutomationMutationService` prepares canonical create/update/enable/disable/delete from the actual claimed Run, immutable root request entries or admitted task, current owned parent and current destinations. Tenant, executor, requester and root are never model arguments. Shared `TenantAutomationAuthoring` and parsing-only `TriggerScheduleCommands.NormalizeTenant` retain the existing timing, trigger, model and provenance owners. Relative timing uses the immutable root reference time. Model-authored resources record `AgentTask` provenance without fabricating human or event authorship.

Normalization resolves the actual source before applying the [model activation defaults](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#canonical-model-authoring). Parent activation remains distinct from child subscription flags and supplies no intent, IAM or consent authority. Saved records are unchanged.

Current raw Agent/Automation/source/destination reads, source operation Review, primary IAM and admitted/current capability policy are independent. Semantic classification checks the exact normalized Before/After against actual task intent and grants no IAM permission. Deny/Ambiguous refuse; unavailable/invalid verification requires exact human review. Existing configuration requiring source Review is withheld from the classifier until consent; the qualified human reviews its saved Before/After instead. Disable/delete retain history and require current parent reads, without unrelated future-source admission rights.

Allowed changes commit parent CAS, Run effect, immutable body-free typed receipt, security revision and audit atomically. Review/exceptional grants use the existing approval and local-effect owners; a primary-only grant never supplies secondary rights. Saved plans, current scheduling horizon/capacity, claim, parent, destinations, requester and reviewer are revalidated. Exact replay returns the factual prior receipt; tool plus arguments bind call identity. A bounded same-root, same-executor receipt/hash chain may explain the task's own later changes to an observed parent; it cannot waive raw reads, unrelated edits or event witnesses.

`automation.run` joins this exact-operation owner with a frozen `PreparedAutomationInvocation`. It admits one manual occurrence from the actual current human request root, retaining the incumbent manual-invoker raw Agent/Automation read/run checks. An independently scheduled task cannot invent that requester. The executor separately needs current `automations.run`, `agent-runs.create`, new-Session creation where applicable, and saved destination rights/policy; a grant substitutes only the primary run permission. Current model/configuration pins, exact conversation snapshot, overlap and active-resource checks remain mandatory. Semantic review covers the saved task and actual occurrence model.

The local transaction commits the invoking Run's effect/receipt, unchanged configuration with advanced parent revision, accepted occurrence and its fresh Session/Run graph together. Existing-conversation admission creates only a Run in that exact conversation, preserving creator/origin; it requires no new-Session permission. A policy-required invocation creates no occurrence or execution graph before the exact qualified decision. Body-free receipt metadata binds the accepted occurrence/Session/Run IDs. Replays return those factual references without another admission. Admission and later execution completion are separate outcomes; future tools retain their own authority.

## Tenant autonomous Automation ownership and occurrence admission

Automation and occurrence owners use the actual tenant, Agent Instance and executor principal, with no global profile or fictitious human. Existing Automation/trigger/occurrence rows and scheduling calculations remain the owners. Canonical IAM/Automation facets read and write those rows with explicit tenant identity; unscoped legacy trigger readers and workers exclude tenant rows. Human authoring, scoped reads, updates, cancellation and explicit run requests use current original-human authority and optimistic security/Instance/Automation witnesses. Registration author provenance is audit context, never a recurring occurrence's requester authority.

`TenantAutomationAuthoring` owns normalized construction of tenant Schedule/Event configuration, preserving existing child identities and optimistic revisions. Both canonical stores independently validate the authoring transition against the actual saved parent. An ordinary edit of an active unchanged Schedule retains its selected next occurrence, including already-due work; it cannot rewrite accepted occurrence counts. Changing timing or re-enabling selects the next future occurrence through the incumbent calculator. An elapsed one-shot may remain due during an ordinary active edit, but cannot be newly created, retimed into the past or re-enabled after expiry. Current trigger policy still applies. Cancellation preserves exact configuration and history. These construction rules confer no IAM, source, destination, model or execution authority.

Each scheduled occurrence starts a fresh activation/root without a human initiator or previous approval/grant. An explicit human manual invocation also starts a fresh root, binds its actual current invoker, and applies requester-based operation policy; it never inherits the registration author or fabricates `agents.chat` admission. The existing atomic occurrence/Session/Run admission graph derives its tenant/executor, current configuration/model/Skills/budgets and source evidence from the owned registration. The Session creator may be the actual executor principal; schema and transactions require a same-tenant creator, and an autonomous creator must equal the owned executor. Current Agent creation/operation permissions, trigger policy, destination ownership and lifecycle constrain admission and execution. Existing coordinator/mailbox owners execute accepted work. No additional scheduler, task engine, grant store or ambient tenant is introduced.

Due admission uses a bounded stable ID cursor; blocked registrations cannot permanently monopolize a batch. Exact technical schedule closure advances without creating execution. Configuration adoption and lifecycle reference counting use the same owned registration rows. `WhenRequesterLacksPermission` permits an explicitly admitted independent schedule under its current executor/trigger controls; `Always` requires Review.

Each event/watch source, destination and authoring surface retains independent qualification. Existing-Session destinations and automatic completion reporting use the contracts below; Email watches use the canonical account and source owners. Model authoring and manual invocation use the port above. An unavailable conversion path cannot silently use a legacy owner or manufacture requester authority.

## Tenant abandoned account dispatch recovery

An abandoned canonical account-action reservation settles through the existing recovery/Run/receipt owner as Indeterminate without entering a native account operation. This remains distinct from any durable native Email send or Drive verification fact already recorded before canonical settlement was lost. Native facts remain intact; absence of a canonical acknowledgement never authorizes a second send, upload, mutation or batch suffix. Claimed exceptional grants become Indeterminate and confer no future authority.

[Focused host qualification](reports/tenant-account-abandoned-host-verification-2026-10-20.md) records the tested crash windows and limitations. A later Drive inspection uses separate exact account-read consent with current raw original Session/Run and account reads. It reports the original canonical Indeterminate outcome separately from retained native success, fresh metadata verification or missing evidence. The old write grant is neither reused nor consumed by that read. Authority loss after dispatch does not prevent body-free factual recovery, and does not release private result content.

## Tenant read-only Drive operation reconciliation

The offered `drive.get` read accepts an exact owned `operationId` instead of `fileId`, through the existing `read_connected_account` consent owner. A frozen optional operation source binds the original canonical account-action request, operation/action hash, factual receipt, effect-owning Run, Session and immutable native intent references. The original and current binding/Credential/auth epoch must identify the same logical account. Current raw original Session and Run reads remain independent of account metadata/read/use; the old write permission or exceptional grant grants no read authority and is not consumed again.

Preparation performs only local owned lookups. Current compound source/reviewer checks govern pending consent, native verification, retained result reuse and atomic final answer persistence. Only a canonical tenant account-action receipt on the same Agent/account can resolve a native ID; a bare legacy/native ID or another owner's ID cannot. Reconciliation uses only existing native operation inspection under a live scoped read callback. Upload inspection sends no bytes and never resumes an upload. Batch inspection reads only existing deterministic item receipts and cannot dispatch an unattempted suffix. Missing retained native evidence remains unknown rather than proof of nonexecution. Original download operations expose only the recorded canonical execution fact; no local file or current-byte verification is implied.

The original canonical effect receipt stays immutable. A subsequent bounded untrusted read result separately reports recorded outcome and current native verification facts, with replay forbidden. Cached native success is labeled as retained verification; fresh metadata verification is distinct. Failed, pending, uncertain and missing evidence remain factual. No new effect, grant, queue, table or reconciliation executor is introduced. Exact operation-source evidence is immutable in the existing approval payload and is guarded on SQLite insertion/update; downgrade refuses retained operation-source consent.

## Tenant account-family discovery through the existing metadata owner

Converted `email.accounts` and `drive.accounts` perform bounded read-only discovery through `TenantCredentialRuntimeReadService`, without Credential use, provider calls or secret resolution. Their cursor/limit arguments derive an internal exact `family=email|drive` discriminator. The existing `inspect_credential_metadata` Review accepts the same discriminator, freezes one exact filtered page and grants no missing raw read permission. The admitted and current family capability, current Session read and each Credential metadata plus family read are independent. Filter before cursor/limit/hasMore; an unreadable family cannot influence visible counts or continuation. Generic `credentials.list` keeps its existing metadata-only scope.

Family discovery returns safe aliases, labels/kinds, saved connection identity/status/auth epoch and Synthetic indication as non-secret metadata. Connection facts are not provider availability, account-use authority or permission to send/write. Saved page/retained output witnesses bind family, exact current bindings and Credential revisions. Recheck independent source and reviewer scope before subsequent model use and within the owning final-answer transaction, including empty pages. Human approval counts/page/detail require every frozen family read as well as metadata and Session reads. Unsupported origins and automatic report execution remain unavailable. This extends the existing read/approval/Run/receipt owners without a second inventory or consent state machine.

## Tenant account-action execution owner

The internal `TenantAccountActionTarget` prepares exact `email.create_draft`, `email.send`, Drive create/upload/download/rename/move/trash/batch intents under actual claimed Run, Session, binding and Credential ownership. Preparation performs no provider I/O: required remote metadata must already exist in current exact approved `drive.get` observations; upload captures canonical owned source bytes and download captures a new-destination footprint. `PreparedAccountAction` freezes canonical arguments, immutable draft content or source witnesses. Primary family authority/grants never supply raw Session, Credential metadata/use, Drive source reads or workspace source/destination access. Secondary operation policy also applies; Core's `credentials.use` minimum requires exact compound Review even when the primary draft/write decision alone would be Allowed.

`ITenantAccountOperationStore` is a facet of the existing IAM/Run/approval/receipt owner. Core draft creation, exact local receipt, grant consumption and Run update commit atomically with `IEmailStore`. Remote actions reserve the actual Run and claim any one-time primary grant before native I/O. `ITenantConnectedAccountTransportFactory` reuses operation-local scoped Gmail/IMAP/Drive adapters; plaintext tokens remain inside Infrastructure. `TenantAccountEffectService` runs outside the mailbox through the existing continuation worker and rechecks current authority before token refresh, dispatch and transfer chunks. Existing immutable draft-send claims and Drive native-operation receipts prevent replay. Factual settlement survives caller cancellation; private result details require current compound authority in the settlement transaction, before later model use and at atomic final output. Abandoned dispatch settles as Indeterminate without provider calls.

The capability-filtered model offering and shared exact account-action inspector now use this owner. `TenantAccountActionResults` reads bounded historical details from the exact existing receipt and retained Run checkpoint after current human scope; atomic local draft facts survive checkpoint compaction, while unretained provider details remain unavailable. Missing details permit no replay. No new executor, queue or table is introduced. [Model/UI qualification](reports/tenant-account-action-model-ui-verification-2026-10-20.md) keeps bounded presentation and actual execution evidence distinct. [Family discovery qualification](reports/tenant-account-discovery-verification-2026-10-20.md) now covers the metadata owner above; read-only operation-ID reconciliation and abandoned-dispatch cold-host qualification remain separate.


[Focused internal-owner evidence](reports/tenant-account-action-owner-verification-2026-10-20.md) distinguishes this checkpoint from remaining module acceptance.

## Tenant Agent account-read consent

Human-attributed conversational, immediate-background and exact approval-successor Runs may request `email.search`, `email.read`, `drive.list`, `drive.search` or `drive.get` through the existing authorization tool. `PreparedAccountRead` freezes one canonical bounded query and exact current binding/Credential witnesses. Primary `credentials.use` supports Review or a primary-only exceptional grant; raw `sessions.read`, `credentials.read` and `email.read`/`drive.read` remain independent and cannot be supplied by that grant. The existing local receipt authorizes only this read; it does not assert data delivery. `ITenantConnectedAccountReadStore` also owns normalized runtime OAuth maintenance and final read audit under the actual claimed frame. Saved untrusted results carry exact request/receipt/source provenance, rechecked before model use and in the atomic final-outcome transaction. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-agent-account-read-verification-2026-10-20.md) is separate from human HTTP read qualification. Agent account-family discovery and exact transfers/writes/send have separate qualification under the owners above. Autonomous origins and remaining proposal families remain open.

## Tenant bound-account reads

Canonical human Email/Drive reads resolve the exact tenant Agent binding under the original authenticated human. Agent metadata, Credential metadata/use and the corresponding email.read or drive.read remain independent raw permissions. List filtering precedes totals/pages. Provider tokens stay inside Infrastructure; the reader receives encrypted canonical account state and callbacks that repeat current authority around I/O and commit normalized protected refresh rotation/reconnect facts through the existing OAuth owner. Returned Email/Drive data remains untrusted. Original Drive bytes require exact version, bounded transfer and post-I/O source/authority checks. No send/write or agent-use authority is conferred. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-connected-account-read-verification-2026-10-20.md) covers scoped HTTP reads, controlled Google transport and exact audit commits in Memory/SQLite.

## Tenant background native effects

Human-attributed immediate-background tasks and their exact approval successors may use the existing tenant Browser and Sandbox native owners. Native mutations/commands remain exact authorization intents; direct inspection remains read-only and policy-bound. Current raw source/secondary permissions, admitted/current capabilities, state isolation, original-human lineage, native target/generation witnesses and receipt/recovery rules remain independent. The same child Session owns native state and generated Artifacts. Background capability is required at native preparation, dispatch and stored-read calls; completion reports cannot create native work. Protected Browser password fill retains its direct UserTurn lineage requirement. No native executor, queue, effect receipt or permission family is added. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-background-native-verification-2026-10-20.md) records actual native Chromium, controlled Sandbox SDK and both-store evidence. This does not establish autonomous or live-container acceptance.

## Automatic report source-read consent

Automatic completion reporting requires source-read authority independently from report-Run creation. A server-prepared `PreparedReportSourceRead` binds the original destination, exact initial/final child, source revision and bounded report observation/hash. It is ordinary Review only and cannot substitute for raw source reads. The terminal child remains unchanged; the existing approval signal settles a local read receipt before separately preparing report creation. The source-read receipt grants only this unchanged report source, with current original-human, executor, capability, configuration and approving-human checks repeated at admission, provider context and final-save. Negative/expired/stale consent does not disclose data or create a report. Pending read/creation consent is excluded before bounded coordinator selection so other ready completions can proceed. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-report-source-read-verification-2026-10-20.md) records the bounded Memory/SQLite, HTTP and browser evidence.

## Exact tenant completion read consent

`PreparedCompletionRead` binds one terminal initial child and its exact final successor, source revision, bounded untrusted observation/hash and independently readable artifact metadata. `inspect_background_completion` in the existing authorization tool prepares ordinary read Review; no exceptional grant substitutes for raw Session/Run/Artifact reads. The existing approval, local read receipt and successor-Run owners deliver the saved observation. The receipt covers this exact source in the same task root, allowing claim/acknowledgment and final-answer use without future discovery authority. Current source identity, raw reads, reviewer qualifications, pinned/current capability and metadata remain required.

Automatic completion reports independently require current child Session and initial/source Run reads at preparation/admission/provider/final-save. Required source-read Review uses the separate terminal-source producer/receipt above before independent report creation. Report-Run creation consent does not substitute.

## Tenant completion inbox runtime facet

`ITenantCompletionRuntimeStore` is a narrow facet of the existing tenant Run store, bound to the actual parent mailbox frame. It projects owned initial children and exact approval successors, checks current child Session and initial/source Run reads and operation policy before paging/counting, and independently filters at most two artifact metadata links. Reads grant no consumption authority. Take and acknowledgment recheck the live generation, pinned/current explicit capability and original requester; the final source Run owns inbox accounting while the initial identity remains the public task identity. Reportable terminal tasks alone support take; `reportCompletion=false` does not acquire automatic reporting intent.

## Independent child and inbox conversion contract

`TenantBackgroundCreationRules`, `TenantBackgroundAdmissionRules` and `TenantExecutionLineage` admit a child only from a current owned task and preserve its exact trusted causal chain. Independent schedules use the actual executor as the Session creator, while preserving a null original requester. Manual invocation retains the actual invoker and current Automation read/run access. Every direct and approved admission repeats root/requester checks. An exact primary creation grant substitutes for only one of `sessions.create` / `agent-runs.create`; the other permission, current capability and source authority remain mandatory.

`TenantTaskExecutionContract` recognizes a child through its trusted immediate-background origin, actual parent/root and frozen AgentRun task source; an approval successor retains that task intent. Store-backed lineage must reach an admitted user root or the exact immutable accepted Schedule/manual occurrence. Model-supplied lineage is never accepted. Existing completion/wait/read-consent and report owners apply their ordinary current read, reviewer, source, claim and final-save checks to these tasks. The same-root refusal and quota rules remain intact. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-independent-child-verification-2026-10-20.md) records the converted scheduled/manual and nested-child paths. Completion-report Runs cannot create further children.

## Tenant typed wait ownership

Human-attributed tenant conversational and immediate-background Runs, plus admitted Schedule/manual Automation Background tasks and their exact approval successors, may use the explicitly authorized `execution.wait` capability. Independent admission requires the actual frozen Automation task and accepted occurrence lineage; a schedule has no human requester, while a manual task retains the actual invoker and current Automation read/run access. A wait supplies no child creation or inbox authority. Core freezes the closed duration/background condition from its pending tool call and current checkpoint. Existing limits remain eight targets, 300 seconds per wait, eight waits and 900 requested seconds per Run. Background targets are exact initial children of this parent Session; each ready/status observation separately requires current executor child Session and initial/source Run read under current operation policy. A policy requiring separate read consent is unavailable through wait, never bypassed by its status result. Persisted exact approval handoffs select the task's current Run; an initial Continued outcome alone is not task completion. Foreign, missing or unreadable admission targets return NotFound without a wait.

The existing Run transaction saves WaitingForSignal without a claim or provider worker. The incumbent coordinator resumes the same Run/Activation/Response with a fresh generation and unchanged logical attempt, appending one normal result. Lost or newly unreadable targets become Unavailable, without disclosing their current status. Duration elapsed, background timeout and unavailable targets do not consume or acknowledge completion accounting. Source observations are checked again before provider context use. Completion-report Runs cannot wait or acquire new work authority.

The existing Runtime-admission checkpoint boundary, with an optional typed signal discriminator, also fences unaccepted tenant signal continuations. Capacity/collision deferral and pre-admission recovery retain the sealed wait result and logical attempt; the actual mailbox clears the flag before running. Waiting retains exclusive Session execution ownership while new human input queues separately. Stop and host recreation retain the existing canonical owners. No new queue, worker, permission family or schema is introduced. Completion list/inspect/take/acknowledge conversion remains separate work until qualified.

Independent Schedule/manual duration waits, including exact approval successors, have [focused runtime and recovery qualification](reports/tenant-independent-wait-verification-2026-10-20.md). Their child/inbox controls remain separate open work.

## Tenant completion-report creation consent

A requested completion report is an independent `agent-runs.create` operation in the existing parent Session. When current policy or missing delegatable authority requires consent, the existing completion coordinator prepares one exact ApprovalRequest against the terminal child Run. The child remains terminal and unchanged; no report Run is admitted while consent is pending. Core binds the completed source, parent Session/revision/input boundary, exact bounded completion evidence, report identities, model and current configuration. This is trusted continuation of the original explicit report intent, not a model-call or arbitrary autonomous initiation API.

The incumbent approval inbox, decision, one-time grant, expiry, processed signal and operation receipt owners remain authoritative. Readers/reviewers need independent source child Session/Run and parent Session read plus raw `agent-runs.create` and current decision rights. Grants cover only that exact report creation, never Session creation, source reads or later work. Current original-human root communication, executor capability, source/configuration/model/parent witnesses and reviewer/grantor authority are repeated before admission. Busy parents defer; changed witnesses refuse rather than adapting consent.

The completion-inbox owner atomically admits the frozen report Run, accounts the source, consumes the exact grant, records a factual receipt/security revision/audit and processes the existing approval signal. Negative decisions skip this requested delivery without rewriting the source result. Approved but unavailable effects record a denied receipt and retire any grant. Retries return saved facts; no second Run, report or provider call follows. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-background-report-creation-verification-2026-10-20.md) records this bounded implementation. The full proposal stays active.


## Tenant background creation consent

An exact background-start request binds the owned parent Session, objective, title, completion preference, Core-generated child identities, selected model and effective configuration witness. It creates no child until the approved local operation commits. The existing approval, continuation, receipt and Session/Run owners remain authoritative. Initial human tasks and conversational approval successors may request this operation; background children and report Runs cannot fan out.

Session creation and Run creation are independent tenant-scoped requirements. Core chooses one missing creation permission as the primary operation when the other remains independently held; an exceptional grant substitutes only for that primary permission. Missing both refuses with no partial graph or blanket grant. Review can cover policy-required compound creation with independent current raw authority on both operations. Reviewers need the underlying permissions and applicable decision/independent-review authority on every required target. Reading the request requires its parent Session and both creation permissions; filtering precedes totals/pages.

The existing local-operation transaction rechecks current requester root communication, executor, configuration/model/Instance witnesses, both creation permissions/policies and reviewer qualification. It atomically creates one child Session/Activation/Run and records the parent successor effect, canonical receipt, exact grant consumption, security revision and audit. IDs are frozen before consent; same-root semantic suppression excludes generated IDs and refreshed witnesses. Retries read committed facts and never create another child. Rejection/expiry/cancellation/revocation creates no child. A child inherits the root and points to the actual executing successor; parent completion reports retain their existing current-scope rules. Completion delivery creates a new Run in the existing parent Session: it rechecks only that actual creation permission plus current root/executor/capability authority. A child-creation grant cannot authorize another report Run. Missing delegatable Run creation authority or policy-required review prepares its own exact completion-report request through the [report consent contract](04-backend-interfaces.md#tenant-completion-report-creation-consent). Negative decisions or unsafe resumption account that delivery without changing the finished child. [Focused qualification](reports/tenant-background-creation-verification-2026-10-20.md) records the executed boundary; the full proposal remains active.

## Tenant background file mutation and publication

The approved tenant conversion extends the incumbent Workspace/Office mutation and Artifact publication owners to human-attributed immediate-background tasks and exact same-task approval successors. Admitted/current capabilities, current independent executor and source permissions, state isolation, exact file witnesses, primary-only exceptional grants, one-time dispatch/publication receipts and original-human/root authority remain mandatory. Direct Allowed operations and reviewed operations use the same existing file stores and operation owners; no background effect executor or new approval authority is introduced.

Every later operation is independently authorized. A mutation grant cannot grant source reads, secondary batch permissions, another mutation, Artifact creation or file download. Pending/rejected/changed actions retain root suppression and exact no-replay recovery; only the terminal task successor reports saved facts. Published files belong to the actual child Session. Neither a publication receipt nor a factual parent report grants recipient file access. Office content remains untrusted and immutable export preserves the observed original bytes.

This module does not open Browser/Sandbox execution, Credentials/configuration changes, child fanout, autonomous origins or report-generated effects. The original full proposal remains active. [Focused backend qualification](reports/tenant-background-file-effects-verification-2026-10-20.md) records integrated workflows, current-scope refusal, host recreation and regression limits.

## Canonical tenant queued human input

Tenant text/file messages retain a fresh human-attributed UserTurn root and one exact admitted Run per message. While a claimed task owns the Session, admission goes through that Runtime's mailbox and serialized persistence worker. It commits current output progress, the new immutable user entry, same-Session Attachment bindings, queued Activation/Run, security revision and canonical human audit atomically. Current original-human authentication, Session read/Agent chat, executor/configuration/lifecycle, revision and exact live-generation checks repeat at commit. Quiescent intake reserves the existing host write barrier against dispatch and uses the incumbent admission transaction.

The mailbox reserves an opaque entry sequence before asynchronous persistence so later output cannot collide with a queued input. Refused commands add no entry, binding, Run or audit and do not fail independent work; a harmless sequence gap may remain. Only committed metadata and sequence/activity high-water marks are carried into later snapshots. The active task keeps its saved input boundary and cannot consume the queued human turn. A fresh Run later reads that exact attributed input; reports continue to yield to human work. Queue bounds, same-message dedupe and restart durability reuse existing owners. No raw audio, copied cookie, new queue table or competing Session writer is introduced.

`TenantHumanInput` carries server-prepared owner, original-human and frozen admission witnesses. `ITenantSessionInputControl` routes intake through the current owner; `ITenantRuntimeInputWriter` is the claimed Runtime's scoped adapter; `ITenantRuntimeInputStore` owns the atomic frame-checked write. `TenantHumanInputCommit` returns the saved admission, filtered Runtime snapshot and security revision. Caller cancellation stops waiting; already-enqueued work remains supervised and must be recovered by exact message identity under current authority.

Queue remains the default and does not cancel the current task. Explicit tenant interrupt/Steer uses the exact atomic extension below; voice admission/arbitration uses the canonical Voice lease and speech-resource boundary. Controller behavior remains in its existing owner; tenant input requires actual trusted execution context.

## Tenant immediate-background file observation

The approved tenant conversion extends the existing file read and durable read-consent owners to trusted immediate-background Runs and their exact approval continuations. Supported tools are the canonical Workspace list/read/search, Office inspection, admitted knowledge/resource reads, bound Attachment reads and Artifact metadata verification. Independent current executor Session/resource reads, admitted/current capability and state-isolation limits, exact source witnesses, current original-human authority on the stored root conversation and claim/generation fences remain mandatory. Background identity is derived from saved lineage; no copied human session or permission snapshot is introduced.

Read Review uses the existing exact request, independent reviewer, same-root suppression, local consent receipt, successor Run and retained-checkpoint revalidation. The successor preserves BackgroundOutcome, task, parent/root identity and same owned Session; it still finishes through work.complete and reports only terminal saved facts. Source changes or permission loss cannot substitute new evidence or replay consent. Background request_authorization exposes these converted file observations and the separately authorized mutation/publication family above. Child fanout, credentials and native Browser/Sandbox effects remain gated until their separate conversions; this is implementation order, not a reduction of the full proposal. [Initial qualification](reports/tenant-background-file-observation-verification-2026-10-20.md) records the earlier active-binding gap. The [queued-input successor](reports/tenant-live-input-queue-verification-2026-10-20.md) qualifies active binding plus actual bound Attachment reads through explicit capable definitions and current consent. Raw IAM still cannot add a missing admitted capability; future human context remains outside the active task input window.


## Tenant Background Activity and explicit foregrounding

`TenantActivityService` uses the original live human and current `agents.read` plus independently scoped `sessions.read` and `agent-runs.read`. Session listing selects the exact Agent and all surfaces (or BackgroundWork only), includes archived/zero-run contexts and orders by updated time descending then Session ID. Readable IDs and owner/surface filters apply before totals/paging. Exact Session/Run reads validate tenant/Agent/Session ownership independently of loaded pages. Missing Run read withholds initial result and completion facts; missing parent read withholds the report target. Exact Agent-wide Run pages intersect allowed Session and Run IDs before count/paging; Background pages reuse the same independently authorized detail projection. Run projections expose normalized task/progress/outcome/failure facts, actual model key, diagnostic ID and cancellation state, never claims, checkpoints, tokens or tool results. Original initial-Run identity survives later human turns.

`TenantSessionForegroundService` prepares trusted owner/human/revision references. Continue requires current Session read/manage and Agent chat plus an active executor/Instance and eligible retained BackgroundWork Session. `ITenantSessionForegroundControl` routes active work through its actual Runtime mailbox and serialized persistence worker; `ITenantRuntimeForegroundWriter` commits the exact surface intent with current human/session/IAM and the live generation. Only that writer may add ChatList. Failed human commands do not fail independently authorized work or optimistically change a later snapshot. Successful visibility is carried into subsequent queued snapshots.

`ITenantSessionForegroundStore` reuses existing Session/security/audit transactions. Quiescent continuation updates only surface/revision/time while the tenant host reserves against Runtime dispatch, sharing the existing receipt-write reservation. A claim/dispatch race refuses rather than creating another writer. Active saves may advance the prepared Session revision under the same proven generation; quiescent writes require the exact observed revision. The command changes no origin, task, Run, root, lifecycle, pending input or approval state. Fresh-witness repeats retain visibility without another audit. Opening an already ChatList-visible Session requires current read/chat, while adding visibility still requires raw management authority. [Backend qualification](reports/tenant-background-activity-verification-2026-10-20.md) and [tenant UI qualification](reports/tenant-background-activity-ui-verification-2026-10-20.md).

## Tenant immediate-background admission

`ITenantRuntimeStore.AdmitTenantImmediateAsync` is an internal atomic admission boundary. It receives a server-held parent Runtime frame and a factory-built child, never public caller-selected tenant/executor/requester/root/parent identity. `BackgroundSessionAdmissionFactory.ForImmediate` derives the child's immutable trusted context from its tenant parent, preserves original initiator and approval root, names the exact parent Run/Activation and uses no local profile. The one existing Session/entry/Activation/Run schema and immutable tenant Session root remain the owners.

The owning transaction requires an active owned UserTurn source with the exact live generation and unexpired claim, current active tenant/executor/Instance, current and admitted background capability, current Instance/persona revision and exact canonical child shape. Independent executor `sessions.create` and `agent-runs.create` apply to future roots. Original requester communication authority remains checked on the parent. Creation policy requiring Review refuses with `background_review_required`; this primitive cannot authorize or invent consent. Current capabilities and policy are checked again even for exact retries.

Same owner/parent/tool-call key and unchanged task/title/report preference returns the original admission without a second write. Conflicting payloads refuse, including after ownership tombstones. Per-parent and active-owner background limits apply. Owned Session, original input, Activation/Run lineage, security revision and body-free executor-attributed audit commit together; refused, cancelled, stale or conflicting preparation leaves no child or audit. SQLite recreation retains the same trusted identity and dedupe; InMemory provides equivalent atomicity.

`TenantBackgroundToolContract` offers `background.start` only to an eligible actual UserTurn and `work.complete` only to its immediate child. The scoped Runtime port binds admission and `CommitTenantOutcomeAsync` to its captured Run/generation. Outcome, Session revision/history and canonical Run completion commit atomically. Child fanout and other protected/native background adapters remain closed.

`TenantExecutionLineage.RootAsync` resolves actual saved parents, same owner/executor/requester/root and recorded continuation handoffs. Current communication authority is checked on the original human conversation, never borrowed from a new child Session or copied web session. Every child/report also requires current background capability.

The bounded technical completion service lists pending owned receipts, admits one factory-built report under current creation/communication authority and settles factual report results through existing owners. Busy parents yield before paging; an admitted report yields again to any newer queued human input. The pinned original input boundary hides newer entries from report input while preserving those durable entries. Core formats admitted terminal evidence without another model generation. Quiet outcomes remain quiet; failed/cancelled child facts may report once; unavailable or failed report work is skipped without replay.

`ITenantSessionStore.ListTenantSessionsAsync` accepts an optional typed surface filter before count/paging. Canonical public Chat listing selects `ChatList`; internal technical queries may include all Sessions. Authorized background metadata/history reads remain independent, while live chat attachment and human-message admission require `ChatList`. Explicit canonical Continue now uses the owner above; direct attachment still cannot implicitly foreground work.

[Runtime qualification](reports/tenant-background-runtime-verification-2026-10-20.md) supersedes the admission-only checkpoint for these core paths. Creation consent, protected/native effects, Activity/foregrounding, inbox/waits and independent Automation admission use their dedicated current owners in this document. That dated runtime checkpoint does not define the status of later conversions; full proposal acceptance remains separately tracked.

## Tenant Agent decommission

Original live humans can inspect exact Agent decommission preflight and retire an archived, unreferenced Agent under independent raw `agents.read` and `agents.delete`. The owning IAM transaction repeats human/session/status, current permission, security/Instance revisions and incumbent live Session, learned-memory, Automation/occurrence, Run/Approval/Activation blockers. Pending Browser reset also blocks retirement. No grant or model identity substitutes for the human.

Retirement uses the existing SystemIdentity/Principal `Removed` status, archived backing Instance and canonical `agents.decommission` audit receipt. This retained owner tombstone permanently fences ID reuse and restore; live inventory/configuration/resource discovery excludes it. Exact Agent, home/Skill/resource assignments and executor assignments retire atomically with credential bindings and local child configuration. Retained history is not cascaded. Same-operation readback requires current original-human authority and exact target/actor/Instance witness; it cannot repeat logical retirement.

Committed tombstones drive bounded technical cleanup in the incumbent supervised resource sweep. This cleanup can retry exact retired roots after logout/restart, unlike a user-requested profile reset with an uncertain outcome. Existing native profile close/path/lock/absence checks and the canonical no-follow workspace purge remain the sinks. Live or uncertain Sandbox mounts block workspace purge. Cleanup never launches a Browser or grants resource use; saved decommission acknowledgement and physical cleanup completion stay distinct. The existing Instance row remains as the FK-backed owner marker, without a parallel ownership/deletion table.

## System Browser privacy administration

`BrowserPrivacyService` now owns only trusted host startup activation, projection and policy preparation. `IBrowserPrivacyStore` is read-only; it cannot save through a singleton owner or Admin history. Human writes use `SystemBrowserPrivacyService` and `ISystemBrowserPrivacyStore.TrySaveSystemBrowserPrivacyAsync` on the existing IAM adapter. The command contains expected policy revision, closed mode, exact origin lists and explicit exposure acknowledgement. Original Account/login/global User Principal, raw `system.governance.manage` and immutable deployment ceilings repeat in the owning policy/audit transaction. No tenant role, model, exceptional grant or owner capability substitutes.

Both stores reuse the shared System-human guard used by System administration/operators, then retain the independent Browser policy/audit checks. They use the existing global BrowserPrivacy row and canonical nullable-tenant AuthorizationAudit. Audit names the real global actor, action and safe reason, without origin lists or policy payload. CAS protects policy revision. The read facade checks current system human access before and after the policy read. The central Authorizer can evaluate a system snapshot with the actual Account already validated inside that transaction, avoiding an unrelated account read. Saved policy remains separate from the effective host-lifetime policy; native capture keeps its incumbent startup and artifact fences. [HTTP](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#system-browser-privacy-http), [state](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#system-browser-privacy-state). [Current common-boundary reconciliation](reports/tenant-approval-boundary-reconciliation-2026-10-24.md).

## Tenant Browser persistent-profile reset

`ITenantBrowserProfileControl` observes and resets the existing persistent Browser profile for a sealed `TenantBrowserAgentOwner` (tenant, actual Instance and executor). Read inspects only existing handles and bounded directory metadata; it does not bind, launch or read page/profile contents. Its private provider epoch and opaque witness cover current context, persistent command/reset revisions and directory identity. Native reset takes the incumbent owner/profile gates, compares that witness, then invokes the trusted begin callback before physical work. Confirmed context closure, an exclusive existing profile lock and a fresh authority callback precede deletion. Only verified directory absence means `reset`; uncertain closure, deletion or authority yields `unknown`. Ephemeral Sessions and other Agents remain independent.

`ITenantBrowserProfileStore` starts and settles reset facts on the existing Agent Instance. Start atomically checks the original authenticated human, actual ownership, active Instance, exact security/Instance revisions and independent raw `agents.read`, `browser.read`, nondelegatable `browser.manage`; it saves `pending`, increments both revisions and appends the start audit. Settlement matches the exact pending operation and records only `reset`/`unknown` with factual audit and new revisions; it cannot authorize physical work. No Run, consent request, exceptional grant, reset table or additional worker is created.

`TenantBrowserProfileService` owns human preflight/command projection and recovery. Pending/unknown facts fence persistent model target preparation and dispatch under current Instance authority. GET can settle an abandoned pending operation from the same native cached result, or record unknown after host loss; it never re-dispatches. Busy work stays pending. Repeating the current operation reads its saved facts; a new deliberate operation needs fresh witnesses. Prior used operation IDs cannot become a later reset. The public DTO omits provider epoch/executor/physical paths. [Protocol](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#tenant-browser-profile-reset-http), [persistence](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#tenant-browser-profile-reset-state).

## Tenant Browser capture and download publication

The existing exact `run_browser_command` branch now admits `browser.screenshot` and optional `command.downloadArtifacts=true` for state-changing Browser commands. Captures use sensitive `browser.use`, independent `browser.read`/`sessions.read` and raw `artifacts.create` on the actual Session. Downloads are published only when explicitly requested; the default Browser command creates no files. Secondary creation authority cannot be supplied by a Browser or Credential-use grant. Reviewer qualification and source-filtered inbox totals include the creation scope.

Optional `PreparedBrowserCommand.Artifacts` binds capture/download choices, host byte/task limits and the capture privacy mode/fingerprint into the exact action hash. Absolute limits are 1,500,000 bytes per capture, four captures per task, 5 MiB per accepted download and two published downloads per task; narrower host limits apply. Task means the retained root activation across continuations. Existing receipts supply durable usage; existing in-flight starts reserve slots. Exhausted budgets refuse before saving new consent, repeat at dispatch and bound the actual publication batch. No separate allowance ledger is introduced.

`ITenantApprovedOperationStore.TrySettleBrowserEffectAsync` accepts an optional process-local `TenantBrowserArtifactBatch`. Both stores publish its immutable owned Artifact metadata with the existing factual Browser receipt, Run/checkpoint, security and audit transaction. SQLite stages private blobs after whole-batch quota/collision checks; failed metadata publication exposes none of the staged members. An unreachable staging file may remain for the incumbent deleted-Session cleanup owner. Native SDK work is never repeated to recover publication. The nullable receipt `BrowserArtifactsJson` contains only a closed status/reason and `kind`/Artifact-ID references; native completion and file publication are separate facts. Empty downloads, published files, partial limits and unavailable publication remain distinguishable. Creation grants no file read.

The Runtime reloads receipt-proven capture images through `ITenantArtifactStore` before every model generation, only under current independent Session/Artifact read and Allowed Artifact-read policy. It verifies actual same-Session creator Run, MIME, size and hash. Policy-required image reads are withheld with `artifact_read_review_required`; creation Review is not a body-read grant. `captureImageProvided` and its unavailable reason describe actual pixels supplied. A final provider-boundary fence rechecks private image hashes, current permissions and claim, and keeps other input-source checks within one unchanged security revision. Provider role projection cannot bypass that check. Image bytes never enter tool JSON/checkpoints, receipts or audit. Independently authorized Browser capabilities may propose file references through the incumbent recipient-filtered Artifact delivery owner. [Qualification](reports/tenant-browser-artifacts-verification-2026-10-20.md).


## Tenant Browser technical retirement

`ITenantBrowserRetirement` enumerates bounded pages of already sealed tenant native owners with existing handles or pending closure, and retires only one exact owner under a Core lifecycle callback. It never binds, launches, inspects page bodies, resets profiles or supplies user-command authority. `TenantBrowserRetirementService` derives retirement from actual tenant/Agent/executor availability and Session archive, non-Active lifecycle, deletion or Ending/Ended state. It validates every returned owner identity and repeats that current check inside native gates. No human is fabricated for technical cleanup.

Successful Session mutations and Agent lifecycle changes reconcile their affected native owners after the canonical metadata commit. The existing supervised resource cleanup sweep retries one bounded page of 100 owners, retaining its cursor until wrap. A five-second TimeProvider budget bounds each sweep/reconciliation; timed-out owners remain pending and advance the sweep cursor so they cannot starve later owners or the other resource cleanup families. Active unrelated Sessions/Agents/tenants and all unscoped handles remain outside the retirement. An ordinary restored lifecycle cancels unstarted retirement; an already initiated uncertain close may finish only its original physical context before reuse. Existing state observation reports partial/`cleanup_pending` with a cleanup count while closure is unresolved.

The native owner/Session/shared context gates prevent forced closure of in-flight work. Shared persistent views close only their owned pages while another view remains; the final view closes the shared context using actual SDK close evidence. Busy or unconfirmed work remains retryable. Pending closure blocks reuse of that old context. Native close events clear matching pending state; stale command witnesses cannot dispatch after retirement. Profile bytes and sealed owner/isolation selections are retained; Artifact/Attachment/workspace byte deletion remains with its independent canonical lifecycle owner. No new reset endpoint, persisted permission, cleanup table or effect ledger is introduced. [Qualification](reports/tenant-browser-retirement-verification-2026-10-20.md).


## Tenant Browser Artifact drop contract
