The existing `run_browser_command` branch admits `browser.drop` with one canonical same-Session `artifactId` and the exact existing target. Artifact input is exclusive with literal `text` or caller `mimeType`; the immutable source supplies its safe name, MIME, byte size and SHA-256. Text drops retain their existing separate literal branch. No inline binary, filesystem path, foreign Session source or caller authority is admitted.

Artifact drops reuse `TenantBrowserUploadRules` / `TenantBrowserUploadTarget`, `ArtifactSourcesJson`, the source-aware inbox filtering and the existing exact Browser-use Review. Independent current Session/Browser reads, nondelegatable Browser use and exact Artifact read govern preparation, decision, dispatch and retained observation delivery. Every source policy and reviewer approval scope remains independent. The stored `FileInputOrigin` member now also represents the exact file-drop destination frame origin; the physical command witness and action hash bind it. The single source uses the existing 50 MiB combined file-transfer ceiling.

The nonserialized `TenantUploadResolver` callback resolves current hash-verified bytes under the existing native gates. Native code repeats live target/frame/page generation and current dispatch authority after source I/O, then constructs one in-memory `File`/`DataTransfer` and dispatches one drop event. Completion proves the SDK drop invocation, not server acceptance or a business outcome. No bytes or resolver enter approval JSON, receipts, checkpoints or audit; no new dispatch or file-read API exists. Existing factual receipts and recovery cannot replay a drop. [Qualification](reports/tenant-browser-drop-verification-2026-10-20.md).


## Tenant Browser Artifact upload contract

The existing `run_browser_command` authorization branch admits `browser.upload` from the immutable Definition capability. Its closed native arguments name one to eight distinct canonical same-Session Artifact IDs and a file-input target. Core independently requires current `sessions.read`, `browser.read`, nondelegatable `browser.use` and `artifacts.read` for every exact source. Browser consent cannot supply missing file read authority. Reviewer qualification composes every applicable source policy and approval scope; list/count filtering checks every saved source before pagination.

`PreparedBrowserCommand` may retain canonical `ArtifactSourcesJson` (ordered Artifact IDs, safe names, MIME types, sizes and SHA-256 hashes) and `FileInputOrigin`. The existing exact action hash includes these facts. Both stores repeat actual ownership/metadata checks in preparation, decision, start and eligible observation settlement. Null properties are omitted for previous command families. Consent shows the exact files and input origin through the incumbent shared Browser parameter summary; setting an input does not confirm server submission.

`BrowserRequest.TenantUploadResolver` is a nonserialized Core callback under the incumbent native gates. It resolves only current canonical tenant Artifact bytes, verifies source hashes and repeats claim/consent/IAM checks after reads. Native execution compares the physical command witness, validates the exact input/frame origin and page generation, then rechecks current authority and live destination immediately before `SetInputFilesAsync`. Source byte payloads never enter saved consent, checkpoints, receipts or audit. Retained Browser observations independently revalidate each original upload's source reads; existing factual recovery never replays an upload. The combined source ceiling is 50 MiB. Capture/download publication now uses the contract above. Artifact-backed drops use the adjacent contract. Browser lifecycle/autonomous adapters remain separate work.


## Tenant model Office inspection

An XLSX explicit range without `sheet` selects the first worksheet; sparse mode without `sheet` traverses workbook order. The saved read scope distinguishes those meanings.

The existing `office.document.inspect` and `office.spreadsheet.inspect` tools use the canonical tenant workspace-read owner for exact absolute home/working files. They require admitted/current Office capability, current independent Session/workspace reads, home-parent read and HomeAccess, original requester policy and a live claimed Run. DOCX binds offset (default 0) and limit (default 20, maximum 100); XLSX binds optional exact sheet and either explicit range or opaque sparse cursor, never both. Cursors convey no authority. No implicit source copy, creation, edit or publication is authorized.

The source byte allocation is bounded by the existing 10 MiB Office package ceiling. Core hashes the actual full source privately, then invokes the incumbent guarded `IOfficeProcessor` and budgeted `OfficeReadResults`. Bounded untrusted semantic output retains its exact tool/argument witness. Processing does not calculate formulas, follow external relationships or establish full-workbook analysis. Current owner, claim, source and IAM fences protect return and checkpoint reuse.

Policy-required inspection uses existing `inspect_workspace` saved-read consent, without a new approval kind/table or exceptional read grant. The exact tool, path and every pagination/sheet/range/cursor parameter bind the operation; current bodies and source hash remain private. Review describes those supplied parameters through the shared inspector, separately from execution. A changed saved source requires cancellation and fresh review. SQLite shape constraints must admit only these supported Office reads, preserve earlier consent families and refuse downgrade while Office consent exists. [Focused model, schema and browser evidence](reports/tenant-office-inspection-verification-2026-10-19.md) records the implemented conversational scope separately from remaining Office writes/publication and full proposal acceptance.

## Approved workspace mutation execution

`ITenantWorkspaceOperationStore` extends the same Run/receipt owner with typed approved start, factual settlement and recoverable-fence queries. Atomic start binds the exact saved request/decision/current grant, actual claimed Run, current Instance/persona/capability/HomeAccess, independent raw prerequisites and deciding-human qualification, then claims the one-time grant and records the existing Run external-effect origin plus audit. Source observation occurs outside the transaction and repeats before native dispatch; write/patch repeat it at commit. Structural effects bind the complete initial footprint, then repeat current owner/authority/claim/grant callbacks without invalidating their own ordered prefix. They retain ordinary concurrent filesystem semantics and possible partial effects, never a filesystem CAS or atomic batch.

The incumbent canonical adapter alone performs I/O. Settlement atomically records a body-free immutable request-bound receipt, Run disposition, consumed/revoked/indeterminate grant and audit. Abandoned or ambiguous dispatch recovers uncertain through retained ownership even after live access loss or parent deletion, without reopening or replaying files. These ports add no file ledger or schema owner. [Focused execution and trusted continuation evidence](reports/tenant-workspace-approved-effects-verification-2026-10-19.md) covers the internal boundary. [Model/shared inspector qualification](reports/tenant-workspace-mutation-review-verification-2026-10-19.md) adds actual offered authorization, HTTP decisions, guarded successor effects, SQLite pending/completed/unknown host recreation and browser approval/cancellation. Full proposal acceptance remains separate.

## Exact workspace mutation consent contract

`PreparedWorkspaceMutation` binds actual Session, offered mutation tool, closed canonical arguments, private source observation, derived primary home/working scope and required workspace permission targets. `PrepareWorkspaceMutation` rebuilds every derived field before accepting its exact action hash. Semantic root correlation includes the same tool/Session/arguments while excluding mutable source and Instance/persona witnesses, so changed files cannot turn a refused action into new authority. The saved witness remains private; display evidence includes only proposed arguments and the actual Agent name.

Compound authorization retains independent parent/source reads and secondary mutation permissions. Cross-root copy's primary permission is destination write; a delete-containing same-root batch uses delete as primary and still needs its own writes/reads. Review requires the Agent's current primary permission; an exceptional grant can replace only that exact primary permission. Deciding humans need every underlying permission, approval authority at each policy-required target, independent-review eligibility, and grant-exception authority at the primary target when applicable. The existing request/decision/signal/grant/Run owners remain authoritative. The trusted internal store/decision boundary has [focused local qualification](reports/tenant-workspace-mutation-consent-verification-2026-10-19.md). Approved execution uses the owner above. Human-attributed `UserMessage` and `ApprovalContinuation` contexts offer `mutate_workspace` only for admitted mutation tools, without requiring Settings or unrelated workspace reads. Its closed `mutation:{tool,arguments}` branches reuse native tool schemas; callers cannot supply identity, permission, grant or source witnesses. `TenantToolAuthorization` prepares the saved exact action through the incumbent owner. Under Allowed policy the model uses the direct mutation tool; an authorization request alone does not execute it. Organization and original Chat share the complete supplied-argument inspector.

## Tenant workspace mutation source observations

Exact mutation preparation uses a private `ITenantWorkspaceMutationObservationStore` facet of the existing canonical filesystem adapter. Core derives exact probes from closed tool arguments: resolved file observations for write/patch, entry observations for mkdir/destinations/nonrecursive deletion, and unlinked recursive observations for copy/move sources and recursive deletion. Link identity is hashed without following structural links; write/patch bind their actual resolved logical route. Whole file bytes, metadata, directory coverage and absent destinations are private witnesses, never read results, file identities, CAS, approval or granted authority. Missing roots/parents are observed without creation; ordinary staging can create missing parents without changing an absent destination witness.

Observation is bounded to 32 probes, 4096 nodes, depth 64, at most the current native per-file ceiling (maximum 50 MiB) and 250 MiB aggregate bytes; tree truncation is refused. Existing hard-link ownership checks remain independently bounded by the native filesystem policy. Only digest/counts cross the adapter boundary; no body, filename listing or raw link target is returned. The Application target derives actual Run/Session owners and primary mutation scope, retains independent parent/source reads and secondary mutation permissions, checks admitted/current capability/HomeAccess and lifecycle, and repeats current ownership/security/Run fences after I/O. A primary delegatable permission may be missing during preparation only; that supplies no dispatch authority. Approved mutation dispatch uses this private observation at the gates described above; the observation never supplies authority itself.

## Tenant workspace direct mutation effects

`TenantWorkspaceMutationEffectService` binds exact closed mutation arguments to the actual claimed conversational Run/Session. `ITenantWorkspaceOperationStore` uses the existing Run external-effect origin and immutable operation receipt owner: atomic start/security/audit, guarded filesystem dispatch, then factual receipt/Run/security/audit settlement. `AgentRunDirectOperation` retains Core operation/call identity, canonical tool arguments/hash and current Instance/persona witnesses; it supplies no permission. A current read/write/delete and parent-scope decision, admitted/current capability/HomeAccess, requester, actual owner/lifecycle and live claim check repeats at the native commit gates.

The direct tool returns a body-free receipt acknowledgement, never source bytes or recipient download authority. If factual receipt settlement is temporarily unavailable, the retained dispatch remains authoritative and the result is explicitly uncertain with the Core operation ID; no receipt is fabricated and no effect is replayed. Its exact Run/call/parameter identity prevents duplicate dispatch or changed-argument reuse. Abandoned dispatch recovery settles uncertainty without replaying I/O, including after the live executor/requester loses authority; factual settlement does not authorize an effect. Sensitive/requester-policy-required mutations return no dispatch. Exact mutation Review/exceptional grants remain required separately; current direct support does not supply that authority.

## Tenant canonical workspace mutation primitives

`ITenantWorkspaceMutationStore` extends the incumbent canonical filesystem adapter with bounded UTF-8 patching, ordered mkdir/copy/move/delete batches and actual owned home/working tree copy. Paths and all batch syntax are validated before mutation. A repeatable trusted callback checks current source-read/destination-write/delete authority; actual tenant/Instance/Session ownership and destination lifecycle are independently rechecked by the adapter. Cross-copy never crosses Tenant, Instance or two distinct working Sessions. No read initializes a root.

Patch freezes edits, requires unique current strict-UTF-8 matches (including overlapping ambiguity), checks byte growth before materializing replacement text and compares source bytes again before protected rename. This remains ordinary concurrent filesystem behavior, not a file version or CAS. Structural authorization repeats before each ordered operation; refusal stops before that operation and reports the known completed prefix. Copy is one coarse bounded tree effect, retains no-overwrite semantics and can leave partial effects on failure/cancellation. No automatic replay, rollback, file ledger, new provider or database/filesystem transaction is introduced.

These internal filesystem primitives supply no authorization on their own. The direct effect/receipt integration is specified above; exact mutation Review and exceptional grants remain a separate required boundary.

## Tenant workspace read Review

The same read boundary includes `workspace.search` over an exact absolute home/working path, literal 1–200 character query, optional bounded relative glob and 1–50 result limit. It retains filename and strict UTF-8 content matching with the existing 200-file, 4096-entry, depth-8 and 256-KiB content-scan limits. A private hash covers observed pages, scanned bytes (including nonmatches), skipped-file metadata, unavailable observations and coverage bounds. Search consent binds canonical query/glob/limit and that traversal, not future discovery. Preview includes only requested scope/query/bounds, never matching names or snippets. Direct and approved results retain current compound authority; changed saved traversal requires fresh review.

`PreparedWorkspaceRead` binds one actual Session, exact supported workspace-read or Office-inspection arguments, the derived home/working scope and a private observation fingerprint. `TenantWorkspaceRuntimeReadService` prepares only under current raw executor parent/workspace reads, admitted/current capability, HomeAccess, lifecycle and claimed Run fences. A file fingerprint covers its current metadata and whole byte content; a directory fingerprint covers the exact bounded page, continuation and truncation observation. No body or directory entries enter consent preview. These are approval witnesses, not filesystem CAS, stable file identities or mutation authority.

The shared `inspect_workspace` intent accepts only an offered read tool and its closed exact arguments. The existing approval owner derives Review and all identities. Parent operation policy can require Review independently of the primary workspace policy. Missing raw reads cannot become an exceptional grant. Source/Instance changes invalidate decision or delivery; semantic root suppression binds Session/tool/arguments while excluding mutable witnesses, so a refused read cannot be retried in the same root after changing file bytes or relaxing policy. An independent new root may request a fresh review.

Human detail/inbox visibility requires independent current Session, workspace and home-parent reads before counts/paging. `TenantWorkspaceReadApprovalVisibility` carries the trusted current allowed workspace resource keys, home-parent Instance IDs and Session IDs; it is a read filter, never authority supplied by the model. A deciding human additionally needs the relevant scoped approval authority, underlying compound reads and current independent-review eligibility. Both stores enforce typed scope/owner/capability/permission invariants at creation, decision and consent settlement. The existing local-effect transaction commits only read consent with Run/security/audit and its immutable receipt; it creates no file effect or grant. The successor resolves only the unchanged saved observation under current executor/reviewer/claim/source fences, then the existing checkpoint owner records bounded untrusted data with `applied=false`, separate read authorization and unavailable-result facts. A retry consumes that receipt/checkpoint without repeating a decision; later direct reads still need current policy. Retained approved data revalidates its exact saved source and current compound authority before model or continuation reuse.

## Tenant model workspace observations

`TenantWorkspaceRuntimeReadService` converts the existing `workspace.read` and `workspace.list` tools for trusted claimed conversational Runs. A closed bounded schema accepts exact absolute `/home` or `/working` paths and, for listing, an optional same-root cursor and limit. Tenant, Instance, Session and executor identities come only from persisted Run/Session ownership. No legacy workspace adapter, relative cwd, virtual `/agent`/`attachments` path, write or publication authority is implied. Search and Office inspection use their exact read contracts above.

Reads require the Agent’s independent current `sessions.read` and exact owned `workspaces.read`; home additionally requires parent `agents.read` and effective admitted/current HomeAccess. The requester’s permissions are never borrowed. Admitted and current tool capability, current original requester/Agent/Session lifecycle, claim generation/lease and operation policy are checked before filesystem I/O and again before returning. A policy-required Review uses the exact saved-observation consent path above; a direct read still returns `workspace_read_review_required` without data. No file read initializes a workspace directory.

The canonical read port supplies current bytes or bounded directory observations. UTF-8 text is inert/untrusted and fitted to the remaining output budget; binary/invalid text receives a supported-reader error. Lists fit whole entries and continue from the last emitted path. Metadata/source checks bracket content reads; mutable file observations are not snapshots, stable file identities or CAS tokens. Core-owned checkpoint markers retain the tool/path read scope; current compound permission, policy, capability, HomeAccess and execution fences repeat before retained output is reused by the model or an approval continuation. No file ledger or grant engine is added.


## Human tenant workspace mutation boundary

`TenantWorkspaceMutationService.InspectAsync` projects actual parent-owned workspace access hints without filesystem reads. Independent read/write/delete and lifecycle hints are fenced against current private owner/security observations before projection; they never authorize later effects. Home can project Instance name/revision under Agent read. Working projects neither live Instance field under Session read; both are null.

`TenantWorkspaceMutationService` authorizes the original human, actual home/working owner, independent parent read and `workspaces.write` or `workspaces.delete`. A current tenant-security revision guards intake; current Instance and Session lifecycle are rechecked before effect. Conversation snapshot revisions remain independent from mutable file operations. Write input is copied and bounded; acknowledgements carry no stored body or metadata and grant no read permission.

`ITenantWorkspaceMutationStore` reuses the canonical file adapter and accepts a protected commit callback. Replacement stages bytes, then the callback repeats original-human/owner/IAM/configuration checks immediately before atomic rename. Single-node deletion repeats the callback immediately before nonrecursive unlink. The existing `ITenantAuthorizationAuditStore` validates actual current human login, owning graph, observed revisions and central IAM again, then appends only authorization evidence. Denied known-owner IAM/lifecycle attempts use the same owner. Audit contains the workspace address and actor, not file path/content, and never claims that a filesystem effect completed. No new table, mutation receipt engine or database/filesystem transaction is introduced.

After a known completed primitive, the service returns without projecting private metadata or turning a factual acknowledgement into a failed replayable request. Unexpected filesystem I/O becomes `workspace_effect_unknown`; it is not evidence of no mutation. Staging directories/temporary files and concurrent last-rename behavior retain the incumbent filesystem semantics. Agent mutations and exact operation grants remain separate boundaries.

## Tenant workspace read boundary

`ITenantWorkspaceReadStore` extends the existing canonical file adapter with actual `TenantWorkspaceReference` owner validation for list, bounded search and bounded inspection. Home resolves the current owned Instance; working additionally resolves the current non-deleted Session and matching executor/Instance. Reads never create a workspace or run templates. The descriptor-relative canonical filesystem retains logical path, link, hard-link, size and traversal rules; metadata/bytes are current observations rather than immutable file versions.

`TenantWorkspaceReadService` requires the original authenticated human's current membership and independent parent/workspace reads, then repeats login, security and owning revisions after I/O. Content projection first bounds metadata, reads the allowed bytes, checks observed metadata consistency, processes through the existing caller-supplied projector and checks source/authority again. The same boundary supplies original downloads and Office preview. It gives no Agent tool, publication, write or deletion permission. [Wire contract](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#tenant-workspace-read-contract), [UX contract](19-multi-tenant-iam-ux.md#tenant-workspace-reading).

## Tenant Artifact cleanup worker port

`ITenantArtifactCleanupStore.ListDeletedArtifactOwnersAsync(after, limit)` returns at most 100 canonical retained tenant/Session deletion witnesses, ordered by Session identity with an exclusive cursor. It includes owners with no published rows so an interrupted private staging write is still recoverable. It is a technical worker port, never a human/model inventory API.

`CleanupDeletedArtifactsAsync(target)` repeats actual immutable owner, exact deletion timestamp and quiescent-Run checks. A live, archived, merely terminal, foreign or mismatched owner returns false without removing bytes. A missing derived byte namespace converges; an unsafe substituted root fails without following it. Metadata/receipts remain unchanged. `TenantArtifactCleanupService` serializes bounded scans and isolates failures per target; the next scan wrap or new service instance retries retained tombstones. The existing five-minute supervised cleanup host invokes it independently of legacy workspace cleanup failures. Canonical Session deletion also retires its exact Artifact grants with Session/Run grants under the existing security/audit transaction; both stores reject a direct deletion transaction that retains those grants.

## Tenant Artifact ports

`ITenantArtifactStore` is implemented by the existing InMemory/SQLite Artifact adapters over their existing records/blobs. `CreateTenantAsync` accepts a `TenantRuntimeFrame`, stable Artifact ID, bounded safe display name/content type and immutable copied bytes. It validates the actual Session/Run, live claim generation/lease and per-item/Session quotas. A same-ID identical retry returns the original record; changed metadata, bytes or source conflict. This is a storage prerequisite only: protected publishers must separately use current IAM, exact Review/grant where required and the existing effect receipt owner. The storage port itself grants no model authority; human HTTP creation is absent. The protected inline model adapter below uses the existing local effect owner.

`ArtifactRecord` adds nullable `TenantId` (omitted when null) to the existing producing `AgentRunId`. Owned records require an actual producing Run and no unconverted Attachment/Workspace provenance. `FindTenantAsync`, `ListTenantAsync` and `ReadTenantContentAsync` resolve actual ownership; allowed Artifact IDs and optional Run filtering precede cursor validation/limit+1 paging. No caller-supplied blob path is accepted. `TenantArtifactService` owns original-human/current membership and independent Session/Artifact/optional Run read checks, plus post-I/O identity/security/Session fences. `ReadContentAsync` yields a bounded checksum-verified buffer only after those fences; the API maps safe metadata and forces attachment delivery. These ports do not grant model read/delivery authority. [HTTP](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#tenant-artifact-http-reads), [persistence](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#tenant-artifact-storage).

`TenantArtifactOperationService` converts bounded inline UTF-8 `artifacts.create` for an original human-message Run. `artifacts.create` accepts the actual parent Session as a creation scope in addition to its Artifact resource family; this grants no `sessions.read` or `artifacts.read`. Exact canonical arguments preserve content/line endings, normalize safe metadata and bind a tool-specific hash. Limits are 32 KiB UTF-8 content and 64 KiB JSON arguments. Caller-selected Artifact/Session/Run identities and duplicate/unknown fields are rejected. Pinned/current capability, live claim, current source/account/Agent/requester state and current IAM/creation policy repeat at the store boundary. Required Review or exceptional authority returns no direct publication. `TenantApprovalService.PrepareArtifactAsync` prepares a typed exact `PreparedArtifactPublication` for the existing authorization tool and durable approval owner.

`ITenantDirectOperationStore.FindDirectOperationReceiptAsync` is the common receipt lookup used by preferences and Artifact publication; its owner is the existing immutable operation receipt table. `ITenantArtifactOperationStore.TryCommitArtifactAsync` commits Artifact metadata, succeeded Run effect marker, exact Run/call receipt, security revision and audit atomically. Identical Run/call arguments return the saved receipt; changed arguments conflict. No Instance configuration revision or read grant changes. The Runtime lets this transaction own its local effect marker instead of imposing a second external in-flight fence. Model results contain only `decision`, `reasonCode`, `applied`, `operationId`; rich references, Artifact bytes/metadata and model read recovery require their own delivery conversion. [Evidence](reports/tenant-artifact-publication-verification-2026-10-18.md).

## Tenant protected Browser Credentials

Protected filling extends the existing reviewed Browser command owner, never ordinary direct mutation. Its prepared command binds a General Password Credential, current admitted binding, Credential/secret revisions, exact current target-frame origin and immutable safe display name. Primary `credentials.use` targets that tenant Credential through the existing Credential FK; independent `credentials.read`, Browser read/use and Session read remain mandatory. Core selects Review when the executor has raw Credential use, otherwise exact ExceptionalGrant under the unchanged delegatable catalog. Only primary Credential use may be exceptional; every independent scope remains raw. A Review never upgrades itself after authority loss. Human reviewers and historical approval inspection require the same resource scopes. Root correlation identifies the actual Credential while the exact action hash includes every mutable witness. The existing grant is claimed atomically with the Browser fence, then consumed on Completed, revoked on known Denied or sealed Indeterminate on an unknown attempt. Current grantor underlying/approve/exception authority and expiry are required before secret use. Only a matching Consumed grant plus Completed receipt permits historical result delivery; it permits no new dispatch. [Grant verification](reports/tenant-browser-credential-grant-verification-2026-10-18.md).

The only eligible source is the trusted human conversation turn or its same-conversation durable approval descendants, with the original accepted input lineage. Durable exact human consent can outlive a disconnected web client; no attached cookie is fabricated. Autonomous/background origins cannot resolve a password. This tenant path replaces the old attached-web-client condition with exact durable human consent; the remaining unscoped sink keeps its incumbent direct attached-turn restrictions.

The incumbent native typed request has a nonserialized Core password resolver, valid only for an exact tenant-owned `BrowserFillCredential`. Its physical witness also captures the target frame's current origin from cached handles without reading page content. Missing resolver, foreign owner, wrong command, changed origin or reused target cannot reach protected resolution. The shared native sink validates the unique live existing-password field, intervention state and allowed frame before resolving. Core rechecks current consent/claim/binding/scopes, unprotects only for that exact origin, and the native sink registers redaction and repeats live target/frame checks plus current Core authority immediately before filling. No secret or callback enters tool arguments, checkpoints, receipts, review evidence, logs or model output. Filling is not submission or verified sign-in.

`approvals.request_authorization` now includes this converted command. `credentials.list` uses the scoped read owner below; neither capability offering nor an alias grants use. [Focused evidence](reports/tenant-browser-credential-verification-2026-10-18.md) separates actual model/native workflows from controlled boundary staging. Other resource/native operations and independent Automation Browser admission use their dedicated typed owners; this dated credential report qualifies only its own workflow.

`TenantCredentialRuntimeReadService` owns `credentials.list` for a live actual tenant Runtime frame. The existing cursor/limit and complete-record output fitting remain unchanged. Ordinary mailbox checkpoint delivery is retained, with no effect fence, provider I/O or secret resolution. `PreparedCredentialRead` fixes the actual Session, normalized discovery cursor/limit and at most limit+1 exact admitted/current readable binding/Credential revisions, aliases and safe labels. ## Tenant Browser sealed result admission

After the same receipt transaction seals a completed exact tool checkpoint, `AgentRun.AwaitApprovedExternalResultDelivery` requires its live uncancelled claim, original external-effect origin, exact approval-resume request/operation/hash and factual terminal disposition. It reuses `AgentRunCheckpoint.AwaitingRuntimeAdmission`, pauses the remaining budget and adds no permission, grant or provider witness. Only this exact state may retain the marker through claim recovery and capacity deferral, including a sealed Indeterminate outcome. Attempt count, consumed steps/output and active native time cannot reset.

`TenantRunContinuationService.ResumeAsync` consumes the saved receipt/checkpoint under a fresh live claim and current delivery checks, then checkpoints admission to restart the remaining clock. Saved observations are still checked against current scope before each generation. The native command cannot replay; a sealed unknown result permits an honest explanation with tools disabled and terminal uncertain failure. No sealed checkpoint means no special delivery recovery. Cancellation wins over admission recovery. ## Tenant Browser direct reads under current policy

`TenantBrowserReadService` owns direct converted read execution for the current claimed tenant Run. The closed `TenantBrowserInspectionContract` shares typed read classification with Review preparation and the native provider. Observation/find/wait, bounded console/network/rule inspection, verification/locator/configuration reads, Tabs list and Dialog inspect are eligible only beneath admitted/current Definition capabilities. Tabs and Dialog expose read-only schemas. The existing normalizer accepts blank payloads only for offered zero-property object schemas; explicit invalid values stay invalid. Mutation, ownership and unconverted resource arguments fail before provider access. Navigation, closure, storage mutations/reads classified as `browser.use`, protected resources and other actions still require their exact Review/resource adapters.

Actual Session/Instance/executor and current original-requester access, raw `sessions.read` plus exact agent `browser.read`, and current policy are required. Either compound read policy can require Review. That refusal precedes even cold binding/target observation and names `browser_read_review_required`; the model can request the same command through `approvals.request_authorization`. An approval never authorizes a later direct read. Reads require no `browser.use`, grant or durable effect receipt.

After safe metadata-only registration and physical target preparation, Core repeats source/claim/permission/policy under the incumbent native gate. Internal nonserialized `BrowserRequest.TenantReadOnly` independently rejects a typed mutation before witness consumption. Existing handles only are read: no context provisioning, navigation, view closure or isolation transition. Execution is bounded by admitted per-tool/overall and remaining claim deadlines. Post-I/O fences recheck scope, source revisions and cancellation; a late/noncooperative observation is discarded.

Results retain the native semantic/configuration shape with bounded untrusted content, `tenantBrowserRead`, exact typed `readCommandHash`, `readAuthorized` and false effect/application-success flags. Existing byte/checkpoint limits remain decisive. The ordinary mailbox checkpoint owns delivery; reads do not start or clear an external-effect fence. Observation/discovery compaction preserves read provenance while keeping its incumbent bounded excerpts. Before each later model generation, all retained direct read data, including compacted excerpts, is checked against current source and Allowed policy without native I/O. Revocation or new Review requirements withhold the historical data. [Focused evidence](reports/tenant-browser-read-verification-2026-10-18.md).

## Tenant Browser model authorization and result continuation

Tenant Definitions with admitted Browser capabilities now offer the existing `approvals.request_authorization` tool with `operation=run_browser_command` and `command={tool,arguments}`. Its nested native command schemas include only Definition-authorized converted tools. Core supplies tenant/Run/Session/Instance, permissions, physical target and witnesses; model ownership/revision/grant fields are rejected. Protected General Password filling uses the compound Credential owner above. Canonical same-Session Artifact upload uses the source boundary above. Capture and explicit download publication use their contract above. Coordinate/geolocation commands remain unavailable in the tenant adapter. Artifact-backed drops use the dedicated source/consent contract above. Ordinary direct mutation tools remain closed. A current policy-Allowed authorization preparation reports permission only; the direct read owner above performs an offered read under freshly checked policy. Policy-required reads and sensitive actions can use exact durable Review.

Model preparation registers only cold/same-mode trusted owner metadata after current raw permissions and source checks. `ITenantBrowserBinding.EnsureTenantIsolationAsync` cannot allocate, navigate, close or change an existing view's mode. A conflicting selection returns pending-transition recovery. Saved targets can reconstruct this immutable metadata after provider recreation before comparing actual physical identity. A lost warm view then reports `browser_target_changed`, disables decision and remains separately cancellable; metadata cannot resurrect its handles or authorize a replacement. Cold consent still requires an exact current match.

The existing shared-capacity host invokes the Browser effect owner outside the mailbox before dispatching the successor Runtime. Pending call parameters must match the saved command exactly. On factual settlement, both stores can atomically append one bounded `approvals.request_authorization` result to the existing Run checkpoint together with its receipt/security/audit. Current authority is repeated inside the transaction before retaining observation data. Checkpoint capacity fallback retains exact outcome and reports unavailable observation. No separate observation store or migration is added.

The result carries `commandCompleted`, `applicationOutcomeVerified=false`, `applied=null`, `replayAllowed=false`, and either explicitly untrusted bounded Browser observation/data or a named unavailable reason. Bytes/downloads are excluded. The model receives historical data, never a reusable physical witness or new permission. A persisted result is consumed from that checkpoint; retry does not call the provider or append another result. Current source/executor/requester/reviewer and live claim fence every retained Browser observation before Resume and each later model generation, including observations from earlier approval hops. Result request/operation provenance is matched to the same actual tenant/Session/Instance/executor/requester/root; a later request cannot replace an earlier observation’s scope. Revocation preserves facts while withholding delivery. Lost/unknown attempts remain nonreplayable. [Focused evidence](reports/tenant-browser-model-verification-2026-10-18.md).

## Tenant Browser effect owner and store boundary

`TenantBrowserEffectService.ExecuteAsync` accepts trusted actual tenant/Run, request, operation, current claim generation and exact action hash. It resolves the current saved source and semantic target, obtains a fresh physical witness, then uses `ITenantApprovedOperationStore.TryStartBrowserEffectAsync` to atomically commit the existing Run InFlight fence, security revision and dispatch audit. Current raw compound permissions, admitted/current capability ceilings, exact Review/decision and root dedupe are repeated by both stores. Review creates no ExceptionalGrant.

Internal `BrowserRequest.TenantDispatchCheck` is a nonserialized Core callback. `NativePlaywrightBrowser` invokes it inside the incumbent owner/Session gate before physical witness consumption and native work; it does not reenter the provider. The callback rechecks current Run/claim, consent expiry, actual source/isolation and current executor/requester/reviewer authority, including final Run/security/expiry reads. A disabled host returns unavailable before consuming the witness. Execution is bounded by the remaining claim, approval lifetime, admitted per-tool budget and remaining checkpoint budget, including time already spent preparing.

`TrySettleBrowserEffectAsync` atomically seals an immutable generic operation receipt and the matching live Run fence with security/audit. `RecoverableBrowserEffectsAsync` selects abandoned original fences, including retained deleted-Session ownership; `RecoverAsync` records unknown outcomes without provider I/O. Cancellation seals uncertainty independently before returning; late provider completion cannot upgrade the saved outcome. Retries return receipt-only with `browser_observation_not_retained`. Fresh successful transient output requires current post-I/O authority and the same uncancelled live claim, with final Run/security checks after access reads, and excludes Bytes/Downloads. SDK confirmation is not proof of application success.

These are internal ports, with no public dispatch/preparation endpoint. The ordinary local effect service still rejects a first Browser dispatch; the model continuation above uses the external effect owner and consumes the saved receipt/checkpoint. [Verification](reports/tenant-browser-effect-verification-2026-10-18.md).

## Tenant Browser Review preparation and decisions

`TenantApprovalService.PrepareBrowserAsync` is an internal trusted-Run operation. It requires the current Running claim/generation/revision, actual canonical Session owner, raw executor `sessions.read` plus exact agent `browser.read`, the derived primary permission and both admitted/current Definition capabilities. `TenantBrowserOperationTarget` reads an already bound provider target; preparation never enrolls an owner, changes isolation, closes a view or allocates a context. Current/pinned isolation and the host mode must match. Navigation/new-tab destinations remain beneath the existing host policy.

`PreparedBrowserCommand` stores bounded normalized arguments, exact typed command hash and canonical semantic target separately. Native preparation supplies an origin-only display value from the same cached URL used for its location hash; it omits userinfo, path and query. The saved target binds mode, native context/tab/generation/dialog identity, location hash and shared command revision. It excludes the transient provider epoch and normalizes a context-free command revision to zero. A changed warm target safely blocks decision after release/restart; the request remains durable and readable. The transient execution witness remains a separate single-use physical check.

Tenant permission mapping is closed and typed: observations/find/wait, console/network/rule inspection, verification/locator generation, configuration reads and tab/dialog inspection use `browser.read`; navigation, closure and mutations use sensitive `browser.use`. The older generic tool-effect label for navigation is not tenant permission authority. Credential fill, uploads, captures, coordinate mouse, geolocation and Artifact-backed drops require their unconverted adapters and remain unavailable.

Required oversight creates Review only, never an ExceptionalGrant. Saved action hashes bind exact arguments/target and Instance/persona revisions; semantic root correlation uses Session/tool/typed-command hash without mutable target revisions. Replacing a native target cannot bypass a terminal same-root request. Both stores repeat canonical Session/source/capability, raw executor and qualified-reviewer checks inside their existing approval transaction. Secondary read policies may require additional scoped approver authority and independent review. Inventory filters Browser read/use/Session scopes before totals/paging; direct reads enforce the same scopes.

Preparation and consent alone create no effect receipt, grant or native dispatch. The internal effect owner above now supports exact dispatch/settlement/recovery. There is no public preparation endpoint; the model wrapper above is the trusted Runtime entry. `TenantApprovedOperationService` still rejects a first dispatch of this non-local family with `tenant_effect_unavailable`; the remaining resource adapters stay pending. [Focused evidence](reports/tenant-browser-review-verification-2026-10-18.md) distinguishes native HTTP proof from controlled UI presentation.

## Tenant Browser command target port

Comparison/consumption and native work retain the existing Session/owner gates throughout, using private call forwarding rather than reentrant public execution. Simultaneous use admits at most one native attempt. Every admitted attempt consumes the physical target, including a failed/uncertain response; no automatic retry occurs. Dialog inspect/accept/dismiss remain eligible while their triggering native action is pending.

`BrowserCommandIdentity` freezes mutable argument collections and upload buffers; hashes bind actual bytes without base64-materializing them. Only a protected-fill witness adds the permitted target frame origin; path/query/user information remains excluded. Missing/changed targets yield `browser_target_required`/`browser_target_changed`; busy target preparation yields `browser_target_busy`. These are internal port facts, never user-editable HTTP/model authority. Core still needs current IAM, durable Review and an exact dispatch/effect fence before enabling tenant Browser tools.

This [ownership checkpoint](reports/tenant-sandbox-runtime-ownership-verification-2026-10-18.md) retains its original qualification scope.

## Tenant effective Sandbox verification ports

`TenantSandboxSetupResolver` resolves actual owned Instance revision, its exact owned/shipped Definition policy, one exact tenant profile and saved sparse script/limits without image auth or SDK work. `SandboxImageAuthorization` can additionally pin Instance ID/revision; that mode requires exact profile read plus agent read/Sandbox read/manage, independently of protected Credential read/bind/use. It does not require reusable profile management or agent configuration update. Original human evidence compares the exact Account/session/Principal fields; a different DTO/record shape grants no authority and cannot reject an otherwise matching identity.

`TenantSandboxInstanceVerificationService` provides explicit full verification and exact/latest original-human-fenced result reads. Its view includes current Instance/profile/security revisions, bounded exact attempt/current observation, current setup status and independent `canVerify`. This hint reflects active owned configuration, current scoped authority/source and no active attempt; it neither saves settings nor grants execution and is separate from configuration `canManage`. Host readiness remains separately observed.

`ITenantSandboxVerificationStore.FindSandboxVerificationAsync` defaults to reusable-profile scope; a server-derived Instance ID selects that exact scope, even for an exact attempt ID. `ReadSandboxInstanceVerificationAsync` returns accepted full evidence for exact Instance/profile revisions plus per-Instance pending state. Instance attempts never update `SandboxProfileVerifications`. The existing reservation/completion/cleanup ports carry optional Instance pins and keep factual settlement after cancellation/authority loss. Read evidence must still match source epochs, host and contract; receipt success alone is no current execution permission.

## Tenant Instance Sandbox configuration reads and writes

`TenantAgentConfigurationService.ReadSandboxAsync` returns `TenantAgentSandboxSetup`: Instance ID/revision/lifecycle, optional shared `InstanceSettingsSection`, safe readable profile metadata/current availability, `completeCatalog`, agent-level `canManage` and security revision. The original-human `TenantAgentReadService.ReadSandboxAsync` fences this entire projection. Exact profile lookup uses only `ITenantSandboxProfileStore`; `ISandboxImageAuthority` and `ISandboxReadiness` supply compatibility and server observations without dispatch.

A partial Definition catalog omits inaccessible profile metadata and hides the settings section until all effective allowed choices are readable. After narrowing to a readable set, the section may be inspected with inaccessible inherited defaults and constraint choices redacted; `completeCatalog` remains false. No hidden profile script, source Credential, epoch or verification output is included in this setup projection.

`PatchSettingsAsync` now supports `sandboxPolicy` under current agent read/update and Sandbox read/manage, independently requiring exact profile read. It reuses sparse set/clear, Definition subset rules, bounded script/limit validation and the existing Instance/security/audit transaction. All effective profiles cap overrides, independently of the default. New allowed profiles and any changed/explicit default require current eligibility and Ready server state. Reset to an unavailable default fails; removal/narrowing can repair unavailable configuration. Saving selection never verifies a script, dispatches an SDK command or grants execution authority.

## Tenant Sandbox profile ports

`ITenantSandboxProfileStore` reads exact owned historical/current revisions, filters a bounded metadata page by authorized IDs and search, and saves a prepared revision with `TenantSecurityState`, expected security/profile revisions, original `HumanSessionReference` and audit in one transaction. It operates on the existing Sandbox profile rows with authoritative `TenantSandboxProfileRoot` ownership; it supplies no execution or disposable-verification operation. Global `ISandboxStore` profile/verification operations exclude or reject owned IDs.

`ISandboxImageAuthority.ResolveAsync(profile, SandboxImageAuthorization, ct)` is the tenant source-preparation entry. `SandboxImageAuthorization` carries the server-derived original human, current security witness and optional expected profile revision, independently of user-authored profile fields. `TenantSandboxImageAuthorization` rechecks live human membership, profile catalog read/manage and Credential read/bind/use. The existing Infrastructure adapter resolves bounded tags and retains secret plaintext/SDK authorization internally. Its global overload and unframed protected-auth helper reject tenant profiles. `InspectAsync` cannot make an owned profile executable from a global verification observation.


## Tenant Sandbox verification ports

`ITenantSandboxVerificationStore` reserves and reads `SandboxVerificationAttempt`, settles trusted `SandboxVerificationOutcome` facts, reads exact accepted compatibility evidence and claims/settles technical cleanup. Reservation atomically advances tenant security and audit; fact completion rechecks the original live human, reserved security/profile revision, current Credential epochs and unexpired attempt lease. A settled reusable-profile full success or failure updates the existing observation store; Instance checks use their own exact receipt evidence, and image-only/retired results do not promote compatibility. Latest reads order by the reservation's security revision, independently of clock resolution.

`ITenantSandboxVerifier` receives the exact saved attempt, immutable source/profile and trusted `SandboxImageAuthorization` including expected current profile and optional Instance revisions. Infrastructure reuses the existing SDK verification core with fresh authorization before protected registry access, provisioning, scripts and acceptance, plus a current-authority watcher during provider work. `ISandboxVerificationHost` supplies an opaque fingerprint of the configured canonical/daemon roots, endpoint and filesystem contract. Vendor configuration/authentication and physical paths stay in Infrastructure.

`TenantSandboxVerificationService` exposes original-human-fenced exact/latest reads and explicit full/image-only checks. `TenantSandboxVerificationCleanup` consumes only `SandboxVerificationCleanup` ownership claims; no saved scripts, logs or human session are supplied to that worker. Neither this port nor a successful observation by itself grants Instance configuration authority or `sandbox.execute`.

The verifier checks private `verificationFencing` and `verificationScopeVersion=2` capabilities before tenant mount preparation or create. Separate tenant/attempt labels stay within the pinned server’s 63-character value bound; both witnesses must match returned metadata. After its sequential probes, authenticated `POST /agent-core/verifications/retire` takes only exact tenant/attempt and returns bounded versioned matching retirement proof. This is private Infrastructure transport, not a public operation/authorization port. `CleanupAsync` rechecks exact current claim/revision/expiry/Instance/host before and after that I/O; only confirmed proof permits purging the attempt directory. Unknown, malformed, oversized, foreign or pending proof retains cleanup. No empty SDK listing or unrelated provider absence can promote compatibility. [Evidence](reports/tenant-sandbox-verification-retirement-verification-2026-10-18.md).

## Tenant Instance exact resource and preview reads

`TenantAgentReadService.ReadResourceDetailAsync` returns `TenantAgentResourceDetail`: the safe Instance, one exact readable `TenantAgentResourceItem` and current security revision. It shares the permission-filtered catalog and typed manage/delete/copy hints with the paged resource editor, independently of that editor's filters or page. Missing/unreadable keys deny; dependencies include only independently readable Skills. Archived resources remain inspectable with no mutation hints.

`ProjectResourceContentAsync<T>` composes the existing exact byte reader and a trusted server projection inside the same original-human/Instance/security fence. The caller's byte-limit function receives fresh authorized `InstanceResourceView` metadata before blob allocation; the owning service still verifies actual length/hash and current exact resource read after I/O. The final facade check runs after projection, so logout/replacement login, revocation and parent/security drift during Office processing cannot release the captured result. The projection callback and limit are server code, never request-selected authority. Ordinary downloads retain their existing byte limits and original bytes. No store/schema or provider DTO changes are introduced.

## Tenant Instance child editor reads

`ReadSkillDetailAsync` supplies one exact readable Skill with safe Instance/current security witnesses and the same action hints, independently of list search/paging. It uses the shared filtered catalog under the original-human/final revision fence. Missing or unreadable keys deny without exposing a protected item. An open frontend draft can refresh this exact item without depending on the collection page.

`TenantAgentReadService.ReadSkillEditorAsync` and `ReadResourceEditorAsync` compose the existing child-read owners under the original authenticated human/session and final Instance/security fence. Each returns the actual `TenantAgent`, readable paged items, visible matching total, page/page size, security revision, `CanCreate` and `AllReadable`. Skill items pair the existing `InstanceSkillView` with `CanManage`/`CanCustomize`; resource items pair `InstanceResourceView` with `CanManage`/`CanDelete`/`CanCopy`. The API maps the agent through the existing safe projection.

Shared private catalogs preserve existing child read projections and dependency filtering. Authorization precedes search, count and pagination; case-insensitive metadata search is bounded to 128 UTF-16 units, page to 0–100000 and page size to 1–100 (default 30). Stable name/path and key ordering avoids page changes from incidental backing order. `AllReadable` describes inherited tenant-wide read permission, not hidden counts. No IAM-inspector permission or client-selected principal is needed for these advisory action hints.

Hints reflect current Active lifecycle and the exact typed permission: manage for existing Skills/resources, independent delete for local resources, and tenant-wide typed read plus manage for new-child destinations. Only inherited Definition Skills expose Customize and only inherited resources expose Copy. A local item grant cannot create siblings; content immutability and dependency validation remain in existing commands. Archived items remain inspectable without write hints. These reads never mutate child state, audit or policy and do not authorize a subsequent write; existing original-human atomic commands reauthorize and require displayed witnesses. Browser delivery remains a separately verified frontend obligation.

## Tenant Instance version reads

`TenantAgentReadService.ReadVersionsAsync` and `PreviewVersionAsync` use the existing original-human facade for exact owned Instance reads. `TenantAgentVersionCatalog` supplies bounded, newest-first versions from that Instance's exact owned Definition or immutable shipped source, the safe agent, security revision and advisory `CanAdopt`. Owned sources additionally require exact `agent-definitions.read`; shipped source lookup never falls back to global durable publications or requires unrelated Instance-creation authority.

`TenantAgentVersionPreview` supplies `InstanceVersionPreview`, security/publication witnesses, child visibility flags and advisory `CanAdopt`. Catalog/preview do not authorize a mutation. Preview and adoption share one private compatibility preparation through existing settings/model, Skill/resource and Automation planners. The pure `InstanceVersionPreviewFactory` also serves the existing administrative preview. Protected child keys are filtered before diff construction; no hidden totals or item names are returned. Compatibility errors use a safe generic validation response; non-validation failures keep their status. Original human and final Instance/security checks surround all I/O.

## Tenant Instance execution controls read

`TenantAgentReadService.ReadControlsAsync(tenant, instance, human, ct)` returns `TenantAgentControlsState`: safe agent identity, existing budgets (overrides, Definition defaults, effective values and host ceilings), existing management projection, one security revision and exact `CanUpdate`. Both child security/Instance witnesses must match the parent capture; the common original-human post-I/O fence then validates the whole result. Exact `agents.read` is sufficient to inspect, independently of IAM inspection; `agents.update` is an advisory action hint. Existing mutation owners still authorize and commit writes.

## Original-human agent mutation witness

`TenantAgentConfigurationChange.Initiator` carries an optional trusted `HumanSessionReference` for canonical human commands; `TryCreateTenantAgentAsync` and `TrySetTenantAgentLifecycleAsync` receive the same optional reference. It is not client input, a grant, an authentication credential or persisted authority. Canonical HTTP mutations always supply the original server-authenticated tuple. Existing trusted non-HTTP execution uses its existing actor/IAM/management checks without fabricating a human session. Stores require any supplied human Principal to equal the audit actor, then validate its Account/session/stamp/expiry/membership in the same atomic write boundary. Configuration services also revalidate before store submission and before returning a no-op Skill reset. A later same-Account login cannot substitute for a retired session.

## Canonical agent human read boundary

`TenantAgentReadService` is the single original-human facade over the existing directory/configuration owners for canonical agent GETs. Typed entry points cover inventory, shipped templates, exact metadata, effective configuration, settings, budgets, management, Skills, resource catalog/content and the composed editor. The HTTP caller supplies the authenticated Account/session/Principal; the facade validates that exact tuple before I/O and again after all protected storage/content I/O. Exact Instance reads require current `agents.read` and matching Instance/Principal identity; collections retain their owning service’s filter/create rules. Tenant revision changes reject the whole captured result with `stale_revision`. Child permissions, byte checks and read projections remain in the existing owners; the facade grants no authority and accepts no client-selected actor.

## Tenant Instance editor read

`TenantAgentReadService.ReadEditorAsync(tenant, instance, human, ct)` composes the existing agent/configuration owners into `TenantAgentEditorState`: exact safe agent identity/persona, typed settings sections, one security revision, exact `CanUpdate` hint and unavailable-section keys. It requires the original current human session and exact `agents.read`, verifies matching Instance/security witnesses after storage I/O, and fails stale reads rather than mixing snapshots. `CanUpdate` uses exact `agents.update` independently of IAM inspection. Sandbox settings are omitted until the tenant catalog/effects exist; there is no global owner fallback. Existing persona and settings mutations retain their original authorization and revision checks.

`TenantAgentAccessService.ReadAsync` supplies the human-facing Agent Access projection over existing `IIamStore`/`ITenantAgentStore`. It independently requires owned-agent read and scoped IAM inspection, derives the actual human/session, computes audience/effective execution permissions through `IamAuthorizer`, bounds each filtered page and rechecks security/Instance witnesses after I/O. Principal selection needs exact-agent assignment authority; organization role grant hints need tenant inspection/assignment authority. Read DTOs contain safe names and actual assignment sources, without login/secret metadata or reusable permission snapshots. Existing commands still own writes and independently enforce source scope, human-only rules, last Owner and CAS. [Protocol](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#canonical-agent-access-view), [evidence](reports/tenant-agent-access-ui-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantConversationApprovalService.ListAsync` returns bounded minimal approval status for an authorized human conversation recipient. It checks exact tenant/Session/Instance ownership and current human/session, Session-read and agent-Chat authority before and after I/O. Optional `TenantApprovalQuery.SessionId` filters both stores before count/page; Guid.Empty is invalid. `CanInspect` uses existing protected approval/action reads and remains advisory. The result exposes no prepared operation body or reusable authority. `DurableApprovalPendingOutput(RequestId, RunId)` is normalized Runtime output after persistence of the actual wait, mapped to the minimal tenant event documented in [Protocol](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#canonical-original-chat-approvals). No port or DTO crosses the existing provider/domain boundary. [Verification](reports/tenant-chat-approvals-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantConversationService.ReadViewAsync` supplies the public pinned agent, Session lifecycle/header and model selection only after current human/session, exact Session read and agent Chat checks plus a final security fence. `TenantSessionMetadata` adds public agent ID/version/name/role and an exact current `CanManage` hint; `TenantRunMetadata.CanCancel` uses current Run-read, Session-read and exact Run-cancel scope, never tenant-wide authority as a substitute. Every mutation still checks independently. `ITenantSessionStore.ListTenantSessionsAsync` accepts `includeArchived` (default true for compatibility), filtering before total/page. `TenantChatService.RequireMemberAsync` gates the safe public model catalog. [Evidence](reports/tenant-chat-ui-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantChatService.ListAgentsAsync` takes the trusted tenant/human session and validated page/search inputs. It checks exact `agents.chat` per active tenant executor, applies public name/role search, then calls the existing `ITenantAgentStore.ListTenantAgentsAsync` with eligible IDs so counts and offsets cannot include hidden agents. It resolves only an exact owned or shipped Definition for public language, projects the Instance's public persona and creation revision, and revalidates actual human/security after I/O. `CanCreateSessions` is an advisory current tenant-scope `sessions.create` plus `sessions.read` check, not an action capability; creation repeats its existing current authority/witness checks. Administrative `agents.read` never substitutes for Chat and is not required by this inventory. [Evidence](reports/tenant-chat-discovery-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantDeliveryReceipt` is trusted server-held transport evidence: immutable Session owner, actual human/session, observed security revision, exact entry ID/sequence/response and published/requested text ends. It is never accepted as a wire payload or model argument. `ITenantDeliveryReceiptControl.ReceiveAsync` selects the existing owning mailbox or a quiescent Session reservation. `ITenantDeliveryReceiptStore.SaveTenantRuntimeReceiptAsync` validates the actual Run frame and commits through the ordered writer; `SaveTenantQuiescentReceiptAsync` updates only an existing owned assistant text receipt. Both recheck the current human/session and prepared security witness in the same transaction/lock as the write. The Hub supplies control to the transport adapter without a dispatcher dependency cycle. Offsets aggregate by maximum across recipients, with monotonicity enforced separately within each attachment. [Verification](reports/tenant-delivery-receipts-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantSessionLiveDelivery.SendTextAsync` uses `TenantRunService.SubmitTextAsync` with the actual subscribed human/tenant/Session and the client event UUID as correlation. `CancelResponseAsync` resolves a response-to-Run mapping observed by that attachment and calls the same Run service after independent read/cancel authority. The Hub supplies these services as adapters, avoiding a singleton dispatcher/control dependency cycle. The subscription holds at most 128 command fingerprints/acks and 128 observed response mappings. Replay creates no new admission/effect authority and rechecks current access; reconnect text replay relies on existing durable admission identity. [Verification](reports/tenant-hub-commands-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantConversationService.AuthorizeRecipientAsync` returns only actual ownership/revision, text transport state and public agent identity after current human/session and exact Session-read/agent-Chat checks, with a final human/security recheck. `TenantSessionLiveDelivery.Source` binds existing `ISessionOutput` to one actual tenant Run/generation and publishes an allowlisted text lifecycle. It reuses the public event mapper, strips untyped effect/memory receipts, and does not publish resource, legacy approval or voice payloads. The tenant host activates/removes that source with its existing Runtime/capacity lifetime. Tenant-bound Hub message/cancellation now use the same canonical services; HTTP remains available independently. [Verification](reports/tenant-live-text-delivery-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantConversationStore.ReadTenantConversationAsync` accepts exact tenant/Session references and bounded existing history cursors, returning one owned Session revision and raw stored page for trusted Application use. InMemory reads under shared locks; SQLite uses a bounded read transaction. `TenantConversationService.ReadAsync` requires actual current human/session, exact `sessions.read` and Instance `agents.chat`, independent of a Run claim. It applies `PublicHistory` plus recipient resource checks and revalidates human/security/Session witnesses after I/O. Unavailable/foreign/deleted content denies; concurrent changes conflict rather than return stale bytes. No permission snapshot, public persistence snapshot or delivery receipt is created. [Verification](reports/tenant-conversation-history-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantRunCancellationService.PrepareAsync` resolves the actual current human/session and exact Run, requiring Run cancel/read, owning Session read and Instance chat. `TenantRunCancellation` contains trusted references, generation and revision witnesses, never a copied permission snapshot. `TenantRunService.CancelAsync` uses `ITenantRunControl` on the same singleton tenant dispatcher. A matching Runtime processes an urgent command and calls `CommitAsync`, which repeats current authority/reference checks before the security/Run CAS transaction. Without a matching owner, the host reserves cancellation against admission. Successful stop returns a Cancelled Run immediately; terminal/stale requests conflict. The store closes saved streaming response entries and can atomically merge the owning mailbox’s not-yet-checkpointed response, pending approval/expiry and Available grants with security revision and audit. Transient response identity, sequence, existing prefix and nonregressing receipts are fenced; this evidence never comes from an HTTP/model payload. Claimed/Consumed/Indeterminate evidence is retained. [Verification](reports/tenant-active-run-cancellation-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`PreparedTenantOperation` has a closed credential-connection-check payload alongside persona/preferences. `TenantApprovalService.PrepareCredentialCheckAsync` derives the actual pinned owned binding, credential-use target and logical/secret/auth-epoch witnesses, with current executor read and mandatory Sensitive Review/ExceptionalGrant. `TenantCredentialCheckService.ExecuteAsync` accepts only trusted Run/request/operation/generation/hash references and rechecks current IAM, qualified grantor, binding, capability, expiry, host and credential witnesses. `ITenantCredentialCheckStore` atomically claims an exact grant, reserves the credential and marks the Run in flight before returning a new dispatch fence. Only that returned fence may invoke `ITenantCredentialOAuthVerifier`; the provider returns normalized protected rotation/reconnect facts, never a bearer token. Completion writes the protected result, immutable receipt, grant status, Run marker, security revision and audit atomically. An uncertain result records Indeterminate and requires reconnect when the original credential witness still matches; a newer grant is never overwritten. Available grants remain revocable; Claimed is a spent dispatch reservation, not permission for another call. Factual completion may retain a known rotation after cancellation/role loss without restoring authority or reviving a terminal Run. `RecoverAsync` closes expired/terminal unfinished fences through the existing worker without provider I/O, including after Session logical deletion. Model initiation and exact Run-B consumption now use the existing authorization tool and continuation service; public preparation/effect endpoints remain absent, and the local configuration executor still rejects fresh credential effects. [Execution evidence](reports/tenant-credential-runtime-check-verification-2026-10-17.md), [preparation evidence](reports/tenant-credential-approval-target-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantApprovalToolContract` adds `{ operation: "check_credential_connection", reference }` with no caller-supplied credential ID, authority or witness. Offering requires pinned credential bindings and Email/Drive account capability, independently of Settings management. The offered schema includes only the applicable operation families; preparation still rechecks actual current target/read/use policy. `TenantRunContinuationService.PrepareExternalEffectAsync` is called only by the shared-capacity host outside the mailbox. It verifies exact source/checkpoint lineage and remaining active budget before the existing check service. `ResumeAsync` replays the external receipt, completes the saved tool call with `applied: true | false | null`, and charges active provider time without charging the human wait. Indeterminate permits no further operation tools and terminates as a failed Run after its factual response. [Verification](reports/tenant-model-credential-check-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantRunContext.CurrentRequesterAsync(AgentRun)` is implemented by the existing `TenantRunExecutionAuthority`, which also remains the current Definition/dispatch authority. It reads actual Run ownership, full immutable execution attribution, Running/noncancelled status, generation and unexpired lease; current agent/requester access is resolved through the existing operation authorizer. `TenantRequesterContext` is immutable, internally constructed presentation data: original human principal/display label, Tenant label, current executor, observed revision/time and at most 16 distinct relevant role-name/scope hints. Only assignments on this agent, this Session or inherited Tenant scope with relevant agent/Session permission types are included. Unrelated resources, other people, permission lists, Account login/security fields, web sessions, grant data and system roles are excluded. Truncation is explicit. Human absence is represented as no original requester, never a fabricated User. Final Run/security and Account rechecks fail closed during changes; Account changes are fenced separately because they need not increment the tenant revision. The Runtime requires this source for tenant execution, refreshes it before generation and verifies the projection belongs to the exact execution. [Verification](reports/tenant-requester-context-verification-2026-10-17.md).

The shared approval lifecycle requires `ITenantApprovedOperationStore`; missing production effect persistence is a composition failure, not a metadata-only mode. Connected-account result projection reuses `TenantOperationAuthorizer`'s tenant-qualified Run lookup. Existing typed Memory, Continuity and Experience owners reobserve saved parameters and compute feature-specific reviewer eligibility/independence; the shared service retains request lifecycle, current human authority, decisions, grants and continuation. No generic workflow registry or alternate authorization engine is introduced.

`TenantApprovalService` adds read-only review projections and authority-fenced paged inventory. `ITenantApprovalStore.ListApprovalsAsync` accepts a bounded typed query and returns a page/count after authorization and status/kind filters. Current qualification uses the pure operation evaluator and actual waiting source Run; UI reads create no grant or decision. Final human-session/security revision revalidation fences returned hints and totals. An optional bounded typed `TenantApprovalSnapshot` is saved in the existing request PayloadJson: original persona or effective preference defaults plus actual nullable overrides and agent name. It is immutable display evidence, excluded from exact action hash, semantic fingerprint and grant authority. New preparation captures the witnessed target; mapping rejects malformed snapshots. Earlier requests without it remain readable with explicit unavailable before/default values when the original witnesses no longer match. No schema migration is required. [Verification](reports/tenant-approval-ui-verification-2026-10-17.md).

## Account and scoped IAM ports (identity foundation)

`ITenantApprovalStore.TryResumeApprovalAsync` accepts Core-generated continuation IDs and atomically acknowledges a model decision with a source-to-successor handoff. `TenantApprovalContinuationRules` derives a closed `TenantApprovalResume` from the actual source/request/signal: exact operation/hash, original input read references and same executor/Session/requester/root. `ApprovalContinuation` owns no input entries. It preserves frozen pins, transcript/response, remaining budget, attempts and capability accounting. Source Run A ends with a Continued result linking B; internal pre-execution requests without a model checkpoint may still requeue A. Claim and effect stores resolve the actual source and require the recorded handoff, not supplied lineage alone. The scoped Run cancel service now accepts any nonterminal Run through the coordination contract above. A fresh B effect also requires the matching pending intent; completed receipts remain replayable. [Verification](reports/tenant-cross-run-approval-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantPreferenceToolContract` defines context-only `settings.update_preferences`, limited to response length, language and the one-question preference through strict `set`/`clear` fields. Core derives the actual executor, target, Run claim and current revisions; model arguments contain no identity, permission or authority witness. `TenantPreferenceOperationService` requires the executor's own read/update permissions, an Allowed requester-aware IAM/review decision and pinned/current Managed Settings plus selected conversation-preference auto-apply. The direct tool returns RequiresApproval or Denied without an effect, request or grant; eligible callers explicitly use the durable authorization tool for review. Persona and Assisted mutations retain their review boundary.

`ITenantPreferenceOperationStore` reads the same durable local-operation receipts by tenant/Run/trusted call ID and atomically commits the exact bounded settings patch, succeeded Run marker, receipt, security revision and audit. The service binds canonical argument hash and target/Run revisions; the adapter rechecks live claim, cancellation, owned target, management and global Account status at commit. Replay validates the same parameters and ownership and returns the saved result while the Run remains executable. Clearing a field restores its immutable Definition default while retaining unrelated overrides. The shared executor requires actual tenant context and generation; missing tenant services never fall back to legacy settings. [Verification](reports/tenant-direct-preferences-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantApprovalToolContract` defines the closed `approvals.request_authorization` alternatives `update_persona` and `update_preferences`. The latter takes the same strict conversation-preference set/clear object as direct execution. `PreparedTenantOperation` carries exactly one typed persona or preference payload, common owned target/revision witnesses and canonical hash. Unknown, mixed, duplicate or caller-authority fields reject. `TenantApprovalService` preserves review for Assisted/unselected mutations and persona even when ordinary IAM would Allow; selected Managed preferences with an Allowed decision create no redundant request/effect. Offering and preparation require trusted tenant identity, live generation and pinned/current Settings eligibility. The shared executor refuses direct wait creation; the Runtime owns suspension. [Verification](reports/tenant-preference-approval-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantRuntimeStore` loads/saves existing Session snapshots and history under an actual live owned Run claim, lists canonical runnable Runs and applies existing `AgentRunCommand` transitions. `TenantRuntimeFrame` is a trusted server-held tenant/Session/Run/generation reference, never a model argument or cookie. `TenantRuntimeMemoryStore` and `TenantAgentRunStore` bind the existing Runtime ports to this frame; Profile, global admission and unsupported continuation paths cannot fall back to legacy services. Bound Run writes atomically reject a replaced generation, even if the stale caller discovers its successor. `ITenantRunExecution` invokes the existing coordinator with these bindings; `ITenantRunDispatcher` uses the isolated tenant host.
