User-turn Runs may now resume their own decided persona approval, with exact request/Run/Session/root/executor/requester checks at claim. Pending requests and unsupported origins remain excluded. [Runtime verification](reports/tenant-runtime-execution-verification-2026-10-17.md) and its [approval-worker successor](reports/tenant-approval-worker-verification-2026-10-17.md) distinguish this from full transport/effect completion.

`TenantRunContinuationService.ResumeAsync` accepts the actual dispatched Run, re-reads its current uncancelled/unexpired generation, resolves the saved `ApprovalRequestId` and calls the typed persona executor only when Approved. It re-reads the Run after effect I/O and returns the fresh Run revision plus `TenantApprovalOutcome`. Rejected, expired or cancelled requests produce stable denial evidence without an effect. Successful/denied approved-effect receipts are reused after recovery. No new caller-supplied action, grant, target or human identity enters this path. `AgentContext.ApprovalOutcome` is validated against trusted tenant/Run/root/Instance references by the prompt builder; its Evidence section is retained under optional-context pressure and supplies no grant/hash authority.

`ITenantApprovedOperationStore` reads the immutable human decision and exact operation receipt and commits one local approved persona or conversation-preference result through `TryCommitLocalEffectAsync`. `TenantApprovedOperationService.ExecuteAsync` accepts only trusted Run/request/operation/generation/hash references; all typed parameters and target witnesses come from the saved request. Current grantor action/approve/exception authority and applicable independence are rechecked through the same IAM engine, without copying roles or requiring the former web session to remain logged in. Review cannot silently become exceptional authority. Pinned and live Settings management must permit the action; freeze, stale target/configuration, expired approval/grant or lost authority cannot be overridden by an approved row. The adapter transaction rechecks the live generation, global Account status, current target and expiry; it records a stable denial and revokes an available exact grant when appropriate. Success consumes the exact grant, applies the saved typed change and writes a Run effect checkpoint plus immutable receipt/security/audit atomically. Retry returns the receipt without another mutation/audit. Original internal requests and recorded model successor Runs are supported for these closed kinds; remaining effect families stay open; original-Run worker consumption now uses the continuation service above. See [approved persona effect verification](reports/tenant-approved-persona-effect-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantAuthorizationAuditStore` appends execution decision evidence to the existing AuthorizationAudit owner. `TenantOperationAuthorizer` requires its acknowledgement for current Run inspection, ordinary operation eligibility and supported approval gates; an unavailable/stale audit cannot produce a usable decision for an effect. The adapter verifies actual saved Run context, observed Run/security revisions and claim generation before append. No permission or Run revision changes merely because a check is recorded. Candidate approver qualification and internal Runtime reasoning remain pure evaluation. Records distinguish Inspection, ApprovalPreparation, ApprovalDecision, EffectEligibility and ToolBoundary; an ordinary RequiresApproval eligibility result is not consent or an effect outcome. Unknown permission text and malformed resource arguments are replaced with bounded safe metadata.

Persona/preference effect transactions additionally attach EffectResult context to their existing immutable outcome audit, with actual Run/root/requester, pre-commit revisions and exact operation/request/grant references. This metadata commits with the setting/Run/receipt/security result. Replay retains the original outcome audit. Same-root suppression and quota denial record their final decision without another request audit or wait. Known owned Run authority failures and blocked tenant tool boundaries retain safe denial evidence; unknown/cross-tenant Run references cannot fabricate an executor. This covers current canonical Run gates and local effects; general human HTTP/read denials and remaining effect families still require completion. See [execution authorization audit verification](reports/tenant-execution-authorization-audit-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantApprovalStore.FindRootApprovalAsync` accepts a typed prepared operation and correlates tenant/root/executor by semantic permission, target and normalized arguments. Operation IDs and mutable Instance/persona revisions are excluded from this correlation; exact saved `ActionHash` and witnesses still bind human decisions, grants and effects. Same-root Pending returns `already_pending`; Rejected, Expired and Cancelled retain stable refusal reasons through configuration/permission changes and successor Runs. Direct preferences recheck the same guard inside their effect transaction. Historical duplicate requests prefer terminal refusals, then pending requests, then approved requests; a conflicting fresh approved effect records `root_operation_conflict` and revokes its exact grant.

Both stores derive root correlation and the operation quota from `PreparedTenantOperation.OperationFingerprint`. SQLite filters by the indexed tenant/root/executor lineage before comparing Domain semantics; effect transactions use the same helper. It does not maintain a second per-family JSON matcher. The stored `Fingerprint` column remains the exact `ActionHash` foreign-key witness for decisions, grants and receipts.

Approval admission atomically limits pending-or-created-within-one-minute requests to 128 per tenant, 32 per executor, 32 per actual human requester and 8 per semantic operation. No-human independent roots have no shared null-requester quota; tenant, executor, operation and root limits still apply. A root also has a lifetime ceiling of 32 distinct requests across its executors. Cutoffs use the store clock; duplicates resolve before quotas and do not create an inbox row, request audit, wait or security revision; the authorization decision itself is recorded. Limit failures return structured Denied `approval_rate_limited` or `approval_root_limit`, leaving the Run unchanged. Pending requests stay charged until terminal, even after the rolling window. These limits bound current durable inbox creation; a separate notification delivery transport is not implemented. See [approval loop verification](reports/tenant-approval-loop-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantApprovalStore` owns normalized request/decision/grant/signal transactions alongside the actual tenant Run and IAM stores. `TenantApprovalService.PreparePersonaAsync` accepts a trusted saved Run reference and typed persona, deriving Core-owned `agents.update`, the actual executing Instance, immutable operation ID, canonical action/hash and current Instance/persona witnesses. It exposes no generic model-selected permission or arbitrary-action authority. Creation atomically stores the request and Run wait under Run/security/generation witnesses; a queued operation may require authorization before its first model attempt. `DecideAsync` qualifies an active human with current underlying action, approve and, for an exceptional grant, grant-exception authority and applicable independence. Decisions require exact action hash plus request/security revisions; the original live human session and expiry are checked again at commit. `CancelAsync` instead requires scoped `approvals.cancel` and may terminate a materially stale request without granting its action. Read/list require both `approvals.read` at the actual request and underlying operation authority at its target; visibility filters precede the bounded inventory limit. The existing worker drains due expiry and processed signals, atomically acknowledging a signal with Run requeue. No legacy embedded approval or login credential enters this tenant path. See [durable approval verification](reports/tenant-durable-approval-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantOperationAuthorizer` resolves `TenantRunReference` through actual owned rows and current executor/Instance, requester Session read/chat and tenant IAM. It composes the existing `IamAuthorizer`, returning current Allowed/Denied/RequiresApproval eligibility with Review/ExceptionalGrant meaning; it does not mint authority. `TenantOperationPolicyService` requires the original live human session with independent tenant `tenant.governance.read` for coherent catalog/detail inspection and human-only `tenant.governance.manage` for writes. Catalog search/count/paging excludes system permissions and derives current editability plus immutable human-only/delegatability metadata; post-projection login/read/security fences reject races. Its dedicated `ITenantOperationPolicyStore` commits current security/policy witnesses and audit while rechecking that session; `TenantSecurityState.OperationPolicies` preserves policy through unrelated aggregate/resource writes. Core approval classes and minimums are immutable catalog metadata.

`PermissionCatalog` declares each action's risk explicitly; the current catalog contains 78 keys. The [reviewed inventory and current catalog audit](reports/tenant-approval-defaults-audit-2026-10-24.md) records all keys, primary resource families, principal flags and minimums. It does not infer risk from action/family names. The minimums are **ReadOnly → None**, **Mutation → WhenRequesterLacksPermission**, and **Sensitive → Always**, with no default independent-review requirement. Tenant policy may strengthen either dimension and cannot weaken the Core floor. Human-only/System-only and nondelegatable reserves remain separate hard gates.

A genuinely admitted independent Schedule/Event root has no human requester. `WhenRequesterLacksPermission` does not fabricate a denied human or require a registration author's permission; current executor authority, task/source and operation-specific gates remain mandatory. An actual human requester is rechecked, and missing action authority under that mode requires ordinary Review. `Always` requires Review even without a human initiator. Independence constrains a required reviewer; it does not turn an otherwise Allowed operation into `Always`. Missing executor authority yields only eligible exact ExceptionalGrant classification, never borrowed requester roles or automatic consent. Risk-specific mandatory consent, including general management changes and native sensitive effects, remains in its existing operation owner. [Catalog, decision-table and actual-effect audit](reports/tenant-approval-floor-verification-2026-10-22.md).

`ITenantRunStore.ResolveTenantSessionOwnerAsync` is a trusted internal owning-root lookup including logical tombstones, never a public private-content read. `TenantToolAuthorization` resolves it and the current live claim before resource/Runtime dispatch. Missing/mismatched tenant/Session/Instance/Run frames, expired generations and inactive authority deny. `iam.check_access` and `iam.get_effective_permissions` inspect only that actual executor and accept resource/permission inputs, no caller identity, policy or grant; they repeat the claim fence before returning. Effective results exclude possible exceptional authority. Current typed reads, mutations and Runtime commands use their dedicated owners; unsupported calls cannot fall through to global services or the generic IAM parser. A legacy tool approval object supplies no authority. [Current registered-owner checkpoint](reports/tenant-model-tool-authority-verification-2026-10-24.md); [original inspection-boundary evidence](reports/tenant-operation-authorization-verification-2026-10-17.md).

The caller-constructed local approval grant and shared/direct Email, HTTP, Drive, management and identity-maintenance preview facades are retired. Canonical tenant preparation derives exact operations from trusted saved Runs and typed owning services; their saved request, current authority and receipt govern execution. Unowned sensitive calls cannot turn model-supplied approval fields into authority or read a provider draft before refusal. Retained Automation argument preparation still fences actual tenant Session ownership; pure normalization and preview formatting supply no authority.

`TenantRunService.SubmitTextAsync` resolves a current human session, exact `sessions.read` and `agents.chat`, actual owned Session/executor and current Instance configuration through tenant-aware stores. `ITenantRunStore.TryAdmitTenantHumanRunAsync` commits the input, Activation, queued Run, security/Session revisions and audit atomically, with original human/session and Instance generation rechecked. Trusted execution context carries immutable references only. Exact Run reads require independent Run/Session read. Run cancellation requires Run cancel/read, Session read and exact chat, with current human/security/Run witnesses. Running work uses its owning mailbox; attached Session state alone does not prevent an exact Run stop. Other Session lifecycle mutations retain their separate quiescence requirements. Logical Session deletion retires Run grants too. `AgentRunOwner.ForTenant` has no global Profile; legacy `IAgentRunStore` queries/workers exclude tenant Runs. [Verification](reports/tenant-run-admission-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`TenantSessionService.ChangeAsync` and `ITenantSessionStore.TryChangeTenantSessionAsync` accept a closed `TenantSessionChange`, not an edited snapshot. Rename/archive/restore and user pause/resume/complete/cancel require exact `sessions.manage/read`; resume also rechecks current Instance chat, lifecycle and executor. Logical delete requires `sessions.delete`, returns no metadata and retires direct Session grants atomically. Current human/session, security revision and Session revision fence every mutation. Stores derive the result through the existing user lifecycle rules and reject attached/ending Sessions, pending input or nonterminal Runs until live coordination is integrated. Deletion retains owning/entry identities while redacting conversation content, preserving Activation foreign keys. [Lifecycle verification](reports/tenant-session-lifecycle-verification-2026-10-17.md) leaves live coordination, automatic recovery and external resource cleanup pending.

`ITenantSessionStore` reads actual owned Session metadata, filters tenant/scoped IDs before counts/paging, and creates the backing Session/snapshot and immutable `TenantSessionRoot` with security CAS/audit. `TenantSessionService` independently requires tenant Session create/read and exact Instance chat; it resolves the real owned publication or immutable shipped definition, current settings/model/persona, active executor and authenticated creating User. Its public methods return `TenantSessionMetadata`, never the internal Definition/snapshot; safe projection belongs to Application, not only HTTP mapping. `TenantSessionInitiator` and OAuth initiators share `HumanSessionReference`: identity references that are revalidated, not permission snapshots. Store commit rechecks human session/stamp, current Instance/executor and source revisions. Global `IMemoryStore` and legacy Run admission cannot read/write/adopt owned Sessions or create unscoped Sessions for owned Instances. Session-to-Instance identity is immutable in both stores. These management ports do not start a tenant runtime. [Verification](reports/tenant-session-ownership-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantCredentialOAuthVerifier` checks an already connected, owned Google Credential inside Infrastructure. Refresh/access tokens and provider responses remain there; its normalized result contains only an optional protected replacement refresh grant or `ReconnectRequired`. `TenantCredentialOAuthCheckService` requires the authenticated human/session and current exact Credential read/manage/use independently, serializes checks per owned resource, revalidates after bounded provider I/O and commits through `TryCheckCredentialOAuthAsync`. Both stores recheck that human/session plus security/logical/secret witnesses atomically with audit. Routine rotation preserves logical revision and AuthEpoch; permanent grant rejection clears the payload and advances both. This operator check remains distinct from exact-approved tenant runtime credential consumption. [Verification](reports/tenant-oauth-check-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`ITenantCredentialOAuthStore` is the specialized, trusted persistence port for begin/claim/commit/disconnect, terminal failure and bounded expiry. `CredentialOAuthAttempt` contains original Account/session/Principal/Tenant/Credential identity, purpose, logical revision/AuthEpoch, state/correlation digests, opaque protected private proof, provider descriptor and a five-minute deadline. Claim atomically consumes the pending state and clears the durable proof, returning it once for an exchange outside the transaction. Commit requires the claimed revision, original live identity, fresh security CAS and initial Credential witnesses; it only accepts an exact connection transition. Disconnect derives its authorization changes in the store. Failures/expiry do not change a saved grant or append a successful grant audit. The registered port has both store implementations. `TenantCredentialOAuthService` resolves ownership and rechecks current scoped read/manage and original human/session authority before preparation, claim and after I/O; no persisted attempt grants authority. `ITenantCredentialOAuthProvider` describes trusted shared client/redirect/mode/host setup, prepares protected private proof, exchanges a claimed proof for normalized verified identity/scopes plus protected refresh grant, and attempts remote revocation after local disconnection. Provider DTOs/raw tokens stay in Infrastructure. [Store](reports/tenant-oauth-store-verification-2026-10-17.md) and [flow verification](reports/tenant-oauth-flow-verification-2026-10-17.md) distinguish persistence, backend HTTP, offline provider and separate [Credential UI evidence](reports/tenant-credential-ui-verification-2026-10-17.md) and pending runtime/live-provider acceptance.

`IIamStore` is the specialized transactional security store: Account lookup, system/tenant security reads, Account membership listing, one-use bootstrap, atomic tenant create/CAS with audit, Account security-stamp CAS, authentication-session persistence/revocation and scoped bounded audit reads. `TenantSecurityState` is an immutable security aggregate whose revision serializes membership, invitations, roles, assignments and agent identities; it is never a cached authorization decision. Each store validates references, freezes collections and protects the last active human Owner, including Account suspension. SQLite security reads/writes use per-operation contexts and transactions; InMemory uses equivalent locked operations.

`IAccountPasswordHasher` keeps the vetted ASP.NET password implementation in Infrastructure. `IIdentityTokens` creates and hashes opaque invitation/security material. `IdentityService`, `TenantAccessService`, `SystemAdministrationService`, `SystemOperatorService` and `IamAuthorizer` remain framework-free Application services. The unused global `AccountAdministrationService` and its registration are removed from production; native tests retain their explicit fixture helper. Account authentication stores only protected session references in the cookie and resolves current status/stamp on every request. Tenant resource owners must resolve target ownership before asking `IamAuthorizer`; a client-provided scope is not evidence of ownership. The [delivery ledger](reports/multitenant-iam-delivery-2026-10-16.md) records remaining store/resource/Hub integration; these ports alone do not complete the cutover.

Human member/invitation/role/assignment HTTP commands derive the original `HumanSessionReference` server-side and carry it into `IIamStore.TrySaveTenantAsync`. The owning lock/transaction validates that exact live tenant User/session alongside current actor, security CAS, active-Owner and real resource references. Authenticated invitation acceptance precedes membership and instead proves the existing Account Principal/session against the new User’s Account; public registration retains its separate exact invitation-proof path and issues no login. Password changes keep password reauthentication and Account-stamp CAS, with original-login checks inside `TryUpdateAccountAsync`. Tenant access/member projections repeat the original-human and security-revision fence after I/O, including late Account-label reads. Account metadata/membership projections repeat their original Account/session/Principal check. These identity references supply no System permission or autonomous Run authority. [Executed verification](reports/tenant-iam-human-authority-verification-2026-10-24.md).

`IamResourceScopes` resolves the security aggregate's `member` (User ID), `role` (Role ID) and `agent` (registered Agent Instance ID) references. Incoming GUIDs normalize to canonical D form. Definition roots/bindings and owner-qualified Instance `skill`/`resource` references resolve through `IIamStore.OwnsResourceAsync` against their actual owning root and current child/source records. `IamAuthorizer` checks target existence/tenant ownership and catalog resource applicability before evaluating grants; aggregate writes reject nonexistent or foreign resource assignments too. IAM inspection/assignment and exact-action approval authority apply at the protected resource's scope; ordinary permissions apply only to their catalog resource family. `TenantAccessService.ReadResourceAccessAsync` requires `iam.roles.read` at that exact resource and returns its direct plus inherited assignments and the security revision. No generic resource registry or caller-supplied ownership assertion substitutes for an owning store. Other families fail closed through resource-scoped IAM until their stores are converted. Registered agent identity ownership is globally unique across tenants and requires a real backing Agent Instance.


`ITenantAgentStore` is the specialized transaction port over existing Agent Instances and tenant security. `TryCreateTenantAgentAsync` atomically inserts the Instance, initial Skill state, its SystemIdentity/Principal and security CAS/audit; it grants no role. `TrySetTenantAgentLifecycleAsync` requires both security and Instance revisions, changes lifecycle and identity status together and preserves backing IDs/grants. Both recheck the actor and current global Account status at commit. Existing identity ownership/backing cannot be removed or rebound. SQLite uses an actual restrictive backing foreign key; InMemory shares the existing Instance store and locks.

`TenantAgentService` authorizes current `agents.create/read/update`; lifecycle mutation responses also require `agents.read` because they contain the saved Instance projection. Template reads/creation use only `IBuiltInAgentDefinitionStore`. `CreateFromPublicationAsync` separately requires `agents.create` and read access to an exact active publication returned by the tenant definition store; it never falls back to the global durable catalog. Owned reads join SystemIdentity to the backing Instance. Inventory filters tenant ownership and allowed resource IDs before count, ordering and pagination; null IDs mean tenant-wide access, an empty set means no results. Lifecycle uses the existing Instance coordination gate and subsequent Automation/Sandbox reconciliation when composed in the full host. Decommission/delete, tenant Sandbox selection, other resource IAM and execution admission remain pending.

`ITenantDefinitionStore` owns the definition root and scoped draft/publication queries over the existing authoring records. Root inventory filters tenant and allowed IDs before totals/order/pagination. `TenantDefinitionService` checks current `agent-definitions.create/read/update/publish/delete` separately from Instance permissions. Create generates an opaque definition ID and server starter; update requires immutable identity plus security/draft revisions; publish assigns the next version, verifies resource bindings/bytes and required evaluation witnesses, freezes the publication, consumes the draft/children and commits security revision/audit atomically. Fork copies an owned exact publication and resource bindings into a new draft. Deprecation updates metadata, retaining immutable payload bytes. There is no tenant/global durable fallback.

Canonical Definition HTTP handlers derive the original `HumanSessionReference` from the authenticated tenant context. `TenantDefinitionService` validates that login during preparation and again after metadata/resource-byte I/O, withholding the captured response if the login retires or tenant security revision changes. All eleven Definition mutation slots carry that reference into the owning lock/transaction, which reuses the current human-session check before any change (and SQLite before final commit). Offline evaluation repeats the witness before computation and at result commit. Logout or password rotation cannot turn an already-prepared HTTP request into a successful authoring mutation. Internal principal-only commands retain their independent scoped IAM contract; this reference grants no autonomous Run or source authority. [Verification](reports/tenant-definition-human-authority-verification-2026-10-24.md).

`IBuiltInDefinitionTemplateSource` reads exact shipped definitions and their explicitly approved bytes; it has no durable publication lookup. Definition template discovery and `ForkTemplateAsync` require tenant `agent-definitions.create`, independently of `agents.create`. Fork generates a new owning ID/draft, retains original `SourceDefinitionId` and exact `SourceVersion`, copies approved content into fresh owned resource bindings, and commits root/draft/bindings/security/audit through `TryCreateDefinitionAsync` atomically. The port now accepts the initial manifest and verifies every actual byte binding before committing any child. Source kind/identity/version and creation time cannot change on draft updates. Existing owned-publication forks retain their own publication version lineage.

`FileBuiltInDefinitionTemplateSource` reads knowledge identities only from the configured agent directory's approved `knowledge/{identity}.md`, binding them at the Definition's declared logical path. Workspace template files come only from the configured `Persistence:TemplateRoot/{templateId}`; the top-level `knowledge`/`harness` exclusions match first provisioning. It uses descriptor-relative reads/listing without link resolution, ordinary files/directories, per-item/count/aggregate limits, a 256-directory traversal bound and the existing resource path/media/secret policies. Skills may reference those approved bindings; other agent-directory files are not an implicit resource catalog. Missing/unapproved dependencies reject the fork rather than create a partial or unpublishable graph. Shared content-addressed storage may deduplicate bytes, but each draft receives new resource IDs and no authority from the source template.

External-resource assignment shape is checked by `IamStateRules`; the concrete IAM transaction additionally proves each definition assignment belongs to its tenant through the actual definition store. Unknown families still fail closed. Definition ownership is retained independently of a consumed draft. Complete authoring UI and runtime execution resolution remain required before the complete definition cutover is accepted.

`TenantDefinitionService.SummaryAsync` reads one active owned root with its draft count, latest publication version and display name under current Definition read authority. `AccessAsync` returns only the current actor's applicable Definition read/update/publish/delete and resource IAM read/assign permissions with the tenant security revision; it does not disclose assignments or establish authority for another actor. Canonical authoring metadata reuses the configured authoring choices, execution ceilings and safe capability descriptors. Its API requires read on an exact owned Definition, or tenant Definition-create authority when no Definition is supplied. It returns no credentials and does not use the old owner capability. The [tenant editor evidence](reports/tenant-definition-ui-verification-2026-10-16.md) records implemented UI and remaining boundaries.

`DeleteDraftAsync` requires current Definition delete plus read authority and both security/draft revisions. It removes that draft's resources/scenarios/results; deleting the last unpublished draft retires the logical root. `DeleteAsync` requires the complete reviewed draft-ID/revision and publication-version/metadata-revision witness (`AdminDefinitionDeleteWitness`), plus current security revision. Any missing, additional or changed child rejects with `stale_revision`. Retiring a root removes only its direct resource assignments in the same transaction, retaining other scopes/roles. The store rechecks current actor/Account status and actual Instance/live Session/retained Run/Activation references under its transaction/locks. `definition_in_use` rejects without content, revision or audit changes. A retained deleted-root marker prevents ID reuse and unscoped authoring access; it confers no current IAM ownership. Shared immutable content bytes are conservatively retained.

`ITenantDefinitionStore` also exposes an atomic owned draft snapshot (candidate/resources/scenarios/latest result per current scenario), publication resource bindings, resource batch/remove and scenario/result mutation ports. Child authoring inherits the parent Definition's permissions: read for metadata/content and read plus update for mutations/evaluation; publication/deprecation returning saved payload also require read plus publish. These are Definition editor workflows; standalone resource-family IAM remains a separate pending boundary.

`TenantDefinitionService.BindResourcesAsync` accepts actual bytes or an already-owned resource ID for metadata-only edits. It never accepts a content hash as ownership proof. Reuse/replacement IDs must be bound to the same draft. The existing path/media/secret/size/aggregate policies apply. New IDs are server-generated. Content staging is private and immutable; binding, draft revision, security CAS and audit commit together. Downloads resolve an owned binding, verify immutable bytes and recheck current read authority after I/O.

Offline evaluation reuses `AgentDefinitionDraftSyntheticEvaluationRunner` outside persistence locks. A result commits only for the same current draft, scenario version, configuration fingerprint and actor authority. Scenario edits change draft revision; evaluation results preserve it. The store assigns `CommitRevision` from its committed tenant security revision, so same-timestamp outcomes have deterministic ordering and callers cannot choose evidence precedence. Snapshots return the latest result per current scenario; retained historical rows are not an execution grant. Publication exposes safe `evaluation_required`, `evaluation_stale`, `evaluation_failed` and `definition_resources_invalid` recovery reasons.

### Tenant Credential management ports

`ITenantCredentialStore` is the specialized transaction boundary over the existing protected Credential and profile rows, `TenantCredentialRoot`, tenant security CAS and audit. Owned queries apply actual ownership and allowed IDs before totals/order/paging. Writes compare logical and secret revisions, preserve identity/kind/purpose/authentication method, freeze mutable inputs and recheck the actor/current global Account at commit. Human HTTP commands additionally carry the server-derived original Account/authentication-session/principal into the same owning transaction. Both stores revalidate that exact live human before mutation; SQLite repeats it before final commit. Logout, password rotation or actor/reference substitution refuses without Credential, binding, Instance, IAM-revision or audit changes. Deletion refuses bindings, prunes exact Credential assignments and retains the owner marker, while removing the protected row/profile. Raw deletion retaining grants and creation that adopts an existing global or retired ID are rejected atomically.

`TenantCredentialService` requires current `credentials.read` for metadata and both read/manage for create/update/replace responses. Creation requires those permissions at Tenant scope; exact existing-item grants do not create siblings. Delete requires `credentials.manage` and both revisions and returns no metadata. `credentials.use` alone does not reveal metadata, protected values or grant another effect. OAuth profiles are created disconnected; normal CRUD cannot supply refresh tokens, verified identities, scopes or Synthetic authorization state. Protection stays behind `ICredentialProtector`; all public views reuse the safe `CredentialView` without payload, protection version or secret generation. Canonical binding configuration is described below; secret use belongs to the separate current typed Browser/account/Sandbox owners. `AccessAsync` resolves the actual owned Credential, requires current `credentials.read`, and returns `TenantCredentialAccess` with the current security revision and independently allowed `credentials.read/manage/bind/use` and `iam.roles.read/manage` keys at that exact resource. This is a UI affordance snapshot, never mutation authority; commands still recheck current IAM. Human list/item/access reads validate the original live login before work and again after source/visibility I/O, with the same security revision. An unavailable initiator withholds the whole projection; revision churn returns `stale_revision`, with no read retry. The [Credential UI report](reports/tenant-credential-ui-verification-2026-10-17.md) records browser evidence.

### Tenant Credential binding ports

`ITenantCredentialBindingStore` reads a coherent owned Instance/binding/Credential snapshot and commits bind/unbind with the same Instance, security CAS and audit. Queries join both actual owners in the requested Tenant. Writes compare security/Instance witnesses, the selected Credential revision for bind and binding revision for removal; they recheck actor/Account status, lifecycle and the supplied original human login inside the transaction. Canonical human HTTP writes always supply that server-derived reference; trusted principal-only internal commands retain their independent IAM/current-policy contract. Reference/credential uniqueness is Instance-local. Credential values and role assignments remain unchanged. SQLite mutation queries explicitly track the owned backing Instance, while inventory queries remain untracked.

`TenantCredentialBindingService` requires parent `agents.read` for inventory, then filters `credentials.read` before visible totals/paging and reports `AllRead`. Bind requires parent read/update and item `credentials.read/bind`; unlink requires parent read/update and item bind, returning only new revisions without Credential content. Permissions precede stale-witness errors. Agent writers additionally require the effective Connections area and an unfrozen policy; human configuration does not inherit the agent's policy ceiling. New bindings require an active non-registry Credential, while disconnected OAuth configuration is permitted without use authority. Existing disabled credentials can still be unlinked; archived Instances must be restored before edits. No role, `credentials.use` permission or admitted runtime credential is granted by binding.

Binding inventory also returns the authorized parent `AgentName`/`Lifecycle`, exact parent `CanUpdate` and `RemovableBindingIds` limited to visible rows on the returned page. Removal hints require parent update, an active Instance and exact Credential bind permission. These affordances come from the existing authorizer and are covered by the same final read fence; commands independently recheck authority and revisions. They neither require IAM-inspection permission nor grant execution rights.

Binding inventory rechecks parent authority and the original tenant security revision after snapshot I/O and item filtering. A changed revision rejects the entire projection with `stale_revision`; a lost parent or unavailable Account denies it. Canonical human HTTP reads use `ListForHumanAsync` with the server-derived Account/authentication-session/principal reference and validate that same human before and after I/O. Logout and password rotation therefore retire an in-flight read even when tenant security has not changed. Trusted principal-only service reads retain their independent current IAM checks. This is a read boundary; bind/unbind transaction semantics remain as specified above. [Read freshness evidence](reports/tenant-credential-binding-read-verification-2026-10-17.md).

### Legacy bindings and cleanup protection

Unscoped `IAgentCredentialBindingStore` queries exclude every tenant-owned Instance, including archived identities, before returning rows or calculating public binding counts. Bind/unbind and bulk cleanup reject an owned target with `tenant_context_required` inside the existing Instance lock or SQLite transaction. Hidden binding rows still prevent deletion of their referenced Credential. These guards grant no secret-use authority. Canonical tenant management and binding configuration use the specialized ports above; Runtime/effect authority stays with the current typed owners; [Instance binding UI](reports/tenant-agent-connections-ui-verification-2026-10-17.md) and Credential management UI have separate evidence.

`IAgentInstanceStore.HasBackingInstanceAsync` answers only whether a retained backing Instance exists, including owned/archived rows hidden by legacy `FindAsync`. It authorizes no read or mutation. Physical workspace cleanup and InMemory deletion-receipt recovery use this protection; a filtered lookup returning null never establishes that an Instance was deleted. SQLite receipt recovery already checks actual backing rows.

## Tenant Instance configuration ports

`ITenantAgentStore.ReadTenantAgentConfigurationAsync` returns one coherent owned Instance/Skill/resource snapshot: shared locks in InMemory, one read transaction in SQLite. `TryChangeTenantAgentConfigurationAsync` accepts exactly one persona, publication, sparse-settings, execution-budget, unattended-model, self-management, Skill or resource change with applicable Instance/persona/publication witnesses. It rechecks current Account, tenant security revision and actual ownership, then commits revisions, inherited Skill initialization, observed Automation-policy CAS and audit atomically. Mixed commands and unrelated payload fields are rejected. No new configuration table is introduced. The old general parent-revision, active-version and persona/history write entry points reject owned Instances with `tenant_context_required`; global Instance find/list/maintenance omit owned roots and child read/write ports reject them too. Canonical snapshots and transactions use internal owner-aware backing reads/staging; remaining resource/runtime paths stay tracked by the full cutover.

`TenantAgentConfigurationService` requires scoped `agents.read` for parent inspection and read plus update for parent mutations. Child content/commands additionally use their separate typed permissions below. Definition inheritance is configuration exposed through authorized Instance inspection; it grants no IAM permission. Adoption of an owned publication additionally requires Definition read. Exact owned GUIDs never fall back to the global durable/builtin catalog. Deprecated publications remain readable for already pinned Instances but cannot be newly adopted. Existing settings, Skill, resource, model and Automation policy resolvers are reused. Sandbox enforcement follows the committed restriction through its existing reconciler; this checkpoint does not qualify tenant Sandbox execution. [Focused evidence](reports/tenant-agent-configuration-verification-2026-10-16.md) separates these ports from pending runtime admission.

The settings extension of `TenantAgentConfigurationService` exposes revisioned settings/default/source/constraint views, budgets and management inspection, sparse field patch/reset, bounded budget reset, configured unattended model/reset and management update/freeze. It reuses `InstanceSettingsRules` validation/view and `HarnessManagementRules` policy/freeze preparation, while keeping tenant lookup and IAM in the canonical service. Sparse settings carry a complete observed Automation reconciliation plan into the existing atomic commit. Reads recheck current authority after dependent work. Management rules derive agent versus human from the current security aggregate, never a caller-supplied flag. Policy changes/freeze do not widen IAM or mutate inert historical preparation bytes. Sandbox patching uses owned catalog/source authority without an unscoped fallback. [Settings verification](reports/tenant-instance-settings-verification-2026-10-16.md).

`TenantAgentConfigurationService.ReadSkillsAsync` returns a bounded page (30 default, 100 maximum), parent/security revisions and effective metadata/procedures under parent read plus item-scoped or inherited tenant `skills.read`, filtering before totals and pagination. `WriteSkillAsync` separately requires `skills.manage` on the existing item, expected security/Instance revisions and applicable child revision. It shares `InstanceSkillRules` pure preparation for create/update/delete, enabled choice/reset and atomic source-disable/customize. Built-in content is immutable; only the two existing enable choices may change, without enabling or unfreezing management.

Resource read/content/upsert/copy/choice/delete likewise require parent read and item-scoped or inherited tenant `resources.read`; changes additionally require `resources.manage`, or `resources.delete` for deletion. Actual owned manifest rows select private bytes; new bindings require bytes, while metadata-only edits require an existing local binding and preserve enabled state when omitted. Copy resolves an inherited binding and produces independent local identity/provenance. Shared preparation/resolution validates paths, media, quotas and Skill dependencies before the atomic child/parent/security/audit commit. Downloads verify hash/length, then recheck live read authority and parent revision after I/O. Effective configuration returns `ChildAccess` flags and redacts denied Skill/procedure and resource manifests/knowledge sources. Creation/customization/copy of a new child additionally require tenant-wide typed read/manage for the destination; an existing-item grant cannot create siblings. Item-scoped inspection omits unreadable dependencies and effective Skill/resource rows; full knowledge-source configuration remains redacted without tenant resource read. [Child verification](reports/tenant-instance-children-verification-2026-10-16.md).

`TenantAgentResourcePreparation` prepares the existing Resource mutations through those same resolvers. The Application collection validator is shared by preparation and both persistence adapters, so duplicate local paths and collection quotas reject before blob staging as well as at commit. Copy preparation verifies the selected current publication binding and its exact hash/length before retaining source provenance. Identical local content/metadata and repeated enabled/reset choices return current item, Instance and security revisions without staging bytes, changing timestamps or appending a mutation audit. They still require current typed read/manage, exact witnesses, active lifecycle and the original authenticated human; Agent commands additionally retain current Resources delegation. Inherited revision zero is valid before any choice, and an explicit enabled default differs from resetting inheritance. Dependency projections remain filtered. This preparation consolidation does not yet convert model Resource writes into canonical exact-consent operations.

Generic tenant security writes also recheck the current acting Principal/Account at commit, in addition to the security revision. Invitation acceptance is attributed to the new tenant Principal and separately fences the accepting Account and current inviter; it does not turn the new membership into an existing-authority bypass. Account suspension between grant preparation and commit rejects without a saved assignment or success audit.

### Instance child Resource scopes

`TenantAgentChildScopes` uses `resourceType` = `skill` or `resource` and canonical `resourceId` = `{instanceId:D}:{childKey}`. Child keys are the existing `builtin:…`, `definition:…` or `instance:…` Skill keys, and Definition/Instance GUID resource keys. No scope is inferred from an unqualified readable ID, path, blob hash or caller owner assertion. Both stores resolve the actual owning tenant/Instance, local rows and its current pinned owned publication or immutable shipped source/approved manifest; dormant inherited state is not a current target. Production identity composition supplies those shipped catalogs.

`IamResourceScopes.Create` and aggregate shape validation accept this canonical syntax; authorizer, access inspection, assignment and store commits still verify actual existence and ownership. Existing resource-type applicability and human-only rules remain unchanged. Child collections filter visible rows before counts/pages. Effective configuration adds `AllSkills`/`AllResources` to distinguish inherited tenant access from an item subset. Deletion and adoption compute the resulting actual child catalog, remove grants for removed targets and commit that removal with child/parent/security/audit; direct store writes retaining removed-target grants reject before mutation. Recreating a deleted readable key or restoring removed inherited content cannot revive retired assignments. [Focused evidence](reports/tenant-instance-child-iam-verification-2026-10-17.md).

### Definition binding Resource scopes

`TenantDefinitionResourceScopes` uses type `resource` and canonical IDs `{definitionId:D}:draft:{draftId:D}:{resourceId:D}` or `{definitionId:D}:publication:{positiveVersion}:{resourceId:D}`. Actual owning root, draft/publication and binding must all match; a hash, unqualified ID or copied Instance reference is insufficient. The same resource ID in a fork or publication is a different scope. Existing IAM parsing, aggregate shape and store ownership checks support these references without a registry or new table.

`ReadDraftStateAsync` returns a filtered `TenantDefinitionDraftState` view alongside `ResourceAccess` (`AllRead`, `CanCreate`, `CanEvaluate`, `CanPublish`, visible item scopes/manage/delete choices); the trusted persistence snapshot remains complete and separate. Binding/metadata writes require parent Definition read and resource read/manage on each existing item, or tenant read/manage for new items. Removal uses resource read/delete. Definition update alone grants neither file operation. Typed permissions are checked before stale witnesses or byte preparation; authorized stale commands still conflict. The bounded input collection is captured before awaits. Mutation resource projections filter unreadable rows too. Downloads resolve owned bytes and recheck exact grant/parent after I/O; changed draft revision returns `resource_changed`.

Publication resource state returns filtered `Items` and `AllRead`. `IamAuthorizer.VisibleResourcesAsync` is shared with Instance child projection; it evaluates current typed grants without hidden totals. Evaluation requires read on every captured file. Publishing or forking resource bindings and copying a shipped template require tenant-wide destination read/manage when files are present. `TemplateCatalogAsync` supplies shipped `Items` plus `CanFork` keyed by exact ID/version, retaining discovery for Definition creators while marking file-copy choices unavailable without destination authority. Definition access supplies publication `CanFork` eligibility.

Resource removal, draft consumption/deletion and aggregate Definition deletion retire corresponding scoped assignments with the existing security/draft/audit transaction; raw writes retaining retired grants reject before mutation. Immutable blobs remain retained. Root aggregate deletion retains its explicit Definition delete/read permission and complete existing child witnesses; it grants no child read response. [Observed evidence](reports/tenant-definition-resource-iam-verification-2026-10-17.md).

## Automation semantic authorization boundary

`ITriggerCommandAuthorizer.AuthorizeAsync(AutomationAuthorizationRequest, CancellationToken)` returns a typed `TriggerCommandAuthorizationVerdict`: Allow, Deny, Ambiguous, ClassifierUnavailable or ClassifierInvalidOutput with an optional safe failure reason. The request carries current trusted-user text, operation, tool, the exact prepared JSON/fingerprint and optional historical referent/draft and bounded conversation evidence. Implementations interpret semantics; Core gates runtime provenance and deterministic structure/policy. A tool call alone never grants mutation authority. The fail-closed default is unavailable, and Synthetic uses typed decision fixtures. Exact UI recovery reuses existing durable AgentRun approvals; no new persistence or provider DTO crosses the boundary. [Backend behavior](12-backend-implementation-spec.md#timeouts-and-retry-policy).

## Typed context projection

Application owns `ContextLayer`, `ContextSectionId`, `ContextSourceKind`, `ContextSemantics`, immutable `ContextSection`/`EffectiveContextSnapshot`, `ContextAssembler` and redacted `ContextManifest`. Core factories accept platform procedure identities, never arbitrary authored strings. Runtime factories reject Evidence sources/section identities. Evidence cannot acquire an authoritative provider role. The optional `RuntimeEnvironmentScope` is a future binding slot, absent from current execution.

`ContextPurpose.AgentExecution` is the default: projection requires nonempty effective Runtime `AgentIdentity` and `AgentInstructions`. Core-only compaction, completion evaluation and schedule classification explicitly select `Platform`. Purpose survives section updates and participates in the fingerprint; it does not grant tools or permissions.

`ILanguageModel.PrepareRequest` returns the request with any selected platform output protocol before its manifest is recorded; the default implementation is identity. Semantic adapters use the same assembler and Core section identity for forwarding.

`ModelRequest` carries its typed context and separate interaction transcript along with immutable projected messages. `EvidenceMessageKind` distinguishes current-task, historical and tool-interaction semantics while preserving actual user/assistant/tool roles. Reprojection preserves response IDs, image/tool parts and opaque continuation tokens. The manifest contains ordered section IDs, source kind/revision, per-field sources, character/UTF-8/token estimates, omission coverage and a content fingerprint; no raw bodies are included.

For admitted user Runs, `AgentContext.CurrentUserEntryIds` identifies the accepted source batch across attempts. Context construction uses the captured Session history and these identities, so a retained streaming response placeholder cannot turn the retry's current user task, explicit invocations or attachments into historical evidence.

Tool-interaction content remains untrusted Tool evidence even when its native transport role is `user`. A pending assistant tool-call batch permits only matching tool results before another user or assistant message. A transcript may end with pending calls for checkpoint/recovery projection.

Every tool-call ID is unique across the complete interaction, including completed batches; identical arguments do not permit reuse. SessionRuntime validates a newly proposed batch before checkpointing or admitting any tool effect. Resuming the existing pending batch retains its original IDs.

`ModelImageContent` takes one defensive copy into immutable bytes and caches its SHA-256 at construction. Reprojection shares these bytes. Fingerprints cover multipart text and image metadata/content hashes, without repeatedly serializing image payloads. Providers still encode the original bytes for their transport.

`ModelRole.Developer` projects explicitly in both Chat Completions and Responses. Catalog/model routes must declare `DeveloperRoleSupport` and use a supported transport; unsupported choices fail selection/admission or generation before HTTP. Pinned provider/model identity must still match that catalog declaration; changed or retired routes fail explicitly without rewriting the pin. Runtime is never downgraded to `system` or `user`. Configured support is a contract declaration, not evidence that an untested remote model honors it.

## Activation and AgentRun admission foundation

`Activation.Start` admits an independent root; continuation constructors require explicit root and parent Activation references. The Application factories derive them from the actual source Run. Both `IAgentRunStore` implementations validate the stored source owner, Session, Activation and inherited root before committing. Completion batches cannot cross roots. Fresh-ID same-input admission replay returns the originally saved root; retries/recovery keep that Activation. Missing serialized roots are rejected rather than inferred. These lineage checks do not grant tenant execution authority. [Verification](reports/activation-root-lineage-verification-2026-10-17.md).

`IAgentRunStore` admits a Session snapshot, accepted source entries, immutable Activation and initial queued AgentRun in one operation. It exposes no standalone Activation insert. SQLite shares `SqliteMemoryStore.StageSaveAsync` inside one transaction; InMemory shares the Session store's gate and execution state. `(SessionId, DedupeKey)`, background source receipts, unique accepted source-entry ownership and unique run-per-Activation identity prevent competing admissions. Replaying a background receipt returns the originally committed child/run even if the proposed child identifiers differ; changed source content or execution pins conflict.

Run and Activation reads are scoped by `AgentRunOwner`. `AgentRunCommand` is the shared transition vocabulary for claim, renewal, checkpoint, result, failure/retry, approval, cancellation, recovery, Skill/capability loading and effect state. Its revision and lease checks precede mutation; domain transitions additionally fence generation and exact action. Response completion must link to an already durable completed assistant entry in the same Session and response; NoAction has no invented entry. Immediate child admission requires an active owned user-turn source and matching Definition/persona/model pins. Execution capability authorization and background resource policy belong to the shared application admission and runtime services, not this persistence port.

`AgentRunAdmissionFactory.ForAcceptedUserBatch` freezes one ordered, accepted batch with stable input-derived dedupe, one response and pinned execution configuration. `AgentRunCoordinator` uses the same CAS claim/recovery path for direct admission dispatch and scheduler dispatch through `IAgentRunDispatcher`. The dispatcher contract requires delivery into the sole SessionRuntime mailbox and current eligibility checks. Expired approval resumes the same attempt; uncertain external effects do not replay. Unexpected dispatch failures retain their lease because mailbox delivery may already have occurred.

`AdmitOccurrenceAsync` commits the occurrence receipt and targeted Activation/AgentRun atomically. Its required pair is `ExecutionSessionId` and `AcceptedAgentRunId`; the Run link is unique, while multiple occurrences can share one existing Session. Background admission additionally creates the child graph. Existing-target admission checks current owner/lifecycle/snapshot under the same transaction or memory gate, preserves transcript and snapshot revision, and inserts zero source user entries. Replay returns the original accepted Run and Session.

Canonical `TenantAutomationService` and `TenantEventProcessor` admit the frozen `AutomationExecutionTarget` and `AutomationCompletionDelivery`, preserving instructions and source times through tenant-qualified owning transactions. Background targets create separate Sessions; existing targets require exact owned eligible metadata and current conversation/model witnesses. Current tenant executor, scheduling/source policy, Automation state and required Tools/Vision are checked before admission. No Profile-based intake or direct live-occurrence fallback remains; native physical-store receipt contracts retain historical data without granting execution authority.

Production conversation and detached execution use the registered `TenantSessionRuntimeHost`/`SessionRuntime` AgentRun path. `SessionRuntime` requires an `IAgentRunStore` adapter; the production `SessionRuntimeFactory` exposes only `CreateTenant` with canonical tenant storage/execution and required source services, and no user-turn, occurrence, capability or retry fallback bypasses admission. Durable accepted-input intent is repaired after restart; occurrence intake admits real targeted Sessions; the shared tool loop persists checkpoints and fences dispatch, and background.start returns a committed independent child. Atomic background outcomes and same-Session user continuation are exercised. Report-back, Session-first APIs/UI and approved legacy/schema retirement are implemented. The original background-session feature was closed/frozen on behavior `8cec78c5d47a43e0236a5c38f2e312f4e36ce283` (2026-10-08), with all five required hosted Synthetic/Compose jobs [green](https://github.com/trannamtrung1st/agent-core/actions/runs/37756244306). The [verification report](reports/activation-agent-run-background-sessions-verification.md) records local/runtime evidence and prior failed gates. P10/P11 remain unopened.

Physical Infrastructure paths use one `Persistence:WorkspaceRoot`: `agent-<instanceN>/home/` contains ordinary durable files and `agent-<instanceN>/sessions/session-<sessionN>/working/` contains ordinary Session-private files. The model sees only `/home` and `/working`. Artifacts, attachments, credentials and definition resources retain separate unmounted roots. The sibling `.provisioned` marker guards one-time template seeding outside the mounted working directory.

## Capability projection interfaces

Domain owns `CapabilityAuthorization` and `CapabilityProjectionPolicy`. Application `CapabilityAuthorizationResolver` materializes Selected/All grants as exact publication snapshots. `ToolDescriptor` supplies trusted category, bounded summary, tags, discoverability and projection class. `ToolProjectionService` produces ordinary `ModelToolDefinition[]`; `PromptContextBuilder.OfferTools` stays a thin caller. Provider ports remain unchanged.

`CapabilityUsageGuidance` renders a registry-derived on-demand guide bounded to 8192 characters, with eligible family counts, exact names and short descriptions. Only authorized, configured, execution-eligible discoverable interfaces absent from the current schemas appear; this guide loads no schemas or Skill procedures. A family overview and round-robin entries retain breadth when descriptions are clipped. `PromptContextBuilder` distinguishes the Definition-authorized count from schemas offered now. Owned Run continuations refresh this environment after capability/Skill projection and browser/dialog/cleanup/finalization restrictions, using the same phase filters as executable schemas. Models use offered tools directly, discover a missing interface before claiming it unavailable, and distinguish query failure, target/argument errors and current execution restrictions. Skills remain optional procedural help, not a prerequisite for tool use.

`capabilities.load` accepts only `{query,limit?}` (1–200 characters, default four / maximum eight total matches). Case-insensitive explicit registered names narrow the search; otherwise canonical name components, registry-owned feature synonyms, descriptions and explicit category scope rank deterministically, with ordinal name ties. Category-only requests can browse a bounded family; category words alone cannot satisfy an unrelated concrete action. Matching never widens authorization or eligibility.

The result contains `outcome`, `loaded` and `alreadyProjected` entries with `name`/concise `summary`, bounded `unavailable` entries with `name`/safe `reason`, and `nextStep`. Outcomes distinguish `load_matched`, `load_already_projected`, `load_no_match`, `load_unavailable`, `load_over_budget`, `load_invalid`, `load_stale`, `load_cancelled` and repeated-strategy refusal `load_strategy_blocked`. Only explicitly requested, Definition-authorized names can appear unavailable; unknown/unauthorized names produce no match without exposing restricted inventories. A loaded tool is offered on the next model request while its current eligibility and existing approvals remain authoritative. Already offered tools should be called directly. Near the cumulative output bound, a valid compact receipt retains outcome/next-step with `truncated=true`; descriptions and trailing entries may be omitted, and next-request schemas remain the executable interface.

Eight admitted load invocations bound one Run; no-match/already/unavailable admissions consume this budget. Identical ineffective discovery uses the existing checkpointed invalid-strategy circuit: two attempts may run, further equivalent calls are refused, while corrected valid calls and unrelated offered tools remain executable within the existing Run budgets, including independently requested cleanup. Valid query fingerprints ignore case/whitespace. No-match/unavailable/budget fingerprints also ignore limit changes; already-projected results retain the limit so a wider search can reveal missing tools. Corrected invalid arguments and different goals remain permitted. Loading changes projection, not authority or the projected-schema ceiling. Skills supply procedures through their existing loader; they do not grant executable capabilities.

Mailbox-admitted `IAgentRunStore` commands atomically guards revision, claim generation, Running status and cancellation; Domain carries `LoadedCapabilityIds` and `CapabilityLoadCount` through recovery transitions. No loaded IDs enter mutable Agent Instance preferences or provider DTOs. AgentRuns reuse their bounded checkpoint and claim guards.

Native live receipts pin their target Session and settle quiet evaluation without a Run. Speaking evaluation atomically links an Activation/AgentRun. Loads use the same persisted run state and current authority checks.


## Managed workspace refinement ports

`ISessionWorkspace.TryWriteNewAsync` exclusively publishes a complete new file and returns false for any occupied name, including a directory or symlink. Attachment materialization uses sanitized names with at most 128 atomic attempts, independent of directory pagination. Ordinary `WriteAsync` retains intentional replacement semantics.

Search consumes `ListPageAsync` / `ListNodePageAsync` lexical cursors within its result, file, entry and depth budgets. Missing/unreadable paths, exhausted budgets and incomplete pages set `truncated`; concurrent filesystem changes do not imply snapshot coverage.

`WorkspaceTemplatePolicy` contains only an optional TemplateId. `ToolExecutionAdmission.WorkspaceCwd` carries the mailbox snapshot; `ToolExecutionResult.WorkspaceCwd` proposes a typed metadata effect applied only by the fenced Session mailbox. `workspace.cwd` validates a directory directly rather than searching a clipped parent listing.

The global `AgentInstanceWorkspaceService` facade is retired. Current protected reads derive actual home/working metadata and current principal/source scope through `TenantWorkspaceReadService`/`TenantWorkspaceRuntimeReadService`; writes use `TenantWorkspaceMutationService` and the existing durable effect boundary. Original human, claimed Run/generation, current policy and actual source/target witnesses are checked at dispatch and output. Native `IAgentInstanceWorkspaceStore`/`ISessionWorkspace` mechanisms retain descriptor-relative byte operations without actor authority.

These C# 14 signatures are the implementation contract, not source files. Ports and normalized provider records live in Application; definition/conversation records live in Domain as specified in [Backend Implementation](12-backend-implementation-spec.md). Use BCL `System`, `System.Collections.Generic`, `System.Threading`, and `System.Threading.Tasks` namespaces. Collections passed to workers must be immutable snapshots, even where typed as `IReadOnlyList<T>`.

## Portable capabilities and concrete configurations

ILanguageModel, ISpeechRecognizer and ISpeechSynthesizer are independent portable capabilities. A conversational agent always requires a language-model alias. Speech recognizer and synthesizer aliases are required only when that Agent Definition has `Voice.Enabled`. MVP voice orchestration composes all three ports; text-only agents compose ILanguageModel alone. No IAIProvider, OpenRouter-specific port or native-audio reasoning dependency is introduced. Each agent selects logical provider aliases, allowing different text models by identity. Infrastructure resolves those aliases to hosted, hybrid or local configurations. The recommended hosted text alias resolves to OpenAICompatibleLanguageModel configured for OpenRouter; the same adapter can target direct OpenAI or a compatible local server. The recommended future hosted STT adapter is OpenAiSpeechRecognizer over an OpenAI **realtime transcription** session (speech-to-text only; not native speech-to-speech reasoning), with configurable model and default recommendation `gpt-live-transcribe`. Until that session is implemented, the selectable hosted STT adapter is `OpenAICompatibleBatch`. See [Configuration](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#hosted-and-on-prem-provider-configurations) and [OpenAI realtime STT](12-backend-implementation-spec.md#openai-realtime-transcription-adapter).

### Speech provider replacement rule

Changing the STT, LLM or TTS provider must not require changes to Agent Runtime or Interaction Controller. Provider selection uses application configuration and dependency injection; concrete adapters belong in Infrastructure. Observed selectable hosted speech is OpenAI TTS plus `OpenAICompatibleBatch` STT. OpenAI realtime transcription remains a planned adapter, not a selectable runtime path, while its live session is a no-op.

```text
ISpeechRecognizer                         ISpeechSynthesizer
├── OpenAICompatibleBatchSpeechRecognizer ├── OpenAiSpeechSynthesizer
├── SyntheticSpeechRecognizer             ├── SyntheticSpeechSynthesizer
├── OpenAiSpeechRecognizer (unselectable) ├── LocalSpeechSynthesizer (future)
├── LocalSpeechRecognizer (future)        └── FutureHostedSpeechSynthesizer
└── FutureHostedSpeechRecognizer
```

Browser is not a leaf of those ports. Selecting Adapter=`Browser` maps to client transports (`clientTranscript` / `clientSpeech`) with no backend recognizer or synthesizer. Do not register a stand-in `ISpeechRecognizer` that pretends to be the Web Speech API. An Infrastructure `SpeechFactory` (same responsibility as `LanguageModelFactory`) resolves an `EffectiveSpeechPlan` plus optional ports. Browser transports still carry explicit **effective client capabilities** on that plan (`PartialTranscripts=true` and `SpeechBoundaryEvents=true` for `clientTranscript`; `Cancellation`, `VoiceSelection` and `SpeakingRate` for `clientSpeech`; `StreamingAudio=false` because no backend PCM port exists). Session Runtime uses those plan capabilities for interruption timing and `session.ready`; it must not treat a missing backend adapter as all-false capabilities. Selected non-Synthetic adapters must not silently become Synthetic. `OpenAiSpeechSynthesizer` is selectable when Synthesis Adapter=`OpenAI` and a backend API key is structurally present. `OpenAICompatibleBatch` is the supported hosted STT adapter when Recognition Adapter=`OpenAICompatibleBatch` and a backend API key is present; it reports no streaming input and no interim partials. `OpenAiSpeechRecognizer` remains unimplemented as a live session and is not a selectable runtime adapter.

Future names illustrate replaceable implementations, not mandatory projects/providers. Adapters normalize vendor behavior and payloads; no OpenAI request/response type crosses into Domain, Application, Agent Runtime, Interaction Controller or wire contracts. Capability differences select the existing [fallback policies](05-interaction-controller.md#stt-capability-fallback-and-local-ducking), not a runtime rewrite. STT `SpeechPartial`/`SpeechFinal` Confidence remains optional (null when unavailable); streaming input, partials, boundaries and cancellation must be reported honestly as **effective adapter capabilities**, never as operator-invented flags.

SyntheticSpeechRecognizer, SyntheticSpeechSynthesizer and ScriptedLanguageModel remain mandatory for offline unit/conversation tests, frontend development, CI, latency simulation, cancellation and interruption tests. They require no API keys. [Configuration](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#provider-selection-and-di) owns adapter selection examples.

## Language model and failures

Language-model Timeout failures use bounded `FailureReason` values `setupTimeout` (before response headers), `streamIdle`, or `totalTimeout`. Unclassified Timeout remains terminal. Caller cancellation uses Cancelled. [Backend timeout policy](12-backend-implementation-spec.md#timeouts-and-retry-policy) owns the durable AgentRun retry fences.

```csharp
public enum ProviderErrorCode
{
    Authentication, RateLimited, Timeout, Cancelled, InvalidRequest,
    InvalidResponse, Unavailable, UnsupportedCapability, Unknown
}
public sealed record ProviderFailure(
    ProviderErrorCode Code,
    string SafeMessage,
    TimeSpan? RetryAfter = null,
    string? FailureReason = null,
    string? ResponseChannel = null);
public enum ModelRole { System, User, Assistant, Tool, Developer }
public enum ModelStopReason { Completed, LengthLimit, ContentFiltered, ToolCalls }
public sealed record ModelToolCall(string Id, string Name, string ArgumentsJson);
public sealed record ModelToolDefinition(string Name, string Description, string ParametersJson);
public sealed record ModelMessage(
    ModelRole Role, string Text,
    IReadOnlyList<ModelContentPart>? Parts = null,
    string? ToolCallId = null, string? Name = null,
    IReadOnlyList<ModelToolCall>? ToolCalls = null);
public sealed record ModelCapabilities(
    bool StreamingText, bool Cancellation, bool Vision = false, bool Tools = false,
    bool StructuredOutput = false);
public sealed record ModelResponseContract(bool SpeechWillBeUsed);
public sealed record ModelSemanticResponse(
    string DisplayText, ModelSpeechProjection Speech, IReadOnlyList<ModelResponseBlock> Blocks);
public sealed record ModelRequest(
    Guid ResponseId, IReadOnlyList<ModelMessage> Messages,
    int MaxOutputTokens = 512, double? Temperature = null,
    IReadOnlyList<ModelToolDefinition>? Tools = null,
    string? ReasoningEffort = null,
    ModelResponseContract? ResponseContract = null);
public abstract record ModelGenerationEvent;
public sealed record ModelTextDelta(string Text) : ModelGenerationEvent;
public sealed record ModelDisplayDelta(string Text) : ModelGenerationEvent;
public sealed record ModelSemanticResponseReady(ModelSemanticResponse Response) : ModelGenerationEvent;
public sealed record ModelReasoningDelta(string Text) : ModelGenerationEvent;
public sealed record ModelToolCallEvent(ModelToolCall Call) : ModelGenerationEvent;
public sealed record ModelCompleted(ModelStopReason Reason,
    int? InputTokens = null, int? OutputTokens = null) : ModelGenerationEvent;
public sealed record ModelFailed(ProviderFailure Failure) : ModelGenerationEvent;
public interface ILanguageModel
{
    ModelCapabilities Capabilities { get; }
    IAsyncEnumerable<ModelGenerationEvent> GenerateAsync(
        ModelRequest request, CancellationToken cancellationToken = default);
}
```

Exactly one terminal Completed/Failed event per successful enumeration, followed by EOF. Caller cancellation may throw `OperationCanceledException` instead. Unexpected exceptions are normalized at the supervised application boundary; provider exception details never go to Contracts. Empty EOF is `Unavailable`, never implicit success. Model events have request-scoped identity; the pump adds ResponseId from its captured request, never from current mutable state. `InvalidResponse` is a distinct provider failure for malformed or unusable assistant output after the request was accepted; it is not `InvalidRequest` (the provider rejected the request). Optional `ModelRequest.ResponseContract` asks for provider-neutral semantic events (`ModelDisplayDelta`, `ModelSemanticResponseReady`); a null contract keeps `ModelTextDelta`. Resolved language models are wrapped by an Infrastructure semantic-response decorator: native completed JSON is validated when `StructuredOutput` is true, otherwise one bounded compatibility instruction and marker parser produce the same events. Raw JSON and marker fragments are never display or speech. Application types do not carry `response_format` or `json_schema`. Unsupported requested capabilities fail before making a request.

The Language Model reasons over normalized text messages only. ModelRequest has no vendor model ID. A trusted `IModelCatalog` exposes operator-configured descriptors (catalog key, display name, trusted provider alias, concrete model ID, capabilities including `StructuredOutput`, supported reasoning-effort values). `ILanguageModelResolver.Resolve(SessionModelSelection, ModelPurpose)` captures an immutable client for Conversation, Initiative, or CompletionEvaluation from the persisted session selection and copies trusted catalog transport, developer-role support, `Tools`, `Vision`, and `StructuredOutput` onto cloned provider options and resolved `ModelCapabilities`. These three purposes use the session-selected model. Platform schedule classification uses `ModelPurpose.TriggerAuthorization` with the operator catalog default and the least expensive recognized effort it supports (falling back to its validated default), independently of the conversation's selection. It uses the same resolver so Core/system and Runtime/developer remain separate on the declared transport. Compaction reuses `ModelPurpose.Conversation`; it is not a separate provider port. Do not mutate singleton `LanguageModelProviderOptions` when a session changes model. Browser input never supplies provider alias, BaseUrl, ApiKey, or arbitrary provider JSON. Hidden provider reasoning fields are not spoken content and never become ModelTextDelta. Responses maps only an explicit max_output_tokens incomplete reason with valid incomplete status and text/reasoning output on a no-tools request to ModelCompleted(LengthLimit), carrying reported usage. Unknown/malformed incomplete events and partial or unsolicited tool channels remain failures; truncated tool calls never execute. See [Technology Decisions](10-technology-decisions.md#decision-session-model-selection-and-inference-controls).

## Terminal Agent Step

After Infrastructure emits `ModelSemanticResponse`, Application `AgentStepNormalizer` validates one terminal Agent Step before Chat delivery. The step is an Agent Core contract, not a provider DTO. `IAgentBrain` / `AgentDecision` (`StaySilent`, `Speak`, `RequestDeactivate`) stays the pre-generation decision and is not this step.

```csharp
public enum AgentStepDisposition { Continue, Wait, Complete, Blocked }
public abstract record AgentAction;
public sealed record ChatRespondAction(
    string DisplayText, ModelSpeechProjection Speech, IReadOnlyList<ModelResponseBlock> Blocks) : AgentAction;
public sealed record AgentStep(
    AgentStepDisposition Disposition,
    IReadOnlyList<AgentAction> Actions,
    IReadOnlyList<MemoryProposal> MemoryProposals);
```

The strict structured-output schema and the `agent_core_respond` response function both require `disposition` (`Continue`, `Wait`, `Complete`, `Blocked`) and `action` (`{"kind":"chat.respond"}` or null), plus display, speech, blocks, and memory. `additionalProperties` stays false. The strict schema uses singular `action`. The parser still accepts one matching `actions` item so a non-strict payload is not dropped, and that array is not part of the strict schema. An omitted disposition and an omitted action, including the plain-text compatibility channel, normalize to `Complete` and one `chat.respond`. An explicit null action does not invent `chat.respond`. Memory proposals are copied beside actions and are not actions. `chat.respond` is not a `ToolRegistry` entry and is not a fake `agentcore.wait`, `agentcore.complete`, or `agentcore.continue` tool. The action carries no session, profile, tenant, or recipient. A model-supplied `sessionId`, `destination`, `profileId`, `tenant`, or `recipient` rejects the whole step. Unknown disposition, unknown action, and malformed payload also reject the whole step as `ProviderErrorCode.InvalidResponse`, execute nothing, admit no memory, and use the existing diagnostic id path. `chat.respond` with empty or whitespace-only `displayText` is `InvalidResponse` with `missingDisplayText`; the runtime does not invent display text. When `SpeechWillBeUsed` is false, a missing or unusable speech object canonicalizes to `same` with null text, redundant `same` text is ignored, and a well-formed `none` or non-empty `custom` projection is kept. Voice turns ignore text on `same` and `none`, reject empty `custom` text as `missingCustomSpeechText`, and reject an unknown mode as `invalidSpeechMode`. Those reasons name the structure only. The strict schema and `agent_core_respond` instructions tell models that ordinary user answers use `Complete` plus non-empty `displayText`, and that `Wait` is only for real external waits. Hidden reasoning is not a step field.

`AgentStepController` chooses the effect and does not itself mutate Session lifecycle, AgentRun, approval, trigger registration, or memory. `Complete` or `Continue` with one Chat action delivers that action once. `Continue` has that same Chat effect and does not start another model generation. Further model work in the same user turn is the existing tool pump, including `app.message.send` and `skills.load`. Zero Chat actions return control with no Chat effect and no memory admission. `Wait`, and `Complete` or `Continue` with no Chat action, return control without delivering Chat, staging memory, or completing the assistant successfully. That is not an interruption: `Interrupted` remains reserved for preemptive stops such as user steer, user stop, supersession, and lifecycle cancellation. For allowed no-chat returns on non-user triggers, Session Runtime removes the in-flight assistant placeholder instead of persisting an empty interrupted row, and `ResponseCompletedOutput` omits `interruptReason`. The bound AgentRun commits its quiet outcome with the same placeholder removal.
