Default read-only floors permit authorized scheduled/manual Automation research without an unnecessary review. Hard explicit source-read policies remain effective.

The closed search contract accepts a 1–600-character query and optional result limit 1–10; fetch accepts only one public HTTP(S) URL with no embedded credentials or caller headers/authority fields. Results are bounded untrusted data. Completed observations retain an argument-bound `readCommandHash` in the existing tool checkpoint; they are reused without refetch only under fresh source authority. Inert unowned or changed observations cannot become model context. Failed calls retain bounded Core error/recovery metadata rather than provider exception bodies. Native DNS/redirect enforcement remains with the existing public-network adapter. The [read cutover report](reports/tenant-public-web-read-verification-2026-10-23.md) owns scoped runtime evidence. The [generic HTTP contract](#canonical-generic-public-http-contract) defines its separate exact-operation approval and dispatch boundary.

### Canonical generic public HTTP contract

The preserved `http.request` escape hatch uses explicit agent-scoped `http.request` IAM authority with the Sensitive classification and mandatory exact review for every method. Missing otherwise delegatable authority may be supplied only by the existing exact exceptional-grant path. This adds one deliberate catalog entry; it does not infer an IAM permission from capability allowlists. Existing catalog entries keep their classifications. The human-only Owner role follows the complete tenant catalog through the guarded upgrade; existing operational and Contributor roles gain no automatic HTTP permission.

One typed prepared operation binds the actual Session, normalized method/public URL/allowlisted headers/exact UTF-8 body and digest, plus existing Instance/persona witnesses. `approvals.request_authorization` uses `operation=send_http_request` with the exact `request` arguments. Raw Agent/Session reads remain independent; current compound reviewer authority and independent-review controls apply. The shared public-web target supplies source checks without reading private credentials or browser state. The native `IHttpRequestClient` retains public-network policy, bounded text, no model credentials/cookies, GET/HEAD-only redirects and no non-idempotent retries.

The existing Run/approval/grant/receipt owners reserve dispatch before I/O. At most one native request consumes one exact operation; uncertain outcomes, cancellation after dispatch and recovery never replay it. Factual receipts retain bounded response metadata/digests, while an authorized live continuation may retain bounded untrusted response text in the existing checkpoint. Response status is an HTTP observation, not proof that a remote business operation succeeded. Current source/reviewer authority is rechecked before dispatch, result release, reentry and atomic output. Completed facts survive later revocation without releasing unreadable bodies. Approval previews render exact URL, headers and literal body as untrusted text. This paragraph is the required implementation contract; qualification remains open until the complete module and runtime checks finish.

Observed public web ports: Application `IWebSearchProvider`, `IPublicWebFetcher`, and `IHttpRequestClient` with normalized bounded results. Infrastructure `PublicWebFetcher` and `HttpRequestClient` share `SocketsPublicWebTransport`: DNS is resolved per connect attempt, private/local/link-local/metadata/machine-local targets (including IPv4-mapped private answers) are denied, each remaining permitted address is tried until one connects, redirects/cookies/auto-decompression are disabled, policy denials map to bounded `forbidden_host`, and connection failures map to `transport_error`. `web.fetch` is GET-only. `http.request` allows GET, HEAD, POST, PUT, PATCH, and DELETE; only GET and HEAD follow redirects, and non-idempotent methods are not retried. Textual bodies decode as bounded UTF-8 for `http.request`. Provider DTOs and raw page-reading bytes never cross into Application; normalized download bytes cross only the dedicated file result and are committed to the owned Workspace, never projected to the model. Profile=Synthetic registers `SyntheticWebSearchProvider`; Profile=Real registers `BraveWebSearchProvider`, which stays unavailable until `BRAVE_SEARCH_API_KEY` or `BraveSearch:ApiKey` is present, and `web.search` is then hidden by the configuration gate. That absence is expected, not a missing tool implementation.

Observed email port: Application `IEmailProvider` is produced per admitted tenant account through `ITenantConnectedAccountTransportFactory`; production uses Synthetic, tenant-authorized Gmail REST or verified-TLS Custom IMAP/SMTP and protected typed Credential profiles. The global factory/account/OAuth/watch authority services are retired; host tool readiness reads `EmailTransportOptions` without resolving an account owner. Core owns durable drafts and exact account/content approvals, plus an immutable-draft dispatch fence. Gmail REST has offline MIME/Bcc/HTTP contract tests and never silently supplies a global account. See [account-scoped Email ports](#account-scoped-email-ports) for the current boundary.


Observed rich envelope: parent `ResponseId` owns validated `displayText` (stored as entry `Text`), optional public `speechText` only when `speech.mode` is `custom` and it differs from display (same-mode derived playback coordinates are internal and never projected as public `speechText`), Markdown/attachment/artifact/unknown blocks, optional controller `memoryReceipts` persisted in envelope JSON apart from display and speech text, independent display vs speech-coordinate receipts, attachment authorization from bound conversation attachment ids plus `fixture-attachment-1`, and fixture-only artifact authorization (`fixture-artifact-1`). Unknown and unauthorized attachment/artifact refs persist a safe fallback without leaking the id. Legacy rows that stored marker-era speech still project through the same public `speechText` field.

Quota numbers: [resource table](10-technology-decisions.md#planned-resource-limits).

## P9.7 semantic Authoring contracts

Optional `HarnessManagementState` on `AgentInstance` defaults to Disabled. Policy includes independent scopes, mode, frozen state and a derived eligible configured-tool set. Legacy explicit source/eligible fields remain compatible for advanced owner candidate APIs; neither is required in normal Chat. Eligibility derives from previously authorized instance selections and currently configured registered ordinary tools, never from model proposals. This is a deliberate P9.7 limitation: Chat cannot propose a tool solely because it exists in the server catalog. A broader server-owned configured, safe-to-propose catalog remains future work; actual selection would still need owner approval. The default policy grants no scopes, so instruction replacement is unavailable until the owner explicitly enables its scope.

Contextual `HarnessChatContext` carries policy revision and active version. `ToolExecutionAdmission` carries execution kind, attached instance, model tools support, execution-local source receipts and current owner turn text. Fixed tools are `harness.inspect`, `harness.knowledge.upsert/remove`, `harness.instructions.update`, `harness.tool.select/configure`. They use `HarnessAuthority`, separate from Definition permissions, and non-replayable mutation admission. Semantic arguments carry expectedVersion/policyRevision and the change; all mutations also require expected/observed assessment. The server binds operation kind and current candidate revision. No operation can change authority, credentials, model/provider configuration or install executable plugins. Skill requirements never grant capabilities.

The standalone candidate/refinement executor is retired. Canonical tenant Harness commands use the configuration planners and protected owner described in [Unified self-management application owners](#unified-self-management-application-owners). Exact source evidence, independent delegation, consent and partial assessment remain required; inert historical preparations cannot execute.

Publication validation permits a fork to retain registered, configuration-gated tools already present in its published source, even when the current host cannot configure them. Runtime offering and execution still require current host configuration. A new unconfigured tool grant remains a blocking publication finding. This lets a knowledge change proceed from an existing published General Assistant version without broadening tool authority.

Runtime exact-version resolution, harness fork-source selection and knowledge readback use one built-in-first source resolver. A same-version durable publication cannot silently replace the built-in baseline or its knowledge; a durable version without a built-in collision remains the source of its own resources.

## P9.8-P9.9 continuity ports

Retrospective Session projection reconstructs only Core-known successful browser-close and email-send receipts with constant safe labels, drops unknown/failed receipts, and caps projected receipts at 20 per entry. Persisted labels never enter model input. Experience recall is delimited JSON historical data beneath an explicit System trust boundary; it cannot grant tools, weaken approvals or override the current task. Harness guidance names the actual execution origin and its pinned Definition.

`IExperienceStore` owns per-instance enabled/revision settings, idempotent source admission, owned bounded listing/lookup, pending request recovery, immutable completion and revisioned visibility/reset. InMemory and SQLite implement the same contract. Optional `AdminEventAppend` on settings/visibility/reset commits safe history metadata with the mutation. Source history remains owned by `IMemoryStore`; canonical learned-Memory admission uses `ITenantLearnedMemoryStore` inside the owning response transaction.

`ILanguageModel` is the sole provider-neutral generation port for ordinary live and durable tool loops. Manual Experience review uses the generic durable loop, semantic inspection/recording and work.complete; no special generation method or restricted synthesis runtime remains.

Response-function guidance uses `agent_core_respond` with structured `blocks`, `speech`, and `memory`; an Artifact reference is an `artifactReference` block using the successful Session Artifact tool result. It never advertises compatibility markers or calls the function path a plain-text channel. Structured `displayText` is ordinary text, not marker-parsed. Marker compatibility guidance remains separate, with `MarkerSemanticResponseParser` as its only marker interpreter; no UI marker parsing is introduced. Workspace paths are not Artifact IDs and the existing unauthorized-reference fallback remains.

The OpenAI-compatible adapter maps returned function names using the request's offered contracts as well as registered tools. `experience.source` and `experience.record` are registered executable semantic tools; their offering and execution require the current Experience authority and owned Run context. When the assistant response function is offered, the HTTP adapter buffers bounded provider prose until the finish reason is known: a tool-call round discards that preamble, while a plain-text completion preserves the compatibility fallback. The validated response function supplies the answer once. Ordinary text and Synthetic streaming retain their existing incremental behavior.

Native `ITriggerStore.SaveAutomationAsync` retains historical revision, provenance, capacity and history semantics. Current human/model authoring and occurrence admission use canonical tenant Automation transactions. No global scheduler/router or Profile-based intake is registered; `TenantRunExecution` and `TenantSessionRuntimeHost` dispatch admitted work through the same bounded `SessionRuntime`.

## Continuity retrieval and owner schedule ports

Canonical continuity reads use `ITenantContinuityStore` and `TenantContinuityReadService` under the actual tenant Run claim. The [historical continuity boundary](#canonical-historical-continuity-read-boundary) owns current source eligibility, exact windows and independent read Review. `ContinuityRelevance` shares only pure lexical ranking; it has no store dependencies. The former Profile-based search/get/automatic-context service and its model dispatch are retired. Caller-supplied Instance, Session, Run and Harness context cannot select a private source.

Canonical manual Experience review commits its exact source witness and background Run graph atomically through `TenantExperienceReviewService`. The old Profile-based request/admission/repair loop is retired; no automatic semantic review cadence or replacement scheduler is introduced.

Canonical human and model authoring share `TenantAutomationAuthoring` through `TenantAutomationService` and `TenantAutomationMutationService`. Original provenance remains immutable. A current human request or actual admitted task must align with the exact proposed mutation; independent current IAM and required consent still govern execution. Existing sources are tenant-qualified and independently readable. `automation.run` requires an actual current human request. The global Admin Automation authoring helper and its local-Profile admission path are retired. [Executor retirement and clarification qualification](reports/automation-executor-retirement-verification-2026-10-23.md).

Canonical search preserves complete bounded records and their source identity/provenance. Incomplete inspection never authorizes consolidation. `IdentityMaintenanceContract` retains the bounded unique-source parser and model guidance only; it is not a registered authority or effect owner. Guidance asks an Automation to finish after one confirmed narrow change and to preserve different integration qualifiers.

## P9.10 identity maintenance ports

`IStructuredMemoryStore.ConsolidateAsync(sources, result)` atomically supersedes two to eight unique unchanged Active sources and inserts one Active replacement. The owner, exact scope and kind must match. Filtered subject uniqueness applies to the replacement after supersession, within the same transaction. An unrelated subject conflict rolls back all changes. `MemoryProvenance.DerivedFromMemoryIds` holds exact sorted immediate parents; `MaintenanceOrigin` records UserTurn, Schedule, ApplicationEvent or ManualInvocation. Optional Core-owned `MaintenanceAgentInstanceId`, `MaintenanceSessionId` and `MaintenanceAgentRunId` identify the initiating execution independently of semantic ownership, including shared User Memory. These fields supplement, rather than overload, promotion's singular origin fields.

`IExperienceStore.ConsolidateAsync` similarly requires completed Eligible observations, same instance/profile, exact sorted lineage and unchanged revisions. It supersedes sources and creates a separate Consolidation observation. `MaintenanceSettingsAsync` and `ConfigureMaintenanceAsync` own the default-off, revisioned `IdentityMaintenanceSettings`; configuration uses CAS and the existing Admin audit transaction. No generic mutation port or Experience-to-Memory promotion is added.

Canonical Memory and Experience operations use their owning tenant services and the incumbent exact approval/Run transactions; the former `IdentityMaintenanceService` is retired. `SessionToolExecutor` dispatches these families only through `TenantToolAuthorization`, with no generic executor fallback. The [protected Memory contract](#protected-model-learned-memory-operations) and [Experience boundary](#canonical-experience-ownership-and-source-intent) own current consent requirements. `AgentContext.AllowAgentConsolidation` comes from `ITenantRunContext.CurrentContinuitySettingsAsync` for both initial projection and capability loading; it is eligibility context, never permission or consent. Private Experience and continuity evidence enter canonical guarded projection, never an unowned context callback.

### Semantic Experience source and record ports

`TenantExperienceToolContract` owns the canonical registered/offered source, record and consolidation schemas and descriptions. `TenantExperienceOperationService` and the owning stores bind actual tenant Run identity, independently readable sources, observed cursors, current settings and applicable exact consent. `TenantExperienceSourceProjection.StableSessionCheckpoint` retains only the shared pure checkpoint selection logic; it selects no owner and grants no authority. The global source/record/recall/admission service is retired. See the [canonical Experience boundary](#canonical-experience-ownership-and-source-intent).

## Agent Instance workspace contracts

`IAgentInstanceWorkspaceStore` exposes path-based List, FindFiles, Inspect, Read, WriteFile, Delete, Structure and lifecycle purge. `AgentWorkspaceItem` is descriptive metadata: owner, logical path, MIME type, byte size, modification time, directory/kind, link target and mode. `AgentWorkspacePage` returns bounded immediate children, a lexical continuation path and sampled/unknown aggregate usage. FindFiles performs bounded current metadata discovery for the composer. No stable file ID, file revision, content SHA or provenance database remains.

`ISessionWorkspace` retains virtual `/agent` and `/attachments` resources and adds path-based physical working operations. `WorkspaceNodePage` exposes entries/nextPath/truncated; listings are observations, not snapshots. `RequireDirectoryAsync` validates cwd directly. `IArtifactStore` remains separate and immutable. Lifecycle purge uses committed deletion receipts and waits for confirmed sandbox retirement before removing only the owner directory.

## Workspace structure ports

Both physical adapters accept `WorkspaceStructuralOperation` (`op`, `path`, `source`, `destination`, `recursive`) and return ordered `WorkspaceStructureResult`. Input syntax, scope and protected roots are checked before effects. Filesystem conflicts and cumulative budget observations are checked during execution; an earlier operation can affect a later one. Normal case behavior follows the qualified host filesystem. Names and depth are not constrained by the retired snapshot planner.

Results expose completion/requested/completed counts, failed index, normalized operations and bounded per-operation summaries. Failure marks remaining operations notExecuted and may set mutationsMayHaveOccurred. Counts describe addressed nodes, not a complete concurrent subtree snapshot. Deleting a link unlinks the link; copying a safe link preserves it; recursion does not follow links. Cross-root copy is explicit; move and batch remain single-scope. No port accepts a host root or caller-selected owner.

## Unified harness inspection

`InspectChatAsync(instanceId, currentRunDefinitionVersion, ct)` reports `activeDefinitionVersion`, `policyRevision`, `authoringEligibleTools`, `activeDefinitionAuthorizedCapabilities`, `currentRunDefinitionVersion`, `instanceRevision` and `changesApplyToNextRun:true`. Authoring eligibility concerns future Definitions, never current execution authority. Authorized capability names are distinct from the contextual turn projection. Inspection clips instruction text to 1024 UTF-8 bytes with `instructionsTruncated`; full owner review remains available in Admin. Admitted Runs retain their immutable configuration after publication or adoption; each new Run resolves the Instance-selected active version, including in an existing Session.

Canonical `ToolRegistry` model definitions supply both discovery summaries and execution schemas. `capabilities.load` must describe the same interface execution exposes. Copy describes exact file/tree transfers between home and working; move describes file/tree rename.


## Agent Instance Skill management ports

`TenantAgentConfigurationService` and the canonical management operation services own tenant-authorized `skills.list`, `skills.inspect`, `skills.create`, `skills.update`, `skills.set_enabled`, `skills.delete` and `skills.customize`. Agent administrative access requires current typed IAM, operation consent where applicable and the effective self-management Skills area. Ordinary `skills.load` remains independently authorized; Human authoring uses the same tenant transaction with current human authority. Shared `InstanceSkillRules` prepares and projects values without reading stores or authorizing effects. Trusted execution supplies the owned instance and pinned Definition; models cannot submit an owner. List returns bounded metadata without procedures; inspect reads current durable content, including after a write, while execution projection remains pinned. Revisions fence state/local writes. Archived owners are read-only. Create accepts an optional readable `id`; omission or an empty value generates a name-based ID with an owner-local numeric collision suffix. IDs are unique within each Agent Instance, can be reused by other instances, and remain fixed during update. Create/update validate bounded procedural content and authorized registry requirements. Definition content cannot be updated/deleted from an instance. Customize atomically creates an independent local copy with source provenance and disables the source; Definition resource bindings reject customization rather than being dropped. Customize returns `{instanceSkill, definitionSkill:{key,enabled:false,revision}}` from the same committed mutation, so both resulting states are immediately observable without another read.

`IAgentInstanceStore` initializes Definition Skill state on creation and version change in the same transaction/lock as the owner revision and optional Admin history. For unowned legacy Instances, `ReadSkillsAsync` reads both collections and `MutateSkillsAsync` commits state/local changes, owner revision and optional safe history together. Matching stable ids preserve explicit enabled choices, newly encountered ids inherit current Definition defaults, removed ids remain dormant and local Skills are untouched. Version transitions also enforce the combined Always procedure budget. SQLite and InMemory have the same CAS, rollback and deletion semantics.

When an admitted Run remains pinned after an Instance upgrade, management authorization and explicit copy content use that trusted execution Definition. A new Run in the same Session resolves current Instance configuration. Combined enabled Always budget validation uses the owner's current active Definition because writes affect future executions; the owner revision fence prevents a concurrent version change from bypassing the check.

## Automation destination and delivery ports

Domain validates BackgroundSession with no target ID, or ExistingSession with one nonempty ID. CompletionDelivery is None or ToSession; ExistingSession requires None. `ITriggerStore.RejectAwaitingDurableWorkAsync` atomically rejects unavailable target input and records its reason; recurring configurations suspend, while one-shot lifecycle remains inspectable. Target suspension requires an explicit eligible owner edit to recover.

`IAgentRunStore.GetCompletionDeliveryAsync` projects NotRequested, Pending, Claimed, Handled, Admitted, Delivered, Failed or Skipped from immutable child origin and its unique receipt. Delivered requires a completed parent Run and durable assistant entry, not merely report admission. Current immediate and Automation initial-child terminal outcomes share `TenantBackgroundCompletionService`; NoAction settles quietly. The global reporter actor is retired. `AgentRunCommand.DeferDispatch` is valid only before checkpoint/effects and preserves identity and provider attempt budget. `AgentContext.OutputContract` and authored-source identity separate conversational scheduling from native occurrence evaluation.

## Completion handoff and typed waits

`IAgentRunStore` owns bounded inbox list/inspect, revision-fenced take and acknowledgment intent, terminal accounting, and typed wait recovery alongside admission and outcome transactions. Ownership is exact Agent Instance, trusted profile, immutable parent/child origin and initial child Run. `background.list`, `background.inspect`, `background.take`, `background.acknowledge` and `execution.wait` are explicit authorizable capabilities. Tool results are untrusted bounded evidence. A take token binds Run, generation, tool call and expiry. Valid parent lease renewal extends an unexpired consumption claim atomically, retaining its token and provisional acknowledgment; expired claims are never revived. A signal resume transfers an unexpired claim to the resumed generation and lease. Acknowledgment requires a nonempty bounded usage statement and is provisional until successful durable final output.

WaitingForSignal has no execution claim and carries the tool call identity, frozen condition/deadline and checkpoint. Duration seconds and background timeout must be finite and positive, at most 300 seconds each; background targets are unique, nonempty, at most eight owned initial children, with `until` all or any. One Run may wait at most eight times and 900 seconds in total. Validation fails with ValidationError; foreign ownership uses NotFound; stale revisions/generations, busy parent, competing or expired claims use Conflict. Timeout returns normally and never acknowledges a result.

Exact GetCompletionInboxAsync lookup and ListBackgroundPageAsync cursor reads retain owner/parent scoping beyond bounded pages. HasCompletionClaimAsync gates acknowledgment projection on the current Run and generation; HasCompletionAcknowledgmentAsync selects the atomic successful-outcome path.

## Original background task reads and Artifact provenance

Background Work reads `IAgentRunStore.GetAsync(owner, InitialBackgroundAgentRunId)` and checks the returned Session identity; newer Runs cannot replace it. The existing completion delivery query remains independent. `IArtifactStore.CreateAsync` accepts optional trusted `agentRunId`, supplied by the current ToolExecutionAdmission for create/create_from_workspace browser screenshots/downloads and sandbox export. Non-execution materialization and historical records keep null ownership. `ListPageAsync` accepts an optional Run filter; both stores filter before cursor validation and paging. No timestamps or file order determine attribution. File bytes, quotas and Session download authorization stay unchanged.


## Instance Activity catalog read

`IMemoryStore.ListInstanceSessionsAsync(AgentRunOwner owner, string? cursor, int limit, CancellationToken)` projects bounded Session metadata for the exact Instance/profile across all surfaces, including archived contexts and excluding durably deleted ones. InMemory and SQLite retain descending UpdatedAt/SessionId cursor semantics. The SQLite read filters ownership before paging and materializes at most limit + 1 metadata rows with their snapshot, without loading entries or performing per-row Run/artifact queries. Existing Chat and Background Work catalog ports retain their surface filters. This is a read projection over existing Session ownership, not a new persistence model or domain relationship.

`IAgentRunStore.ListPageAsync` excludes Runs without a matching, undeleted Session before applying the page limit; archived Sessions remain eligible. Cursor validation uses the retained owner/Session Run scope before this visibility filter, so deletion of a cursor's Session does not invalidate an already-loaded page boundary. `GetAsync` continues to retain Run evidence; HTTP projection separately checks Session availability as defined in [Protocol](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md).

## Browser reliability follow-up

`browser.close` accepts `{"operation":"close"}` or the compatible empty object `{}` (insignificant whitespace is allowed). Its offered schema exposes only an optional string `operation` with the single allowed value `close`, giving models an explicit call form without invented wrappers. The tool description and validation error show both valid forms. Core interprets genuinely absent/blank argument payloads as `{}` only for an offered zero-property object schema, or this exact optional fixed close selector; both require no required fields and `additionalProperties=false`. Other operations, duplicate properties and additional members remain invalid. Explicit JSON null, arrays, malformed JSON and unexpected fields/wrappers remain rejected before dispatch. Adapters preserve string argument bytes for Core validation. Chat Completions and Responses non-string transport arguments remain nonblank, content-free invalid JSON, never coerced executable objects or absent zero-parameter payloads; durable calls retain the normalized invalid payload, identity and continuation. Equivalent invalid strategies stay blocked across checkpoints without disabling a corrected valid call or unrelated authorized tools. Existing step/time/output/checkpoint, approval, cancellation and cleanup/finalization boundaries still bound recovery. An SDK-confirmed close remains separate from verified application logout. [Scoped recovery verification](reports/browser-close-capture-reliability-verification.md).

Retired-instruction validation applies to Definition system instructions and procedures active in the current execution. An inactive OnDemand Skill remains discoverable without blocking unrelated work; loading its retired executable procedure returns `browser_contract_retired` with the exact Skill key and revision-checked owner update guidance. Historical prose and explicit warnings about removed parameters do not invalidate current instructions. Direct retired argument shapes remain rejected by the tool validator regardless of prose detection. [Verification](reports/browser-final-reliability-verification.md).

## Execution budget contracts

`ExecutionBudgetPolicy` carries optional Standard, InteractiveBrowser and UnattendedBoundBrowser profiles on Definition/Candidate and Instance. Null class entries inherit independently. `EffectiveExecutionBudget` stores class, values, source, whether frozen user input requested cleanup and optional `BrowserCleanupIntent` with independent logout/closure flags in immutable `AgentRunAdmission`; it grants no capability. `AgentRunCheckpoint` adds nullable active-execution milliseconds and a budget-clock timestamp. Running-to-wait/retry/terminal transitions charge elapsed time through the claim lease, then pause; reclaim resumes from the same pin and counters. Historical null fields preserve the earlier context-selected profile and checkpoint remaining time. `IAgentInstanceStore` updates budgets with the existing expected-revision mutation.


### Model transport continuation

`ModelDescriptor.Transport` expresses a trusted inference protocol preference; `PreferResponseFunction` selects the existing schema-bearing semantic channel independently of native schema capability; HTTP/vendor DTOs stay in Infrastructure. `ModelToolCall.ContinuationToken` is bounded opaque adapter state. Application forwards it unchanged with assistant tool-call messages and durable checkpoints; it never parses it or projects it to Chat/Admin. The adapter rejects malformed, oversized or model/transport-mismatched continuation with `InvalidRequest` before HTTP; absent continuation is valid for a first round. Cancellation, normalized tool events, usage and semantic final responses use the existing ILanguageModel contract. See [catalog configuration](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#real-model-catalog).

`RunBudgetDiagnostics` projects ordered durable browser receipts into independent `ClosureRequested`, `ClosureConfirmed`, `LogoutRequested`, `LogoutVerified`, and `CleanupBlocked` fields plus `CleanupStatus` (`notRequested`, `unverified`, `partial`, `completed`, `blocked`). Historical pins without structured intent expose unknown request flags. `LogoutVerified` stays false under the generic browser contract: `applicationOutcomeVerified` proves a predicate, not application authentication semantics. Model result wording remains available as observed evidence without becoming Core proof. A successful retry clears its operation's blocker; a later SDK-confirmed alternate interaction may clear non-close blockers. Close uncertainty requires a successful close receipt. Original failures remain in checkpoint/result evidence.

## Core Event and shared filter ports

`AutomationTriggerKind.CoreEvent` and `TriggerSourceKind.CoreEvent` append to the existing discriminants; `EventTrigger` continues to mean a shared webhook resource. `CoreEventTrigger` contains a catalog key, optional `FilterExpression`, and `EventDispatch`; the same filter/dispatch fields belong to `EventTrigger`. Semantic equality includes all matching fields. `TriggerProvenance.PresetId/PresetVersion` are immutable optional creation provenance and never select ongoing execution restrictions.

`IEventFilterEvaluator.Validate(string?)` returns a safe validation code or null; `Evaluate(string?, JsonElement, CancellationToken)` returns a strict nullable Boolean decision, safe status/code, schema and expression version. Infrastructure owns Jint; no engine/AST objects cross the port. `ICoreEventStore` owns pending trusted receipts, exact-owner subscriber snapshots, per-subscription decisions, durable bounded buckets, and `CoveragePageAsync(owner, automationId, cursor, limit)` with deterministic continuation. `MarkSourceInspectedAsync` advances only validated owned source coverage. `IExternalEventStore.DecideDeliveryAsync` persists and returns the authoritative shared webhook filter decision. Both decision CAS methods accept an optional exact prior transient `expectedDecision` and return the winner so a late evaluator cannot act on its discarded result. `EventFilterResult.Attempt/RetryAtUtc` record bounded recovery in existing decision JSON; only worker contention and execution timeout codes are retryable. Receipt admission freezes webhook subscribers; first durable Core fan-out freezes Core subscribers.

`TenantAutomationPresetRules` owns the same three immutable versioned templates and computes prerequisites from the current owned Agent, tenant settings, capabilities, management and model policy. Generic preset eligibility and catalog DI are retired. Human/model authoring uses the canonical owners above; presets and background occurrences grant no Automation mutation authority.

### Event recovery scan cursors

`ICoreEventStore.PendingAsync` and `DueBucketsAsync` accept an optional `EventRecoveryCursor(AtUtc, Id)` after the cancellation token. Pending receipts use received time/Event ID; due buckets use due time/Bucket ID. Both return at most 32 rows, ordered by UTC milliseconds then canonical GUID text, strictly after the cursor. `IExternalEventStore.ListPendingDeliveriesAsync` accepts `ExternalEventRecoveryCursor(EventId, AutomationId)` and orders canonical GUID text by that pair; limits stay clamped to 1–64 (the recovery service requests 32). Null selects the first page. A cursor selects unfinished work; it never acknowledges, mutates or deletes it.

The single hosted recovery pass advances independent in-process cursors for Core receipts, due buckets and webhook deliveries, wrapping to the beginning when the next page is empty. Webhook recovery processes the exact selected subscriber page, including continuations within a single receipt. Restart begins a new scan while retaining durable snapshots/decisions. No cursor schema, externally visible pagination API or execution authority is added.

## Browser operational contract

Browser hardening adds immutable host operational budgets on `BrowserHostPolicy.Limits`. `IBrowserContextUse.EnterUnattendedAsync` uses nullable origins explicitly: null inherits host policy, empty denies all, nonempty further restricts it. Instance/capability admission is unchanged. `BrowserResult` outcome flags stay independent through normal and truncated projections; close never verifies application logout. Text verification requires non-whitespace expected text, while explicit empty `value` verifies field clearing. See [operational configuration](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#browser-operational-budgets) and [native implementation](12-backend-implementation-spec.md#native-browser-hardening).

## Adaptive browser observation

Browser strategy is a single contextual `BrowserObservationGuidance` projection derived from eligible browser authority and the active model's Vision capability. Semantic snapshots and direct native semantic targets remain the default. Authorized vision executions may inspect screenshots proactively for unfamiliar dashboards, hierarchical layout, weak labels or unexpected states; they combine image and semantic evidence before preferring semantic actions. Text-only executions receive artifact metadata plus semantic observation and an explicit `imageDelivered=false` diagnostic. There is no model switch or second inference call.

`browser.screenshot` retains Session-owned Artifacts and request-local `ModelImageContent`. Its receipt adds `imageDelivered`, `snapshotId`, `tabRef`, a bounded matching `observation`, `observationUnavailable` and `coordinateEvidence`. Semantic collection failure can retain masked image context with empty semantic fields and an explicit unavailable flag; independent privacy inspection is mandatory. Coordinate authority requires successful settlement plus independently fresh visual state, even when semantics are unavailable. `BrowserHostPolicy.ScreenshotAvailable` describes effective host capture availability for contextual guidance. Only a current viewport capture can supply coordinate evidence; target/full-page captures provide context only. Output clipping preserves capture identity where it fits and reports `observationTruncated`; obtain `browser.snapshot` for omitted semantics. Browser Mouse accepts the screenshot's `snapshotId`; native dispatch checks the owning Session, active page, generation and viewport/layout state under the browser gate. Semantic snapshots do not confer coordinate authority. A missing/stale ID returns `stale_visual_evidence` without attempting the effect. Recovered image bytes remain useful historical context, but do not reconstruct native coordinate authority.

Successful screenshots also expose `coordinateEvidenceUnavailableReasons` (bounded reason codes) and `captureDiagnostics`: settlement success, observation availability, DOM-change polling samples, peak/last observed in-flight fetch/XHR counts; observer availability, in-flight requests at capture, font loading and running animation count; page/generation, viewport/scroll and visual fingerprint changes across capture. Diagnostics contain no page content, URLs, resource names or private fingerprints. These are observations within a bounded window, not proof of continuous mutation or complete network coverage. Screenshot coordinate admission is fail-closed across the settlement window: `settlement.settled` describes eventual quietness, while `settlement.observationAvailable` requires every polling read to have been available. Quiet reads after a transient failure may report the former as true and the latter as false; that capture retains image context, returns `coordinateEvidence=false` and matching `observation.settled=false`, and reports `state_observation_unavailable` rather than falsely claiming a settlement deadline. A new capture with complete observations can restore authority. Whole-page quietness and native freshness checks remain required. `context_only_capture`, `settle_deadline`, `state_observation_unavailable`, `dom_mutation_during_settle`, `network_inflight_observed`, `fonts_loading`, `animation_running`, `page_changed_during_capture`, `viewport_changed_during_capture` and `visual_state_changed_during_capture` identify observed limitations.

Application reconstructs explicitly unusable screenshot IDs from durable receipts and suppresses a mouse call against those IDs with `coordinate_evidence_unavailable`, `strategySuppressed=true`, `effectAttempted=false`; unknown IDs still require native freshness validation. Contextual guidance recommends authorized semantic observations/actions or justified bounded waiting, advises against equivalent capture loops, and preserves independently requested `browser.close` after failed logout. A newly eligible screenshot can restore coordinate recovery; historical images never gain authority. Closure is independently verified and does not establish logout.


`IBrowserPrivacyStore` persists the global saved `BrowserScreenshotPolicy` with revision CAS and an atomic sanitized audit append. `BrowserPrivacyService` pins its effective policy at native host startup beneath `BrowserPrivacyAuthority` deployment ceilings. `IBrowserCaptureLifetime` fences capture/storage publication on host retirement. These ports carry Core policy records only; no provider DTO crosses the boundary. Repeated semantic misses use the existing evidence-progress projection, while logout verification and native close evidence remain separate.

## Canonical child-trigger ownership

`Automation.Triggers` is the canonical collection of stable `AutomationTriggerRecord` values. A parent selects one Schedule or 1–32 Events; ownership, execution target, completion delivery, model, instructions and preset provenance belong to the parent. `EventSourceReference` validates exactly `{kind: builtin, key}` or `{kind: webhook, eventId}`. Internal single-trigger construction helpers produce this collection immediately; they are not a stored or wire compatibility model.

`ITriggerStore` atomically saves the submitted child set with parent CAS; unchanged children keep revisions, configuration/enabled changes advance only the affected child. A retained child cannot change source identity. Delivery/retry and bucket APIs identify subscriptions by `TriggerId`, while owner queries and history retain `AutomationId`. Event occurrence admission checks current parent/child enablement inside the store admission gate. Current policy/model/destination checks remain Application owned. A frozen snapshot retains its exact child/source/filter/dispatch/revision through recovery; removal or disabling prevents further admission. Manual Run now is parent owned and uses ordinary execution authorization, without Event filters or arbitrary child selection.

## Scoped settings, resources and execution ports

`InstanceSettingsOverrides` uses explicit typed `InstanceField<T>` wrappers for field presence. `InstanceSettingsResolver` owns allowlisted section set/clear, full effective validation and authority narrowing; it does not perform arbitrary JSON merge. `TenantAgentConfigurationService` reads effective values, Definition defaults, sparse overrides and field sources from one owned generation, using pure `InstanceSettingsRules` projections. `ExecutionBudgets` remains a separate atomic per-class owner.

`IAgentInstanceStore.ReadResourcesAsync/MutateResourcesAsync` owns local metadata and nullable inherited enablement state. Mutations check Instance and item/state revisions, quotas, ownership and active lifecycle; content uses the existing immutable `IDefinitionResourceContentStore`. Pure `InstanceResourceRules` normalizes paths/media, checks hashes/lengths, refuses disabled dependencies and projects origin-qualified identities. Canonical tenant configuration/management services and owning store transactions authorize and commit changes; the global resource actor is removed. Copy creates an independent local identity with Definition source provenance. `InheritedDefinitionResourceCatalog` projects approved built-in knowledge references alongside durable publication records, with deterministic resource IDs scoped to Definition ID and trusted knowledge identity (stable across versions). It uses `IApprovedKnowledgeCatalog` and deduplicated immutable content storage without creating publication rows or rewriting source files. Admin read/copy, next-Run admission, adoption and dependency validation consume the same manifest. `knowledge.remove` maps ordinary trusted knowledge identities to inherited resources and saves an Instance disable override; exact origin-qualified keys remain supported. Instance authoring/copy accepts Knowledge, Reference, Template and StaticAsset; EvalFixture remains Definition-only.

`TenantRunConfigurationResolver.ResolveAsync(tenant, instance)` returns the owned effective Definition, persona, Skills and Instance budget overrides at a stable owner revision. The global resolver/Composer reader actors are retired; shared `ResolvedAgentRunConfiguration` remains a value record. Pure `ComposerInputRules.ActiveSkills` retains selected/Always Skill validation without resource I/O or authority. `AgentRunAdmission.Configuration` persists the immutable generation; ExecutionResources is a runtime manifest reattached during rehydration. Model pinning and `ExecutionBudgetPolicy.Resolve` consume that same generation before atomic admission. No mutable owner re-resolution occurs during admitted Run recovery.


## Unified self-management application owners

Generic unowned configuration, settings, Resource, credential-binding, unattended-preference and Skill management dispatch is retired. Caller-built delegation cannot offer or execute these operations. `SessionToolExecutor` retains neither the old configuration/remedy nor Skill executor and no longer injects their five direct management services. Canonical descriptors, schema helpers and shared semantic planners remain with the tenant owners below. [Scoped retirement qualification](reports/generic-management-retirement-verification-2026-10-23.md).

Canonical tenant Runs obtain live management eligibility through `ITenantRunContext.CurrentHarnessContextAsync`, implemented by `TenantRunExecutionAuthority` against the actual claimed Run, tenant Agent and immutable publication. The Runtime uses this path for initial context, model continuations, capability discovery and Skill-load admission. It never falls back to the legacy Instance policy reader for a tenant owner. The admitted and current policies intersect mode, areas, auto-apply choices, source/tool eligibility and Built-in choices; either freeze wins. Current reductions constrain the existing Run, while newly enabled delegation waits for a new Run. Instructions, settings, persona, publication and the Skill catalog remain pinned. A raced security/Run observation is discarded and read once more; continued change fails closed. This is eligibility metadata, not a protected configuration read or effect grant.

Canonical `skills.load` uses the existing mailbox/CAS activation owner for the actual Run's pinned catalog. Immutable Core Built-ins keep current/admitted management delegation and exact offered Core keys. Ordinary Definition/Instance procedures use independent current `agents.read` and exact-child `skills.read`; management Off/Frozen does not revoke this ordinary path. The loader is the incumbent runtime bootstrap operation, not a Definition-authorizable tool grant. Required capabilities remain requirements and cannot authorize tools, resources or credentials.

`TenantSkillLoadContract` accepts one to four distinct canonical keys. Private read-consent selectors normalize the selected set and reject caller authority fields or Core keys. Existing `inspect_management` consent can carry `read.tool = skills.load` with those exact private IDs; it reads saved pinned procedures and activates nothing. Current source/reviewer/raw reads remain mandatory, read consent creates no grant, and rejection remains within the same semantic root. The ordinary mailbox call separately applies the existing two-invocation/8000-character limits. Procedure-dependent validation follows private source authorization; the invocation ceiling uses only the Run's existing accounting.

Private metadata and Always/active procedures are filtered before prompt/tool projection. Under required read Review, private content remains withheld until exact consent, even while an Always key remains in immutable activation history. Consumed private observations join existing monotonic checkpoint source references and revalidate before model reentry and protected output. Source changes never substitute new content into a Run; exact same-task Skill effects may retain historical observations through their receipt chain. New content still requires fresh admission. This path also supports admitted Scheduled, manual, Core and webhook Automation tasks through their actual origin and existing worker; no execution framework or permission is added. [Event and recovery qualification](reports/canonical-automation-integration-verification-2026-10-23.md) covers direct/no-read boundaries, exact approve/reject and pending SQLite host recreation. Canonical `skills.inspect` remains an independently authorized data read and does not activate a procedure. [Qualification and limits](reports/tenant-private-skill-verification-2026-10-22.md).

`TenantAgentConfigurationService` and canonical management operations resolve the selected Definition defaults plus sparse Instance fields in the existing `HarnessManagementState`, using pure `HarnessManagementRules`. The existing tenant configuration transaction compares current security/Instance revisions and commits the configuration and body-free audit together. The current principal determines human versus agent behavior; caller-supplied actor flags grant no authority. Inert records with no sparse overrides preserve their exact policy and historical preparation bytes. An authorized human can reset fields or overrides and unfreeze; an agent cannot enable or unfreeze management. Freeze survives Definition adoption. Global Self-management/settings/Skill/isolation actors and their DI registrations are retired.

Canonical tenant Settings, Skills, Resources, compiled contextual Harness commands and inspected remedies use `TenantAgentConfigurationService` with the shared incumbent semantic planners and protected effect transaction described above. The former unscoped Harness orchestrator, its preparation executor and generic tool dispatch are removed. Serialized historical preparations remain inert; canonical commits reject creating or changing them. Human `TenantCredentialBindingService` commands and canonical model binding proposals share the incumbent association mutation in each store. No generic Admin transport or parallel mutable owner is exposed. Bind/unbind increments Instance revision and appends redacted audit atomically; protected values remain in the credential owner. `AgentRunConfiguration` pins credential binding identities/revisions alongside existing settings/catalog/resources. Current binding revocation defeats those pins.

`ConfigurationPendingTask` binds source Run ID, diagnosed target, bounded goal and committed Instance revision. Canonical tenant continuation derives it from the actual approved remedy, completed effect receipt and saved authorization checkpoint. The incumbent response-outcome transaction may atomically admit one `ConfigurationContinuation` with a fresh Run/response identity, current configuration/model pins, the same human root and Session, no newly owned input entries and an authoritative saved history boundary. Rejection, stale sources, cancellation, later human input, other active/later work or prior ordinary write attempts prevent automatic continuation. `AgentContext` and `ToolExecutionAdmission.ConfigurationContinuationTarget` restrict offering and execution to the diagnosed task and ordinary reads; management remains excluded. Every resumed write requires new exact consent, including through approval successors. Their target, history boundary and pins remain bound to the source; direct write transactions reject bypasses. No provider DTO, new worker/mailbox or public continuation endpoint is introduced. `automation.preferences.inspect/patch` uses AutomationPreferences scope and the existing unattended model service.

## Account-scoped Email ports

`CredentialPurpose.EmailAccount` requires a validated typed profile and no Browser origins. Gmail requires `Kind.Token` / `EmailAuthMethod.GoogleOAuth2`; Custom requires `Kind.Password` / Password. Kind/Purpose remain immutable in ordinary CRUD; only the forward Gmail migration converts legacy rows. Browser password sinks reject every EmailAccount.

`TenantCredentialOAuthService` and `ITenantCredentialOAuthStore` own bounded durable start/claim/callback/disconnect intent under the original Account/login, tenant membership, credential revisions and exact purpose. `TenantGoogleOAuthProvider` owns Google proof exchange, scoped runtime refresh and safe provider failures. Current authority is repeated before private proof or protected-token persistence; restart preserves the supported durable attempt. No global OAuth service or credential owner fallback remains. Safe profile metadata and protected refresh material remain separate; access tokens stay ephemeral inside Infrastructure. The canonical OAuth ownership sections below own the detailed callback, refresh and concurrency contract.

`TenantConnectedAccountService` and `TenantAccountRuntimeReadService` resolve actual tenant-owned accounts and bindings under independent current IAM, purpose and raw-source authority. `ITenantConnectedAccountTransportFactory` supplies a scoped Synthetic/Gmail/Custom transport with live recheck callbacks. Current account scopes and host policy independently bound sending; `IEmailSendPreflight` obtains valid Gmail authorization before the durable claim. `IEmailStore` retains physical Core drafts and immutable claims/receipts beneath the canonical tenant transaction owners; Gmail never creates provider Drafts. Refresh does not change draft approval hashes, while grant epoch changes do. [Protocol](14-api-and-realtime-protocol.md#account-scoped-email-contracts) owns exact wire shapes.

## Email monitoring ports

`ITenantEmailChangeFeed.ReadTenantChangesAsync` accepts the actual tenant/account source, a typed cursor and owning live authority/maintenance callbacks, then returns a bounded `EmailWatchBatch`. No Google DTOs, MailKit objects or tokens cross Infrastructure. `ITenantEmailWatchStore` owns canonical configure/check, due claims and atomic cursor/Event/subscriber commits under tenant IAM. Configuration revision is independent of Credential and runtime revisions; ordinary checks never revise account configuration. OAuth refresh-secret rotation preserves a cursor; mailbox/auth changes rebaseline. New watches default Off with a 300-second interval. The retained `IEmailWatchStore` is a physical historical-store contract; no canonical watcher falls back to its global source selection. The old global change feed and watch dispatcher are retired.

Email Check now queues a recovery request independently of delivery eligibility. A paused watch remains fenced during the queued/leased scan and after a failed recovery; a successful scan confirms authorization. Email subscriber snapshots are created with the source commit, using existing Event subscription records.


## Office processor port

`IOfficeProcessor` creates, inspects and edits semantic `OfficeDocumentSpec` / `OfficeWorkbookSpec` values and byte snapshots. Requests/results contain Application records, never vendor OOXML/ClosedXML objects. Document inspection returns content-anchored paragraph/table selectors and a SHA; table-cell selectors append zero-based row/column indices. Spreadsheet tools default to `ReadWorkbookContent`: bounded sparse content from one worksheet, original addresses, sheet identity/order, source SHA, formula expression, displayed value, number format and cached-value status without recalculation. Explicit A1 ranges use `InspectWorkbook` and retain rectangular rows, blanks, sheet extents and `nextRange`. `OfficeSheetExtent` records first/last used rows and columns (zero for an empty sheet). `OfficeReadResults` preserves whole sparse cells or rectangular rows within caller budgets; `truncated` describes partial coverage of the selected sheet, not an inaccessible file. Agents choose requested ranges and can read the complete supported workbook over successive calls. The separate `PreviewWorkbook` projection adds normalized presentation records (font/fill/border/alignment, geometry, merges, origin/used bounds and frozen panes); vendor objects and FortuneSheet types stay out of the port. Tool create/edit/inspect semantics and hard safety guards remain independent of the host preview policy.

DOCX inspection accepts validated external hyperlink relationships as inert metadata in Word content relationship parts (document, headers, footers, footnotes, endnotes and comments). Only standard Transitional/Strict OOXML hyperlink types, explicit external mode and HTTP/HTTPS or a single-recipient mailto URI are admitted. Targets are never fetched or followed. Supported edits to unrelated simple paragraphs/cells preserve existing hyperlinks, relationships and styles; editing a hyperlink-containing paragraph/cell remains unsupported. This is limited business-template compatibility, not full Word fidelity. Preview keeps its existing href/navigation removal and scriptless frame policy. Other external relationships and XLSX external links remain blocked; [the backend guard](12-backend-implementation-spec.md#bounded-office-processing) owns the exact target/part restrictions.

A spreadsheet range outside the used rows or columns returns empty `rows`, null `returnedRange` and null `nextRange`, while preserving the selected range, original SHA and sheet extents. `truncated` still reports incomplete coverage of a nonempty sheet; an empty intersection is not an invalid package or a reader failure. [Stabilization verification](reports/office-productivity-stabilization-2026-10-14.md) covers attachment and Workspace reads plus subsequent populated-range recovery.

`OfficeSparseWorkbookInspection` carries up to 500 meaningful cells, available sheet names, one-based worksheet ordinal, the fixed optional sheet selection, whether more cells remain, initial-position evidence and warnings. `OfficeReadResults.SparseWorkbook` fits whole cells to the caller's UTF-8 budget and derives a stateless `nextCursor` from the last emitted address or the next sheet. `coverage`/`sheetCoverage` describe content in this response alone. `sheetCoverage` is complete only when this page starts at the beginning and reaches the worksheet end. `coverage` is complete only when that complete sheet is the selected scope or the workbook has one sheet. `pageCoverage` is `complete_sheet` or `partial_sheet`. `worksheetEndReached` and the existing `traversalComplete` describe end positions independently; neither verifies earlier reads. `analysisCoverage` is always `not_verified`: the stateless reader cannot verify an assembled analysis. `coverageScope` distinguishes workbook from selected-sheet traversal. Empty and formatting-only sheets emit an empty page and advance one sheet at a time. Completing a later cursor page does not put preceding data into that response; consumers must combine actual successful pages. The cursor binds version/mode, SHA, selected-sheet option, current sheet name/ordinal and last coordinate. It conveys no authority: Workspace ownership or Session/AttachmentId permissions are checked on every read. Changed bytes return `office_revision_conflict`; malformed or mismatched continuation returns `office_cursor_invalid`; a cell/metadata set that cannot fit returns `office_output_limit` without an invented cursor. No read-session storage is added.

The six `office.document.{create,inspect,edit}` and `office.spreadsheet.{create,inspect,edit}` tools use closed schemas and Session scope. Inspect is ReadOnly/ReplaySafe; create/edit are Write/NonReplayable. Existing `attachments.read` authorization also permits direct read-only DOCX/XLSX inspection by owned AttachmentId with optional sheet/cursor (sparse XLSX), sheet/range (rectangular XLSX) or offset/limit (DOCX); no Workspace copy or Office write grant is required. Writes return path/SHA/bytes/warnings; publication is a separate `artifacts.create_from_workspace` call. General Assistant v26 explicitly selects Office; v27 adds Drive. Registration never grants immutable older versions.

`TenantCredentialOAuthService`, `ITenantCredentialOAuthStore` and `TenantGoogleOAuthProvider` own current tenant Drive connection/proof/refresh/disconnect authority. `ITenantConnectedAccountTransportFactory` produces the scoped `IGoogleDriveProvider` with live source and protected-token maintenance callbacks. The global OAuth service and provider factory are retired; readiness consumes only the current provider's safe `CredentialOAuthDescriptor`, so conflicting old client settings cannot advertise an unavailable canonical connection. Provider methods use safe `DriveFile` / `DriveFilePage`, IDs, bytes and cancellation, never Google DTOs or tokens. Binary download has a separate preview-bounded overload: adapters check metadata/headers and stop transfer at the supplied byte ceiling; original downloads retain their independent hard transfer limit. `IDriveOperationStore` owns owner/credential-scoped generated-ID claims, snapshot hashes and revision CAS; upload session authority is protected backend-only material. `ISyntheticDriveStore` supplies deterministic fixture bytes.

`DriveEffectNotExecutedException` is normalized adapter evidence that no initial effect request was sent or that Google explicitly rejected it. It cannot represent transport loss, missing metadata or upload-session expiry. A terminal Failed operation persists safe `DriveOperationFailure` code/message/proof in its existing result field. Verified upload-target Trash evidence additionally includes the current safe `DriveFile`; the exact known ID must match and continuation authority must be cleared. This evidence means the target is unavailable as an active upload, not that no write ever occurred. The same operation ID remains terminal; an explicit new operation may claim the released effect. Upload continuation uses `UploadAsync` with the original protected session and immutable source, after status inspection; it never creates a replacement session. Read-only operation inspection never sends content.

Eleven tools: `drive.accounts`, `list`, `search`, `get`, `create_folder`, `upload`, `download`, `rename`, `move`, `trash`, `trash_batch`. Accounts/list/search/get are ReadOnly/ReplaySafe; download/create/upload are Write/NonReplayable; rename/move SensitiveWrite and trash/trash_batch Destructive, all NonReplayable. Mutating sensitive tools require exact action-hash approval. Upload requires source SHA and current authEpoch and creates a new binary file; download verifies remote version/size/checksum before a new local commit. `drive.get` accepts a fileId or owned operationId for read-only reconciliation. Every tool requires the current owned Session/Instance and admitted alias binding.

`TenantAccountActionTarget` prepares exact owned metadata/bytes, current binding/epoch and whole-manifest eligibility under actual Run/executor/root authority; the prepared action and owning approval/effect services retain exact arguments, scopes and concurrency witnesses. `DriveService` accepts only their live `DriveAccess.TenantAuthority` binding and performs no global Credential/Instance/binding resolution. Reads repeat the callback before and after provider I/O; effects and receipt inspection require it before native-ledger access and retain incumbent inner rechecks. `DriveService.TrashBatchAsync` accepts 1–20 distinct exact ordered targets and composes existing single-trash receipts under its bounded deadline. Receipt Synthetic provenance derives from the accepted account profile, never a global factory. No caller-built access object or matching epoch alone grants authority.


## Public file download port

`IPublicWebFetcher.SupportsDownloads` advertises the optional binary path; `DownloadAsync(PublicWebDownloadRequest)` returns normalized `PublicWebDownloadResult` (final URL, declared content type, suggested filename, exact bounded bytes, error code/message). `PublicWebFetcher` and its Synthetic wrapper implement it through the existing `IPublicWebTransport.SendAsync` GET path. A default port implementation reports unavailable, so older text-only adapters do not falsely advertise downloading.

The canonical public-download path uses the existing tenant workspace mutation owner. `web.download` is an exact-authorized, configuration-gated Session `Write`, with normal non-replayable Run effect fencing and no separate sensitive-action approval. Execution checks active Session/Instance ownership, validates explicit logical destinations before dispatch, and replaces the present file atomically through an authorized tenant-owned file effect in durable `/home` or owned `/working`. File revision/hash preconditions are rejected; normal Run effect fencing and exact transferred-byte receipts remain. Every result/file is untrusted. Tool JSON contains metadata only; file bytes never enter model messages or Run checkpoints. Discovery and publication grant no implicit authority; General Assistant v29 explicitly authorizes the tool, while earlier versions retain their original grants. The old unowned runtime/download fixture and global workspace facade are retired. Canonical model download→inspect→edit→Artifact export, exact review, automatic destinations and SQLite reopen now have [scoped local qualification](reports/tenant-public-download-verification-2026-10-23.md). `workspaces.write` supplies primary authority; explicit tenant review and requester-aware defaults remain unchanged. Omitted destinations create a unique non-overwriting `/home/downloads/{operationId}-{safeName}` file. Explicit destinations bind the current saved file observation for review. Completed factual receipts retain requested/final URL, owned path, media type, byte count and SHA-256, without bytes or read authority. Late or uncertain transfers never authorize replay.

## OpenSandbox ports

`StopSessionAsync` carries a typed `SandboxStopIntent` independently of optional generation/execution identity fences. `SessionLifecycle` and `PolicyChanged` preserve retained selection; only `OwnerReset` clears it. Policy enforcement supplies `PolicyChanged`, including cancellation receipts and reconciliation after pending remote cleanup. A retained revoked Profile remains unavailable until explicitly reselected or reset.

`ITenantSandboxCommandExecutor.ExecuteApprovedAsync` is the command entry: it requires the actual tenant Runtime frame and saved exact approval request. Current owned verification uses `ITenantSandboxVerifier`. The native `ISandboxService` retains only generation cleanup and supervised reconciliation; `SandboxAccess`, raw Execute, global inspection/process controls and the registered Profile-based `AgentRunAuthority` are retired. The `IAgentRunAuthority` port remains implemented by canonical `TenantRunExecutionAuthority`. `SandboxCommandResult` returns a Core execution ID, bounded streams, exit/duration, truncation, possible filesystem effects, cleanup evidence and diagnostic ID; provider IDs, endpoint/key, SDK objects and host paths remain Infrastructure-only.

`ISandboxStore` owns append-only profile revisions, one CAS lease per Session and durable operation receipts unique on `(SessionId, AgentRunId, ToolCallId)`. Repeating the same operation returns evidence and never dispatches it again; changed arguments conflict. Infrastructure resolves the exact canonical working/home directories, verifies root identity, numeric UID/GID and effective RW/RO/disabled home authority, and mounts only those children. Bytes are visible immediately; command completion and recovery reconcile processes without capturing, importing, swapping or rolling back a file tree.


`ISandboxReadiness` supplies safe offering/UI state without coupling ordinary startup to Docker. The old `ISandboxExecutor`, `SandboxRequest`, `SandboxResult` and direct CLI backend have been removed. See [persistence](15-persistence-and-configuration.md#opensandbox-durable-records-and-configuration).

OpenSandbox process reliability: uncertain termination retains its exact generation/operation receipt and never replays the command. `ISandboxStore.GetVerificationAsync` / `SaveVerificationAsync` store normalized `SandboxProfileVerification` observations for an exact immutable profile revision. Canonical `TenantSandboxInspectionService` and its typed HTTP/model read owners expose stored receipts/evidence; supervised tenant reconciliation or exact approved Stop owns completion processing. No unowned process inspection/control facade remains.

### Observable setup projections

`SandboxProfile.InstanceInitializationScript` and `InstanceRevision` are resolved generation pins, not mutations of the reusable Global Profile. `SandboxSetupResolver` applies sparse ceilings and validates the combined setup. `SandboxInitializationEvidence` records state, pinned revisions, bounded streams, exit, times, duration and diagnostic/phase on the existing lease. Owner HTTP environment inspection includes lifecycle and cleanup observations; model `sandbox.status` omits initialization logs. Model self-management retains the existing authorized `profileId` selection; initialization and new limit fields stay owner-only and are removed from reads, patch results and approval previews.

`ISandboxStore.GetInstanceVerificationAsync` / `SaveInstanceVerificationAsync` store an observation keyed by Instance and saved revision. Owner projections discard evidence from a different effective Profile revision. `VerifyProfileAsync` checks the full Profile/effective Instance scripts by default; `imageOnly` checks readiness without executing scripts. Both use disposable environments and normalized evidence without Session filesystem mutation.

### OCI image authority port

`ISandboxImageAuthority.ResolveAsync` validates owner image input and host/credential scope, resolves tags through bounded registry HEAD/auth requests, and returns a profile with a canonical immutable digest before persistence. Docker Hub short names (including dotted single-component names) normalize to `docker.io`, with `library/` for single-component names and `latest` for omitted tags. Provider auth stays inside Infrastructure. `ISandboxImageAuthority.InspectAsync` returns safe availability/status, recovery guidance and nonsecret credential epochs for a saved profile. Infrastructure owns strict source policy and protected credential lookup; vendor `ImageAuth` and registry tokens do not cross this port. Run resolution combines source eligibility with server readiness and pins only currently executable profiles; owner inspection and disposable Instance verification can inspect saved ineligible configuration. Tool admission's independent configuration resolver uses the same authority. `ISandboxReadiness.State` describes the server; `IsAvailable` additionally requires an eligible catalog entry. First-ever verification works with no eligible entry. Session environment inspection adds safe `profileName` and `imageReference` from its existing lease pin.

### Ordinary-image workspace boundary

`ISessionWorkspace` no longer exposes capture/commit/transfer APIs. Infrastructure resolves exact owner mounts and persists `SandboxMountAuthority` with working/home root identities, effective HomeAccess and UID/GID. The public workspace contract remains version 3; profile verification is version 4. `SandboxMountAuthority.TranslatedOwnership` records demonstrated Docker Desktop translation, independently of the canonical root identities. The private authenticated trusted-server mount attestation remains an Infrastructure/deployment detail; no Docker DTO or physical path enters Application/Contracts. Fresh data and reverification are required; no legacy mount fallback exists.

### Sandbox discovery, selection and safe failure evidence

`AgentDefinition.ExecutionSandboxProfiles` is an immutable Run snapshot of every effective authorized profile's exact revision, resolved Instance script/ceilings and credential epochs, including an `AdmittedForExecution` eligibility observation. Ineligible observations are discovery evidence only; later catalog/verification changes cannot expand that Run. `ExecutionSandboxProfile` retains the compatible effective-default projection. Current revocations and host/source eligibility are rechecked before execution/import.

`sandbox.profiles` is read-only and uses ordinary exact capability authorization. Its bounded list exposes IDs, names, clipped informational descriptions, admitted revisions, current availability, default and Session selection. It excludes credentials/authentication, protected values, host paths and initialization scripts. `SandboxCommandRequest.ProfileId` is optional: explicit selection must match an admitted authorized eligible profile; omission uses retained Session selection, otherwise the effective default. No fallback occurs.

Command results additionally project profile ID/revision, generation, phase, failure category, cancellation reason and `TerminationConfirmed`. A blocked generation returns `error: recovery_required`, safe environment state/generation and next action without dispatching. Receipt identity remains separate from environment identity. Inspection reads persisted evidence without server readiness or sandbox mutation. Owner reset checks both generation and latest/active Core execution identity under the same gate. Instance setup observations retain up to 16 exact `(Instance revision, Profile ID/revision, credential identity/epochs)` keys in additive JSON, with in-memory/SQLite parity.

`SandboxCommandRequest.TimeoutSeconds` is nullable: null means resolve the command allowance only after the Session selects its actual admitted profile. An explicit allowance remains validated and profile-clamped; existing Run deadlines are independent.

## Generic finalization evidence

`SandboxCommandResult` and durable `SandboxExecution` expose process status, real exit, bounded output, filesystemEffectsMayHaveOccurred, generationRecycled, remoteTerminationConfirmed and terminationConfirmed. Termination concerns processes/container ownership only. Files are already canonical and can remain partial. Read-only status never dispatches, imports or replays work. Exact execution identity and independent Run/configuration/lease revisions remain authoritative.

`SandboxEnvironmentInfo` exposes effective homeAccess and sampled storageEnforcement. Derived EnforcementPending means saved restrictions have not yet been confirmed by retirement of the old mount. CleanupPending describes uncertain process/container retirement. No file synchronization state is exposed.

## State observation and coordinated isolation configuration

Application `Ports/RuntimeState.cs` defines `DurableSessionState`, `DurableRunState`, `HostedSessionState` and `ResourceStateObservation`. Store and live-reader methods accept trusted owner scope; single-Session variants exclude sibling Session counts. Browser observations read existing handles only. Sandbox observations read existing leases and readiness only. No contributor launches a browser, provisions a generation, executes a command or clears a receipt. The fixed Application fan-out has a two-second TimeProvider budget, cancellation and partial failure semantics; null is unknown, not zero.

`IBrowserIsolationBinding.BindIsolationAsync` binds a trusted Session and Instance to a host-permitted resolved mode. A transition during an active or uncertain native operation reports `policy_transition_pending`; it cannot borrow another Session's pages. Persistent profile contexts coordinate native access while each Session owns its page handles, snapshots and interaction state. The resolved browser mode is pinned in `AgentRunConfiguration`.

Pending shared-context mutations retain their originating Session identity, including a cancelled page creation whose view was never published. They fence the owning Session and other users of that shared context. An unrelated ephemeral Session keeps independent observation, native access and fresh-view binding; owner-wide State can still report the shared context as partial.

Canonical `TenantAgentConfigurationService` uses `IsolationConfigurationPlanner` for the shared pure recipes, finite patch validation and exact fingerprint. Original-human `TenantAgentReadService` fences owned metadata reads and previews before and after I/O. `TenantAgentConfigurationSnapshot.Isolation` carries the actual Experience/maintenance values and revisions from the same owned read snapshot. `TenantAgentConfigurationChange.Isolation` extends the existing tenant configuration transaction; it is valid only alongside the one settings operation. Both stores compare all three owner revisions before mutation and commit metadata, IAM revision and one body-free `agents.state-isolation.update` audit together. No preset store, second configuration authority or unowned service is introduced. The global `StateIsolationConfigurationService` actor and unused `IStateIsolationConfigurationStore` writer/adapters are retired. `IsolationOwnerRevisions` remains a value on the canonical tenant configuration port; recipes and the safe audit vocabulary live in `IsolationConfigurationPlanner`. Metadata previews do not fan out to Runtime, Browser or Sandbox observers.

Canonical `TenantRuntimeStateService` uses `ITenantRuntimeStateStore` and `ITenantHostedRuntimeStateReader` with a complete tenant/Instance/executor owner and independently readable Session, Run and Sandbox-profile sets. Null sets mean the whole authorized family; empty sets authorize no source calls. Durable aggregates read metadata only; Runs additionally intersect readable Sessions. Existing native Browser handles are observed without binding or allocating a context; physical contexts are deduplicated and pages remain scoped to readable Session views. Sandbox observations validate actual profile ownership/revision and retain durable cleanup uncertainty when the provider is unavailable. The existing tenant host and delivery registry contribute distinct hosted/attached Session counts, without a second Runtime owner.

Human reads fence the original Account login, exact Instance and current security revision before and after collection. `ObserveForRunAsync` derives the owner from the trusted Session/Run, validates the active claim generation and both admitted/current grants, then resolves CurrentSession or explicitly permitted InstanceSummary. It rechecks claim, lifecycle, security and changed visibility after collection. Model arguments accept only optional `scope`; owner, Session, Run, tab and provider IDs are rejected. Every source uses its own current permission and operation policy: review-required sources remain unknown without creating or consuming approval. `SessionToolExecutor` resolves the canonical observer lazily through `TenantToolAuthorization` to avoid a host/factory dependency cycle. Runtime facts bypass the tool refresh gate only inside the trusted model-call pipeline; ordinary tool calls are limited to one per second per Run with bounded temporary gate metadata. The obsolete unowned `RuntimeStateService` and hosted-reader registration are removed. See [focused qualification](reports/tenant-runtime-state-verification-2026-10-21.md); broader context/isolation parity remains open.

### Canonical request-isolation settlement
